### Stage 5.4 E2E suite and phone-class timing (2026-10-24)
- Dependencies (owner approved the downloads): `@playwright/test` 1.64.1 and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 as development dependencies; latest releases, rechecked on 2026-10-24 04:03 UTC. `npm install` under `strict-allow-scripts` reported no install script; `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities. Browsers with `npx playwright install --only-shell chromium webkit` into `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright`: Chrome Headless Shell 156.0.8078.4 (203 MB unpacked), WebKit 27.2 (299 MB), and FFmpeg (3 MB; installed by default, which answers the 要確認 of Stage 5.1 P4).
- Suite (`playwright.config.ts`, `e2e/`, `tsconfig.e2e.json`): projects `desktop-chromium` (1280×800), `mobile-chromium` (Pixel 7 at 375×812), `mobile-webkit` (iPhone 13 at 375×812), and `timing` (on demand). The web server is `npm run build && npm run preview` on port 4174. Strings come from the typed catalogs. Every test fails on a request to another origin or a CSP violation. Specs: the full flow (search, "show a different type", exclusion, comparison, detail, servings change, back, reload notice), deep links, not found, the about page, locale switching with unchanged digits, input errors, a blocking contradiction, "all shown" with close alternatives, close alternatives when nothing meets the targets (microwave only, egg and rice left out), no recipe passing the filters (≤ 5 minutes), the content error with retry, axe WCAG 2.2 AA with color contrast on five views in light and dark, keyboard operation with the skip link and a visible focus ring (Chromium; WebKit does not reach links with Tab by default), and the security headers on documents, scripts, styles, content bundles, and the license file. Result: 33 tests per project; `desktop-chromium` 33 passed, `mobile-chromium` 33 passed, `mobile-webkit` 32 passed and 1 skipped.
- Findings: (1) Safari did not move focus to a field when an error summary link was followed; the link now focuses its field on click (`src/views/TargetsView.tsx`, asserted in `src/views/TargetsView.test.tsx`). (2) WebKit reports a `style-src-attr` violation for every `<select>` under a strict CSP, also on a two-line page without inline styles (Chromium reports nothing); the suite tolerates only that report in WebKit (`e2e/support.ts`). The CSP is unchanged. (3) The "all shown" notice also appears in the visually hidden live region; tests check the status box.
- Phone-class timing (open question 17; `npm run test:e2e:timing`, Chrome Headless Shell 156 on an Apple M1, CDP CPU throttling, 375×812, `ja`; click to the first DOM commit under `main` plus layout; median / p90 of 15 runs after 2 warm-up runs, ms):

| Scenario | 1× | 4× | 6× |
|---|---|---|---|
| Search, cut | 20.1 / 23.5 | 80.8 / 93.6 | 121.1 / 140.0 |
| Search, bulk | 19.3 / 23.1 | 78.1 / 92.9 | 117.8 / 139.7 |
| Search, maintain with tofu required | 9.1 / 9.5 | 36.4 / 41.8 | 58.0 / 65.9 |
| Search, bulk at ≤ 15 min | 11.8 / 13.2 | 48.3 / 52.9 | 78.7 / 90.6 |
| Re-suggestion | 4.5 / 5.4 | 19.1 / 24.2 | 28.6 / 31.5 |
| Recalculation (servings step) | 2.7 / 3.0 | 11.3 / 12.1 | 17.0 / 18.4 |

- Investigation required by Stage 5.1 P5 (4× median above 50 ms): the engine alone in Node (`evaluatePool` and `selectCandidates`, warm, median of 15) takes 14.9 ms (cut) and 14.4 ms (bulk) on the 60 recipes against 7.7 and 6.7 ms on the 20 validation recipes; the warm UI search at 1× is about 20 ms, so the UI overhead is now about 5 ms. The Stage 4.7 gap (25 against 7 ms) came mostly from the unwarmed first call. Time grows with the library through the engine. The owner chose option C on 2026-10-24: the recommendation stays on the main thread and the ADR-030 D10 threshold becomes about 100 ms at 4× (options considered: A, a worker, which keeps the page responsive but adds a second runtime path and test setup without shortening the computation; B, optimizing the engine, which needs profiling with snapshot checks; C, the main thread with the revised threshold). Known limitation: about 120 ms at 6× and no real-device measurement.

### Stage 5.5 security, dependency, and secret checks (2026-10-24)
- The owner approved the plan with L1, L2, G1, and C1 as recommended and the gitleaks download.
- `npm audit` (all dependencies and `--omit=dev`): 0 vulnerabilities.
- Licenses (L1, L2): `tools/licenses.ts` with `tools/check-licenses.ts` (`npm run check:licenses`, part of `npm run check`) checks the 291 packages of `package-lock.json`. Production allowlist: MIT, ISC, Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, 0BSD. Development adds MPL-2.0 (axe-core, @axe-core/playwright, lightningcss), BlueOak-1.0.0 (lru-cache, minimatch), MIT-0 (two @csstools packages), CC0-1.0 (mdn-data), and CC-BY-4.0 (caniuse-lite browser data, used at build time only). Result: production MIT 9 and Apache-2.0 1 (`typescript`, a peer of `i18next` that is not in the bundle); no problem. The bundled licenses are in `dist/third-party-licenses.txt` (Stage 5.3).
- Secret scan (G1): gitleaks 8.30.1 (released 2026-03-21, MIT) from the GitHub release `gitleaks_8.30.1_darwin_arm64.tar.gz`, SHA-256 `b40ab0ae55c505963e365f271a8d3846efbc170aa17f2607f13df610a9aeb6a5`, matching the release's checksum file (both files come from the same release, so this checks integrity, not authorship); kept in the session scratch directory, nothing installed. `gitleaks git --log-opts=--all --redact`: 53 commits, 1.89 MB, no leaks. `gitleaks dir` on the tracked files of `HEAD` (`git archive`): 1.55 MB, no leaks; on `dist/`: no leaks. `dist/` contains no source maps; a search for api key, secret, token, password, and bearer in `dist/` finds only React DOM's list of input types (`password`). No commit touches `data/raw`, `*.xlsx`, `*.pdf`, or `.env*`. Browser storage is used only in `src/i18n/localePreference.ts`.
- Content policy re-check (C1, [CONTENT_POLICY.md](../en/CONTENT_POLICY.md)): forbidden expressions in content and UI catalogs pass content validation and `src/i18n/policy.test.ts`; a search for further health or allergen-safety wording (healthy, diet, muscle, "contains no", 安心, gluten-free in three languages) finds only the forbidden list itself. Presets are labelled as examples (`preset.*`, `disclaimer.presetsAreExamples`). Per recipe (60): every meat and poultry recipe states a visible doneness check, either a `*_doneness` note or `even_heating` or `residual_heat` text that names red or pink; fried recipes have `hot_oil` notes on fire risk and not leaving the oil; eggs with soft yolks carry `soft_yolk` or `soft_egg`; no raw-egg dish; batch recipes carry `storage` or `cooling`; microwave steps carry `steam` or `even_heating`. Two gaps were fixed with existing reviewed texts (content version `2026-10-24.3`): `beef_potato_tomato_stew` gains `beef_doneness`, and `cod_herb_crust` (microwaved potatoes) gains `steam`. `salmon_tempura` has no fish doneness note; the policy requires one for meat and poultry only.
- Checks: `npm run check` (46 test files, 624 tests) and `npm run test:e2e` (98 passed, 1 skipped) pass; nutrition snapshots are unchanged.

### Stage 5.6 CI, README, deployment runbook (2026-10-24)
- The owner approved the plan with W1–W3, D1, and R1 as recommended.
- CI (W1–W3): `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pushes to `dev` and `main` and on pull requests, on `ubuntu-24.04`, with `permissions: contents: read`, no secrets, no deployment, and cancellation of superseded runs. Job `check`: `npm ci` (strict-allow-scripts, ADR-018) and `npm run check`; job `e2e`: `npx playwright install --with-deps --only-shell chromium webkit` and `npm run test:e2e`, uploading `playwright-report/` and `test-results/` only on failure (7 days). Actions pinned to commit SHAs, *Verified* with the GitHub API on 2026-10-24: `actions/checkout` v7.0.1 (`3d3c42e5…`), `actions/setup-node` v7.1.0 (`949feb24…`, Node 24 runtime, `node-version-file` and `cache: npm`), `actions/upload-artifact` v7.0.2 (`cf430e03…`). The workflow has not run yet: it starts when the owner pushes. Run time per workflow (estimated 8–10 minutes) and the owner's GitHub plan remain 要確認.
- Cloudflare (D1): `platform/cloudflare/wrangler.jsonc` (assets only: `name`, `compatibility_date`, `assets.directory`, `not_found_handling: "single-page-application"`). *Verified* (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/, updated 2026-10-20): `name` and `compatibility_date` are required, `main` is not needed for assets only, and `wrangler.jsonc` is recommended. Not stated: how `assets.directory` resolves when the file is passed with `--config` (要確認 with `wrangler deploy --dry-run` at deployment time; the file uses `../../dist`, relative to itself). Wrangler stays out of the repository.
- Caching: `platform/headers.ts` adds a `/assets/*` rule with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` to the generated `_headers` (every file under `dist/assets/` has a content hash); HTML, the about route, and the license file keep the host default. `test/node/headers.test.ts` checks both rules.
- Runbook: [DEPLOYMENT.md](../en/DEPLOYMENT.md) (contents of `dist/`, pre-release checks including the repeated secret scan, Cloudflare steps with dry run and post-deployment checks, rollback, Netlify, nginx, and GitHub Pages notes, CI). Open items in it: the `assets.directory` resolution, whether `_headers` applies to SPA fallback responses, and the rollback options for an assets-only Worker.
- README (R1): rewritten for the current state (features, status, setup, commands, data handling, attribution, known limitations, documents); the license stays "not chosen" (要確認, owner decision).
- Checks: `npm run check` (624 tests) and `npm run test:e2e` (98 passed, 1 skipped) pass; `dist/_headers` contains the `/*` and `/assets/*` rules.

- The owner approved the plan with R1–R3 as recommended: the report lives in [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md); open questions 11, 13, and 16, the first CI run, and a secret scan of the release history block the release, while open questions 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9, and 12 and the real-device timing are released as documented limitations; the first deployment uses the free `*.workers.dev` subdomain.
- [SPEC.md](../en/SPEC.md) §15: all ten criteria are met on commit `9bf2356` (`npm run check` with 624 tests, `npm run test:e2e` with 98 passed and 1 skipped, `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities), with the evidence per criterion in [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §1; the CI part waits for the first run.
- Open question 11 closed: *Verified* on 2026-10-24 (Consumer Affairs Agency, https://www.caa.go.jp/policies/policy/food_labeling/food_sanitation/allergy/, PDF "アレルギー表示とは" 令和8年4月時点 and the notice of 2026-04-01): 9 mandatory and 20 recommended items, including cashew (mandatory since 2026-04-01 with a two-year transition) and pistachio (recommended), match `shared/content.ts`. The same PDF notes that a mere "may contain" statement is not allowed on packaged-food labels; the app's "may contain" is its own best-effort classification shown with the disclaimer, not a food label.
- Open question 13: the texts still to be reviewed by the owner are listed in [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §2 (20 validation recipes with 194 localized texts, 50 Phase 0 foods, 406 UI catalog keys outside `about.*`).
- Known limitations, manual checks, and the deployment proposal: [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §3–§5. Nothing was deployed.

### CI first run (2026-10-24)
- The owner pushed `dev`; in GitHub Actions, the `check` job failed in `src/App.test.tsx` ("renders Japanese by default and sets document metadata"): the document title was still the loading title ("Recipe Discovery — 栄養目標に合うレシピを見つける") when the view heading had appeared. All 624 tests had passed locally.
- Cause: `ViewHeading` set `document.title` in a passive `useEffect`, which React may run after the heading is in the DOM; on the slower runner the test read the title in that gap. A new test reads the title from a `MutationObserver` at the moment the `h1` is inserted and failed the same way before the fix.
- Fix: `src/components/ViewHeading.tsx` sets the title in `useLayoutEffect`, so the title changes in the same commit as the heading (also for users). `npm run check` (625 tests), 8 runs of the title tests under CPU load, and `npm run test:e2e` (98 passed, 1 skipped) pass locally. The CI run on the fix commit is still to be confirmed.
- Second run (fix commit `911c091`): the title test passed; `src/App.test.tsx` "returns to the targets view with a notice when the search is gone after a reload" failed because `history.state` was still `{ view: "candidates" }`. Same cause: the redirect ran in a passive `useEffect` in `CurrentView`. Effects that run after a user event are flushed with the commit, but those of an initial load or a `popstate` are not, so only non-event commits race. Audit of the passive effects with visible results: the redirect (reproduced), the heading focus, `<html lang>`, and the focus after a re-suggestion or exclusion (not reproduced). All four now use `useLayoutEffect` (`src/App.tsx`, `src/components/ViewHeading.tsx`, `src/components/DocumentMetadata.tsx`, `src/views/CandidatesView.tsx`); the loading title effect in `Shell` stays passive because it only acts while no view is shown. `src/App.test.tsx` "effects visible in the same commit as the view" reads the title, the history entry, `lang`, and the focus (after a link and after the back button) at the DOM mutation that shows the change. Locally: `npm run check` (627 tests), 8 runs of the App and UI tests under CPU load, and `npm run test:e2e` (98 passed, 1 skipped) pass.

### Deployment preparation and dry run (2026-10-24)
- The owner approved the deployment proposal of [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §5 up to the dry run.
- Wrangler 4.152.0 (still the latest on 2026-10-24 08:25 UTC) installed with `--ignore-scripts` in the session scratch directory (36 packages, 213 MB); nothing in the repository changed. `npm audit` there reported `undici` 7.29.1 (pinned by `miniflare` 5.20261019.2-beta) with three advisories published on 2026-10-23 (GHSA-xjj6-f33q-929c high, GHSA-4qv8-2p9j-2685 and GHSA-f72v-6w45-q472 medium), patched in 7.30.1 (released 2026-10-15); an `overrides` entry in the scratch `package.json` sets `undici` 7.30.1, after which `npm audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities. The tool is not part of the app or its bundle.
- Install scripts (ADR-018): `esbuild` 0.28.2 and `workerd` 1.20261019.1 run `node install.js`, which checks that the platform binary from the optional package (`@esbuild/darwin-arm64`, `@cloudflare/workerd-darwin-arm64`) runs and otherwise fetches that package from `registry.npmjs.org` and rewrites the launcher. The binaries were already installed, so the scripts were not run. `fsevents` was not run either.
- Dry run on `e07fc48` (`npm ci && npm run build`, then `wrangler deploy --config platform/cloudflare/wrangler.jsonc --dry-run` with `WRANGLER_SEND_METRICS=false`): Wrangler read `/Users/…/recipe-discovery-app/dist`, so `assets.directory` (`../../dist`) resolves relative to the config file (answers the 要確認 of Stage 5.6); it listed the 9 files plus the `/assets` directory, read `_headers` as configuration ("Ignoring asset: _headers"), found no bindings, and exited without uploading. `wrangler telemetry status` reports telemetry disabled by `WRANGLER_SEND_METRICS`; the Wrangler log confirms that nothing was dispatched.
- This Mac already has a global Wrangler configuration (`~/Library/Preferences/.wrangler/`, from before this project) that may hold a login; it was not opened. The account is confirmed with `wrangler whoami` before deploying.

### Stage 6.2 recommendation speed: plan and baseline (2026-10-24)
- The owner approved the plan as recommended: optimize the solver's grid loop (O1) and the nutrition engine's per-call allocations (O2) without changing any result; estimate the 100-recipe browser timing from the Node ratio; commit a solver results snapshot before optimizing. Not pursued: reusing engine probes across searches (O3; only later searches gain, and the engine would keep state between searches), pruning the grid (O4; equivalence is hard to prove), and a worker (decided in "Stage 5.4").
- Profile (Node 24.19.0, Apple M1, `--cpu-prof` over 300 searches): about 40 % of the time is the solver's grid loop (`visit`, `fromVector`, `evaluateFit`, with garbage collection of the objects created per grid state) and about 30 % is the nutrition engine (`record` with `Object.fromEntries` alone 11 %). The 60 recipes have 19,338 grid states and need about 450 engine calls per search (about 14 µs each); `beef_steak_potatoes` alone has 5,376 states. Every solution uses the linear method; none falls back to the exhaustive search.
- Tools: `tools/benchmark-recommendation.ts` (`npm run bench:recommendation`) times a full search (`evaluatePool` and `selectCandidates`) in the four scenarios of `e2e/timing.spec.ts` on the repository recipes and on a synthetic library of 100 recipes (the first 40 recipes again under new IDs, in memory only). `test/node/solver-results.test.ts` keeps every pool entry's solver result (method, tier, forced and solver sides, amounts, relative change, per-serving values at full precision) for eight requests, including forced sides, forced substitutions, and required foods. A scratch comparison outside the repository hashes complete pool and selection results for 1,500 requests (five condition sets, 250 target combinations, servings 1 and 2); it is repeatable and is compared before and after each optimization.
- Baseline, Node (median / p90 of 15 runs, ms): repository (60 recipes, 19,338 grid states) cut 16.0 / 18.2, bulk 15.2 / 15.6, maintain with tofu required 2.8 / 3.5, bulk at ≤ 15 min 5.8 / 7.2; synthetic (100 recipes, 35,322 grid states) 24.9 / 33.7, 26.0 / 30.1, 5.2 / 5.7, 9.9 / 10.2.
- Baseline, browser (`npm run test:e2e:timing`, same profile as "Stage 5.4"; median / p90, ms):

| Scenario | 1× | 4× | 6× |
|---|---|---|---|
| Search, cut | 20.1 / 23.8 | 85.8 / 96.8 | 130.5 / 144.8 |
| Search, bulk | 19.8 / 22.7 | 81.2 / 97.5 | 119.3 / 148.5 |
| Search, maintain with tofu required | 9.3 / 11.7 | 38.3 / 42.8 | 58.2 / 66.9 |
| Search, bulk at ≤ 15 min | 11.9 / 13.6 | 48.6 / 51.4 | 73.6 / 82.8 |
| Re-suggestion | 4.7 / 5.4 | 19.4 / 21.9 | 31.2 / 41.1 |
| Recalculation (servings step) | 2.5 / 2.9 | 11.7 / 14.4 | 18.3 / 19.8 |

- Estimate for 100 recipes at 4× (browser median × Node ratio of 100 to 60 recipes): cut 85.8 × 1.56 ≈ 134 ms, bulk 81.2 × 1.71 ≈ 139 ms, above the 100 ms threshold of ADR-030 D10. The optimization needs to save about 30 %.
- A first browser run overlapped with the scratch comparison and read about 10 % slower at 6×; it was discarded and the timing repeated alone.

### Stage 6.2 recommendation speed: optimization (2026-10-24)
- O2, nutrition engine (`domain/nutrition/engine.ts`): `record` builds its object in a loop instead of `Object.fromEntries` over an array of pairs; key order is unchanged. About 15 % alone in Node.
- O1, solver (`domain/recommendation/solver.ts`, `domain/recommendation/fit.ts`): `fitEvaluator(targets)` computes the target windows once per solve (`evaluateFit` calls it, so its behavior is unchanged); the linear model writes into one reused vector and one reused per-serving and range object per side mask, read at once by the fit evaluation, which keeps numbers only; the relative change adds precomputed terms in the same order as before; the value vector is copied only for a new best state. A loop version of the fit counts was tried and dropped (no measurable gain).
- Equality: `test/node/solver-results.test.ts`, the engine snapshot, the scenario snapshot, and every other test pass without updates; the scratch comparison of 1,500 requests is identical before and after. The comparison was checked by adding 1e-7 to the cost of a switched-off side, which changed the result of 654 of the 1,500 requests; the change was reverted.
- Node (`npm run bench:recommendation`, median / p90, ms): repository cut 11.3 / 13.5 (−29 %), bulk 10.7 / 11.1 (−30 %), maintain with tofu required 2.0 / 2.5, bulk at ≤ 15 min 4.3 / 4.8; synthetic 100 recipes cut 17.8 / 20.0 (−29 %), bulk 17.9 / 18.3 (−31 %), 3.6 / 4.3, 6.9 / 7.6.
- Browser (`npm run test:e2e:timing`, run alone; median / p90, ms):

| Scenario | 1× | 4× | 6× |
|---|---|---|---|
| Search, cut | 16.3 / 18.3 | 66.0 / 73.7 | 99.7 / 106.5 |
| Search, bulk | 15.9 / 19.7 | 66.0 / 77.0 | 96.6 / 108.1 |
| Search, maintain with tofu required | 7.9 / 8.5 | 33.2 / 34.4 | 48.8 / 57.0 |
| Search, bulk at ≤ 15 min | 10.0 / 11.2 | 40.9 / 47.7 | 61.5 / 69.6 |
| Re-suggestion | 4.6 / 5.2 | 19.2 / 20.8 | 28.9 / 31.9 |
| Recalculation (servings step) | 2.6 / 2.8 | 11.0 / 12.5 | 16.4 / 18.1 |

- A full search at 4× fell from 81–86 ms to 66 ms (about −20 %). Estimate for 100 recipes at 4×: 104 ms (cut) and 110 ms (bulk) when the whole browser time is scaled by the Node ratio of 100 to 60 recipes, or 92 ms and 95 ms when only the engine part grows (60-recipe browser time plus 4 × the Node difference). The synthetic library repeats heavier recipes than average (15,984 grid states for 40 copies against 12,892 for an average 40), so both estimates lean high. The real timing is measured when the library grows.
- Not changed: reusing engine probes across searches (O3) and pruning (O4), as approved in the plan.

### Stage 6.4 egg- and soy-based recipes (2026-10-24)
- Report: of the 19 recipes with egg or soy as the main protein, six met no preset after adjustment (`egg_tomato_stir_fry`, `eggs_tomato_pepper_bake`, `poached_egg_spinach_toast`, `tortilla_espanola`, `vegetable_omelette_bread`, `tofu_egg_champuru`). Whole egg has about as much fat as protein (12004: protein 12.2 g, fat 10.2 g per 100 g; scrambled 12022, which includes oil: 13.3 g and 16.7 g), so adjusting the egg amount cannot meet a protein minimum and a fat maximum together. Second sources: cheese, fried potato, atsuage, stir-fried vegetables with their oil, and cooking oil (2–8 g each). Egg white has almost no fat (12014 raw: 44 kcal, protein 10.1 g, fat 0 g; 12015 boiled: 0.1 g).
- The owner chose part of the whole eggs replaced by egg whites (E1) with less oil and leaner preparation (E2), the two validation recipes included, and `tortilla_espanola` unchanged (a classic slow-fried dish; known limitation). Not chosen: relabelling only (E3) and adding another main protein (E4).
- New food `egg_white` (卵白 / Egg white / Clara de huevo; aliases; raw 12014; egg allergen; protein group egg; 78 foods, 140 extracted codes). Cooked egg white has no table value, so recipes use the raw value with the cooking method (`raw_value_for_cooking`, band −15 to +5 %, ADR-028) and say so in a note, which also mentions the leftover yolks and carton egg white. One egg white is authored as 33 g.
- Recipes (content version `2026-10-24.8`; goal labels unchanged, so ADR-023 counts are unchanged: cut 20, maintain 27, bulk 17; egg 9, soy 10; 46 quick):
  - C1 `egg_tomato_stir_fry`: 3 whole eggs → 2 whole eggs (1–3) and 3 egg whites (2–6) scrambled together; egg oil 5 → 3 g; 2 g of oil for the whites counted in full; tomato oil 3 → 2 g.
  - C2 `vegetable_omelette_bread`: 3 whole eggs → 2 (1–3) and 3 egg whites (2–6); egg oil 7.5 → 5 g; 2 g for the whites counted in full.
  - C3 `eggs_tomato_pepper_bake`: 3 eggs → 2 (1–3) and 3 egg whites (2–6) stirred into the sauce before the eggs are cracked in; vegetable oil 6 → 4 g.
  - C4 `poached_egg_spinach_toast` (validation recipe): mozzarella 30 → 15 g; spinach boiled (06268) instead of sautéed; 3 egg whites (2–6) scrambled in 2 g of olive oil counted in full; bread 1–3 slices instead of 1–2; 15 minutes instead of 10.
  - C5 `tofu_egg_champuru` (validation recipe): 2 eggs → 1 (1–2) and 3 egg whites (1–5); egg oil 5 → 3 g; tofu oil 5 → 4 g. Change from the approved plan: the plan cooked the cabbage in the tofu oil with its raw value, but content validation (`cooking-state`) requires the tabulated stir-fried value (06333), which includes its own oil. The cabbage is blanched instead (06062, exact) and tossed in the remaining tofu oil; 20 minutes instead of 15, and a pot is needed.
- Fit after adjustment (cut / maintain / bulk; before → after): C1 not met in all → near / met / near; C2 → near / met / met (the 2 g of oil for the whites, added for an honest count, turned cut from met in the simulation to near); C3 → near / met / met; C4 → near / met / met; C5 → not met / met / near. Egg recipes meeting maintain: 2 → 6 of 9; soy recipes: 5 → 6 of 10.
- Tests: the expectations of the tests of reviewed counts on the validation recipes follow the new content (`test/node/recommendation.test.ts`: met and near counts per scenario, tiers, and three examples whose recipes changed — close alternatives now use salmon required in maintain, the gap reasons use tofu required in cut, and the time relaxation uses bulk at ≤ 10 minutes; `test/node/engine.test.ts`: raw-value reasons 9 → 11, oil counted in full 17 → 18, included in cooked values 17 → 15; `test/node/composition-report.test.ts`: exact 142, approximation 18, not counted 15). The solver still chooses the same states as the exhaustive search for every validation recipe.
- Snapshot diffs: the engine snapshot and the solver results change only for the five recipes. Scenario snapshot: `eggs_tomato_pepper_bake` enters the first sets of P2, P3, and S3, `tofu_egg_champuru` the first set of S7, and `vegetable_omelette_bread` the first set of S8, replacing `beef_broccoli_microwave`, `chicken_breast_tomato_simmer`, `tuna_tofu_bowl`, and `beef_shimeji_bowl` there through diversification; P4, S5, and S6 are unchanged; met and near counts grow. No change is caused by an engine change.
- `npm run eval:coverage` (60 recipes, met after adjustment / near; before → after): S1 cut 26/11 → 26/15, S2 cut ≤ 20 min 22/8 → 22/12, S3 maintain 39/11 → 44/11, S4 bulk 29/17 → 32/19, S5 cut without egg 22/7 (unchanged), S6 chicken required 8/1 (unchanged), S7 tofu required 5/3 → 6/3, S8 bulk ≤ 25 min 25/15 → 28/17. `npm run report:composition`: exact 410, approximation 74, hard to estimate 2, not counted 26, fried 5.

Browser check (`npm run preview`): the five detail pages at 375 px in `ja`, `en`, and `es` with the egg white row, note, and steps and no element wider than the viewport; `poached_egg_spinach_toast` at desktop width in `ja` (adjustment rows and the ingredient table with 卵白 99 g, 12014, raw, approximation). Not checked: dark mode, and desktop width for the other four pages.

### Stage 6.5 evidence for the error bands (2026-10-24)
- The owner approved the plan as recommended: research the evidence for the bands of [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §7 in the MEXT explanation PDF and workbook, with published studies for `slow_frying` only; transcriptions and analysis scripts stay in the session scratch directory; nothing in the engine changes without an ADR.
- Sources: chapter 3 "調理による成分変化率区分別一覧" (PDF pp. 433–437; medians and food counts per food group and cooking method; change rate = cooked value × weight change rate ÷ raw value), Table 13 (pp. 64–65), and the 61 transcribed raw–cooked pairs of `data/composition/cooking.json` with the extracted values.
- `raw_value_for_cooking` (−15 / +5 % for every nutrient): *Verified* for protein — chapter 3 medians 87–100 % for meat, fish, and eggs; transcribed pairs 0.87–1.03 for meat and fish. Fat and energy fall further for fatty meat and fish — medians down to 74 % (fat, boiled chicken) and 83 % (energy, boiled chicken and steamed fish); pairs down to 0.66 fat and 0.73 energy (grilled ground beef) and 0.69 and 0.77 (grilled farmed salmon). Vegetables lose protein and carbohydrate in boiling (pairs 0.67–1.01 and 0.70–1.10). The band understates the lower side for fat, energy, and carbohydrate; the direction of the error (raw values read high) is the cautious one for fat and energy maximums.
- `related_cooked_value` (±5 %): between non-oil methods of the same food, protein differs by up to 8 %, fat by up to 34 % (sasami boiled and grilled; beef round 32 %), and energy by up to 14 %.
- `post_cooking_refuse` (±5 %): no spread is published; the tables give single analyses. Remains an assumption.
- `fried` (±30 % of the ingredient's fat): Table 13 gives fat changes of −5.2 to +32.3 g per 100 g of raw food by food and coating (for example salmon tempura without skin +0.7 g, pork fillet tonkatsu +20.9 g), each from a single analysis; no repeat spread is published. Because the band applies to the ingredient's total fat, it overstates the uncertainty of foods whose fat is mostly their own. Remains an assumption; a band on the absorbed fat only (from Table 13) is a possible refinement that needs an ADR.
- `slow_frying` (0 to +50 %): studies disagree on the direction. Moyano and Pedreschi (LWT 39(3), 285–291, 2006) report about 32 % more oil uptake at 120 °C than at 180 °C for unblanched potato slices and no temperature effect after blanching; other studies found more oil at higher temperatures. The upper bound covers the reported increase; the lower bound of 0 is not supported. Remains an assumption.
- Impact estimate (scratch copy of the engine, 60 recipes): nutrient-specific bands at the observed extremes (option E: `raw_value_for_cooking` fat −35 / +5 %, energy −25 / +5 %, carbohydrate −30 / +10 %; `related_cooked_value` protein ±10 %, fat ±35 %, energy ±15 %, carbohydrate ±10 %) show a fat range for 35 instead of 17 recipes; after adjustment, met recipes per preset fall from 26 / 44 / 32 to 23 / 41 / 30 (8 met → near, 9 not met → near), and every coverage scenario keeps at least 6 met recipes. A milder option M (fat −25 %, energy −20 %; related fat ±20 %, energy ±10 %) gives 24 / 42 / 31.
- Decision (owner, 2026-10-24): option E; ADR-028 revised. Implementation: `CONTRIBUTION_BANDS` in `domain/nutrition/engine.ts` replaces the fixed factors, with a test that pins the table (`domain/nutrition/engine.test.ts`). The engine changes no point value and no class; it changes ranges and therefore statuses and the chosen adjustments.
- Results on the repository (matching the estimate): a fat range is shown for 35 recipes (was 17), energy 44 (41), protein 40 (39), carbohydrate 16 (6); the engine snapshot changes the ranges of 42 of 60 recipes and no point value. Met / near / not met after adjustment: cut 26/15/19 → 23/24/13, maintain 44/11/5 → 41/17/2, bulk 32/19/9 → 30/21/9. The solver still chooses the same states as the exhaustive search for every validation recipe.
- Snapshot diffs: scenario first sets change in P2 (`vegetable_omelette_bread` for `eggs_tomato_pepper_bake`), P3 and S3 (`chicken_lemon_herb_grill`, `pork_loin_ginger` for `pork_fillet_mustard_saute`, `eggs_tomato_pepper_bake`), P4 (`cod_vegetable_hot_pot` for `pork_fillet_paprika_grill`), S4 (`beef_potato_tomato_stew`, `mackerel_can_tomato_salad`, `chicken_thigh_karaage` for `beef_steak_potatoes`, `chicken_breast_tomato_simmer`, `squid_red_pepper_stir_fry`), and S7 (`tuna_tofu_bowl` for `soy_milk_udon_tofu`), as recipes whose ranges now cross a target limit become near; P1, S1, S2, S5, S6, and S8 keep their first sets with more near recipes. Validation tests: met / near counts (cut 9/4, cut ≤ 20 min 8/2, maintain 12/6, cut without egg 9/2), tiers (9/4/7 and 8/4/7), and the gap-reason example, now salmon required in maintain (`salmon_tempura` with a fat range across the limit, `salmon_tomato_pasta` more than 2 g of fat over).

### Stage 7.1 expansion plan (2026-10-24)
- Owner decisions (2026-10-24): add the cuisine values `latin_american`, `mediterranean`, and `southeast_asian` (ADR-026 revised); Spanish dish names follow the region the dish comes from, while food names keep the V1 rule ([CONTENT_POLICY.md](../en/CONTENT_POLICY.md) §6); the recipe list below and the ADR-023 revision; new recipes keep their adjustment grid within about 400 states, and the speed is measured after batch 1 before batch 2 starts.
- Library of release 2 (60 recipes): Japanese 29, Western 12, Chinese 8, Spanish 7, Korean-style 3, Okinawan 1; ≤ 10 minutes 5; `batch` 4; `breakfast` 5; grilling 4; steaming 5; microwave only 7.
- Batch 1 (13): arroz con pollo with kidney beans (Latin American, bulk, chicken, batch); beef and bean chili (Latin American, maintain, beef, batch); grilled pork tenderloin with avocado salsa (Latin American, cut, grill); shrimp and corn lime rice (Latin American, cut, fish); huevos rancheros-style egg whites with beans (Latin American, maintain, egg, breakfast); taco rice with lean pork (Okinawan, maintain, pork); chicken and chickpea lemon stew (Mediterranean, maintain, chicken, batch); grilled salmon with yogurt-cucumber sauce (Mediterranean, maintain, grill); tuna and chickpea salad (Mediterranean, cut, fish, ≤ 10 minutes, no cooking, breakfast); chicken curry with a little coconut milk (Southeast Asian, bulk); pork larb (Southeast Asian, cut); pho-style beef and rice noodle soup (Southeast Asian, maintain, beef cooked through); tofu and garlic chive stir-fry with fish sauce (Southeast Asian, maintain, soy, ≤ 10 minutes).
- Batch 2 (13): salt-grilled mackerel with grated daikon (Japanese, maintain, grill); yellowtail teriyaki (Japanese, bulk); microwave-steamed chicken, Chinese style (Chinese, cut, ≤ 10 minutes, steam, microwave only); steamed tofu with minced chicken (Chinese, maintain, soy, steam); beef bulgogi (Korean-style, bulk, batch); sundubu-style tofu soup (Korean-style, maintain, soy); piperrada with egg whites (Spanish, maintain, egg, ≤ 10 minutes, breakfast); natto and egg white omelette (Japanese, maintain, egg, ≤ 10 minutes, breakfast); miso-marinated grilled salmon (Japanese, maintain, batch, grill); microwave "salad chicken" (Japanese, cut, batch, steam, microwave only); beef and kabocha simmer (Japanese, bulk, batch); grilled pork tenderloin with steamed sweet potato (Western, bulk, grill); shrimp and egg white stir-fry (Chinese, cut, egg, ≤ 10 minutes).
- Planned library (86): cut 27, maintain 40, bulk 23; chicken 15, pork 14, beef 14, fish 17, egg 13, soy 13; 64 quick (74 %); 4 fried (5 %); Japanese 35 (41 %); ≤ 10 minutes 11, `batch` 11, `breakfast` 9, grilling 9, steaming 8, microwave only 9. Goal labels may move once the adjustment of each recipe is checked.
- Foods: about 17 new foods, 12 in batch 1 (kidney beans and chickpeas boiled, avocado, lime juice, coriander leaves, frozen sweet corn, cucumber, chili powder, coconut milk, fish sauce, rice noodles, garlic chives) and the rest in batch 2 (mackerel, yellowtail, daikon, kabocha, sweet potato). Tortillas (no table value) and quinoa (no cooked value) are not used; rice noodles use the dry raw value with the raw-value band, like freeze-dried tofu.
- Food safety: no raw or undercooked meat, poultry, fish, or eggs (no ceviche, no rare beef in pho; larb and taco rice meat cooked through); herbs and vegetables eaten raw are washed; the existing doneness, egg, batch cooling, and microwave notes apply ([CONTENT_POLICY.md](../en/CONTENT_POLICY.md) §3).
- Speed: a full recommendation took about 73 ms at 4× on 60 recipes; scaling by the library size gives about 104 ms for 86, above the ADR-030 D10 threshold of about 100 ms. The grid is the main cost (the 60 recipes average about 350 states; `beef_steak_potatoes` has 5,376, about a quarter of the total), so new recipes stay within about 400 states (at most two adjustable ingredients, coarser steps). If batch 1 brings the estimate over the threshold, options (for example Stage 6.2 O3, or coarser steps for `beef_steak_potatoes`, which changes results) are proposed before batch 2.

### Stage 7.2 batch 1: recipes (2026-10-25)
- Owner decisions (2026-10-24): the ingredient amounts and adjustment bounds of the 13 recipes as evaluated with the engine; D1 `beef_pho_rice_noodles` labelled cut instead of maintain (at most about 560 kcal, near the lower maintain bound); D2 bread instead of tortillas in `egg_white_beans_ranchero` (no table value for tortillas); D3 three egg whites in `tofu_garlic_chive_stir_fry` (tofu alone stays below the maintain protein minimum; the main protein group stays soy); D4 `arroz_con_pollo_beans` as a quick version with cooked rice.
- Recipes (content version `2026-10-24.10`): `arroz_con_pollo_beans`, `beef_bean_chili`, `pork_fillet_avocado_salsa`, `shrimp_corn_lime_rice`, `egg_white_beans_ranchero`, `taco_rice_pork`, `chicken_chickpea_lemon_stew`, `salmon_yogurt_cucumber`, `tuna_chickpea_salad`, `chicken_coconut_curry`, `pork_larb`, `beef_pho_rice_noodles`, `tofu_garlic_chive_stir_fry`. Every recipe meets the preset of its goal label after adjustment; met counts per preset: cut 23 → 31, maintain 41 → 53, bulk 30 → 36. `pork_larb` defaults to 160 g of pork (150 g was off its 20 g grid).
- Adjustment grid: 970 states in total for the 13 recipes (at most 350, `tofu_garlic_chive_stir_fry`); 21,891 states in the library.
- Safety notes: the existing doneness, `egg_set`, `cooling`, `no_cook`, and `opened_can` notes; new `wash_raw` (raw vegetables and herbs washed, nine recipes); `beef_doneness` of the pho says not to put raw beef on the noodles. Fish sauce links shrimp as may contain, so excluding shrimp also excludes the four recipes that use it.
- Spanish: dish names follow the region (`Arroz con pollo y frijoles`, `Chili con carne y frijoles`); summaries, steps, and notes use V1 vocabulary (`alubias`, `gambas`); `Salmón asado` matches the food state label "Asado" of Stage 6.3 R4.
- Owner review of the English and Spanish texts (2026-10-24/25, review sheets in two parts, safety notes back-translated): G1 English `wash_raw` word order; G2 "añade el huevo" instead of "casca el huevo" ("cascar" means to hit in some regions); G3 the Spanish note of the boiled beans ("Si son de conserva…"); G4 `Claras y frijoles al estilo huevos rancheros` (bread replaces tortillas); H1 "pártelo con las manos" for torn tofu; H2 "deja enfriar un poco" instead of "deja templar".
- Snapshots: the engine and solver-result snapshots only add the 13 recipes (no existing line changed); the scenario snapshot changes the first and next sets because more recipes qualify (owner approved).
- E2E: "close alternatives when nothing meets the targets" also excludes bread; `tuna_chickpea_salad` (no equipment, no egg, no rice) met cut under the old conditions. With bread excluded, nothing meets and two recipes are near.
- Library (73 recipes): cut 25, maintain 33, bulk 19; chicken 13, pork 13, beef 12, fish 14, egg 10, soy 11; 55 quick (75 %); 4 fried (5 %); Japanese 29 (40 %), Western 12, Chinese 8, Spanish 7, Latin American 5, Southeast Asian 4, Korean-style 3, Mediterranean 3, Okinawan 2; ≤ 10 minutes 7; `batch` 7; `breakfast` 7. ADR-023 is met as revised.
- Speed: `npm run bench:recommendation` (Node, median) cut 11.3 → 13.5 ms and bulk 10.7 → 13.1 ms from 60 to 73 recipes; `npm run test:e2e:timing` at 4×: cut 74.7 ms, bulk 69.4 ms (release 2: 72.8–73.6 ms); at 6×: 112.2 and 103.3 ms. Scaling the 4× time by the Node ratio gives about 88 ms for 86 recipes, within the ADR-030 D10 threshold, so batch 2 needs no speed work first.
- Browser check (built preview): the 13 detail pages at desktop width and 375 px in three locales (78 views) without page overflow or missing keys; the per-ingredient table scrolls inside its frame as on existing pages. Not checked: candidate cards of the new recipes, real devices.
- Checks: `npm run validate:content` (90 foods, 73 recipes), `npm run check` (640 tests), `npm run test:e2e` (98 passed, 1 skipped), `npm run report:composition` (73 recipes: exact 515, approximation 96, hard to estimate 2, not counted 29, fried 5), `npm run eval:coverage`.

### Stage 7.3 batch 2: plan and foods (2026-10-25)
- Engine evaluation of the 13 planned recipes: oily fish has about as much fat as protein (raw chub mackerel 20.6 g protein and 16.8 g fat, yellowtail 21.4 and 17.6 g per 100 g), so salt-grilled mackerel and yellowtail teriyaki stay near for every preset (maintain: protein 28 g; bulk: fat 31 g with protein 45 g).
- Owner decisions (2026-10-25): E1 keep salt-grilled mackerel with grated daikon, labelled maintain (near), like `tortilla_espanola` a recipe that meets no preset; E2 replace yellowtail teriyaki with swordfish teriyaki, labelled maintain (meets it), and leave yellowtail out of the foods; E3 commit the foods before the recipes. The other 12 planned recipes and their amounts and bounds are approved as evaluated (each meets the preset of its label; about 1,150 grid states in total, at most 140 per recipe).
- Composition subset: 7 codes added (02006, 02007, 06048, 06049, 06134, 10154, 10156); 162 foods extracted, the existing entries unchanged.
- Cooking tables: weight change rates 02007 (steamed from 02006, 98 %, post-cooking refuse 表皮、両端, PDF p. 41), 06049 (boiled from 06048, 98 %, p. 44), 10156 (grilled from 10154, 77 %, p. 54). `npm run data:verify-cooking`: 88/88 entries verified.
- Foods (94 in total): `mackerel` (raw, grilled; mackerel allergen), `daikon` (raw value for grated daikon eaten with its juice), `kabocha` (raw, boiled), `sweet_potato` (raw, steamed; the steamed value is an approximation because the tables peel after cooking). Spanish names follow V1 (`Caballa`, `Rábano daikon`, `Calabaza kabocha`, `Boniato`) with regional aliases (`zapallo`, `auyama`, `batata`, `camote`). No recipe uses them yet, so no snapshot changes.
- Checks: `npm run validate:content` (94 foods, 73 recipes, `2026-10-25.1`), `npm run check`.

### Stage 7.3 batch 2: recipes (2026-10-25)
- Recipes (content version `2026-10-25.2`): `mackerel_salt_grill_daikon` (maintain label, near for every preset; E1), `swordfish_teriyaki` (maintain; E2), `chicken_steamed_chinese`, `tofu_sasami_steamed`, `beef_bulgogi`, `sundubu_tofu_soup`, `piperrada_egg_whites`, `natto_egg_white_omelette`, `salmon_miso_grill`, `salad_chicken_microwave`, `beef_kabocha_simmer`, `pork_fillet_sweet_potato`, `shrimp_egg_white_stir_fry`. Met counts per preset: cut 31 → 35, maintain 53 → 65, bulk 36 → 41.
- Food mapping: the eggs cracked into the sundubu are cooked until set and use the poached value, as in `egg_white_beans_ranchero`; kabocha simmered in broth uses the boiled value; steamed sweet potato uses the steamed value with its post-cooking refuse approximation; the miso salmon is cooked in a toaster oven (grilled value). Chili flakes have no table value, so the sundubu uses doubanjiang.
- Safety notes: new texts for small bones in fillets, microwave even heating of chicken, chopped tenderloin, beef strips cooked until they change color, eggs set inside, and refrigerated storage of miso-marinated raw fish (no storage duration); the other notes reuse existing texts.
- Owner review of the English and Spanish texts (2026-10-25, safety notes back-translated): J1 `Caballa asada con sal y daikon rallado` ("a la sal" means baked in a salt crust); J2 `Omelet de claras con natto` ("tortilla" is a flatbread in Mexico, and the dish is not Spanish, so the dish-name exception does not apply); J3 `Microwave chicken breast salad` / `Ensalada de pechuga de pollo al microondas` (not poached); J4 "espolvoréalas con la fécula" (a dusting, not a batter).
- Snapshots: the engine and solver-result snapshots only add the 13 recipes; the scenario snapshot changes the first and next sets (owner approved).
- Library (86 recipes): cut 28, maintain 40, bulk 22; chicken 15, pork 14, beef 14, fish 17, egg 13, soy 13; 66 quick (77 %); 4 fried (5 %); Japanese 35 (41 %), Western 13, Chinese 11, Spanish 8, Latin American 5, Korean-style 5, Southeast Asian 4, Mediterranean 3, Okinawan 2; ≤ 10 minutes 11; `batch` 11; `breakfast` 9; method families grill 7, simmer 26, stir-fry 18, pan 18, steam 8, no cooking 5, fried 4; microwave only 7. ADR-023 is met as revised. Grilling reaches 7, not the planned 9–10: pan-fried pork tenderloin and swordfish count as `pan` in the method family (ADR-029 D5).
- Speed: `npm run bench:recommendation` (Node, median) cut 15.2 ms and bulk 14.7 ms on 86 recipes (23,041 grid states); `npm run test:e2e:timing` at 4×: cut 85.3 ms (p90 95.4), bulk 79.1 ms (p90 88.9); at 6×: 125.0 and 114.4 ms. Within the ADR-030 D10 threshold of about 100 ms, with little margin left for further recipes.
- Browser check (built preview): the 13 detail pages at 1280 px and 375 px in three locales (78 views) without page overflow or missing keys. Not checked: candidate cards of the new recipes, real devices.
- Checks: `npm run validate:content` (94 foods, 86 recipes), `npm run check` (643 tests), `npm run test:e2e` (98 passed, 1 skipped), `npm run report:composition` (86 recipes: exact 600, approximation 116, hard to estimate 2, not counted 31, fried 5), `npm run eval:coverage`.

### Third production deployment (2026-10-25)
- CI passed on `c3b4911` (owner's report). The owner approved the deployment proposal, then the production deployment after confirming the account.
- Cloudflare documentation re-checked on 2026-10-25: static asset requests are free and unlimited and storage has no cost (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/billing-and-limitations/, updated 2026-04-23).
- Wrangler 4.152.0 (still the latest) from the Stage 6.6 installation in the session scratch directory; `npm audit` there: 0 vulnerabilities with the `undici` 7.30.1 override. `wrangler whoami`: account [account name] (`[account ID]`), confirmed by the owner; `wrangler deployments list` showed release 2 (`81bc7642…`) at 100 %. Telemetry disabled by `WRANGLER_SEND_METRICS=false`.
- Build: `npm ci && npm run build` on `c3b4911` (content version `2026-10-25.2`, 9 files, 1.03 MB). Dry run: Wrangler read `dist/` (9 files plus the `assets` directory, as for release 1), 0.44 KiB of Worker upload, no bindings, nothing uploaded. Deployment (2026-10-25 02:44 UTC): 7 new or changed assets uploaded (`index.html`, the JavaScript and CSS bundles, the calculation bundle, and the three text bundles), 1 already present; version `992ce5ff-56e4-4cf3-85d8-7b8ff6f37ab5` at 100 %, same URL.
- Post-deployment checks (`curl -I` and the built-in browser): `/`, `/recipes/beef_pho_rice_noodles`, `/recipes/tofu_sasami_steamed`, `/about`, `/recipes/no_such_recipe`, an unknown path, and `/third-party-licenses.txt` return 200 with the CSP, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, and `Permissions-Policy`; `/assets/*` has `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, the rest `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`; `/_headers` returns the app's HTML, not the file; `index.html` references the new bundle and the calculation bundle has content version `2026-10-25.2` and 86 recipes. In the browser at 375 px: the targets view lists nine cuisine choices in Spanish with all checkboxes at 20 × 20 px; a maintain search preferring Southeast Asian shows `chicken_coconut_curry` first, then `natto_tofu_rice_bowl` and `piperrada_egg_whites`; detail pages of `tofu_sasami_steamed` (`ja`), `arroz_con_pollo_beans` (`en`), `beef_pho_rice_noodles` (`es`, "Ternera o res, Gamba o camarón (puede contener)"), and `mackerel_salt_grill_daikon` (`ja`) without overflow; the about page has eight sections and content version `2026-10-25.2`; every request goes to the same origin; the only stored key is the locale preference; no cookies; no console messages.

### Stage 8.1 shopping list design (2026-10-25)
- Facts checked in the repository content (86 recipes, 94 foods, 749 ingredients): every food is used on one amount basis only (40 foods have only the `as_is` state and are always used as sold; the others are always stated in raw edible grams), and forms bought differently are separate foods (`rice_cooked`, `pasta` in dry weight, `tomato_canned`, `udon_boiled`), so combining by food ID never adds different bases. Roles: seasoning 254, vegetable 184, main protein 114, fat 89, main carbohydrate 88, other 11, coating 9; 26 foods appear as seasoning or fat. 31 ingredients are `includedIn` another (26 oils, 4 coatings, 1 vinegar); frying oil appears in 4 recipes (250–600 g). 10 foods use both teaspoons and tablespoons across recipes. A new search resets the detail adjustments (`searched` in `src/state/reducer.ts`), so a list that followed them live would change silently.
- Owner decisions (2026-10-25): every recommended option of the seven design items (ADR-032); at most 10 recipes; the Spanish title "Lista de la compra" (peninsular; "lista de compras" in Latin America); the ADR-016 re-check of `i18next` and `react-i18next` (not before 2026-10-26 12:40 UTC) as its own commit between stages, shipped with release 4 instead of a separate deployment.
- Documents: [SPEC.md](../en/SPEC.md) §3, §4, §13, and the new §18; [ARCHITECTURE.md](../en/ARCHITECTURE.md) (layer `domain/shopping`); [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §2, §3.4, §3.5, the new §3.7, §4, §6.

### Stage 8.2 shopping list module (2026-10-25)
- `domain/shopping/list.ts` (ADR-032 D1–D5): `buildShoppingList(entries, recipes)` takes the entries in the order added (recipe ID and `RecipeState`) and the bundle recipes, and returns items with key, food, frying flag, section, grams at full precision, household measure total, and per-recipe contributions, or typed errors (`too_many_recipes`, `duplicate_recipe`, `unknown_recipe`, `invalid_state` with the `resolveAmounts` errors). `changedItemKeys(previous, next)` names items whose total changed or that disappeared, for clearing checks. `SHOPPING_LIST_MAX_RECIPES` is 10. No locale parameter, no nutrition value, no change to `domain/nutrition` or `domain/recommendation`.
- Frying oil is detected with `isFriedIngredient` of `shared/recipe.ts` on the resolved target of `includedIn` (after substitutions) for ingredients with role `fat`.
- The household measure rule (scaled with the grams, shown only as a multiple of 1/12) moved from `src/views/detail/model.ts` to `shared/measure.ts`, so the detail view and the list share it; the detail view behaves as before (`test/node/detail-model.test.ts` unchanged and passing).
- Tests: `domain/shopping/list.test.ts` (19 tests with fixtures: combination across recipes and states, servings, adjusted and linked amounts, sides, substitutions, included and frying oil, measure totals, sections, order, errors, the limit, determinism, no locale parameter, `changedItemKeys`); `test/node/shopping.test.ts` (one amount basis per food in the calculation bundle; every recipe at its default state lists all resolved grams; a snapshot of four selections: one recipe, three changed recipes, the four fried recipes, and ten recipes).
- Snapshot observations: frying oil is 1,600 g of vegetable oil for karaage, tonkatsu, and tempura together and 250 g of olive oil for the tortilla, apart from the oils used otherwise; same-unit totals are shown (soy sauce 3.5 tsp from two recipes), while olive oil in the ten-recipe selection has none because one recipe states it without a measure; coatings are in the ingredients section.

### Stage 8.3 shopping list view (2026-10-25)
- Navigation and state: view `shoppingList` at `/` with the view name in `history.state`; `shoppingList` in the reducer (entries in the order added and checked item keys), kept by a new search; actions `shoppingSaved` (add at the end or replace in place, at most 10), `shoppingRemoved`, `shoppingCleared`, `shoppingChecked`. Views compute the checks to clear with `changedItemKeys` and pass them in the action (`src/views/shopping/changes.ts`), because the reducer has no content. A page load that opens `shoppingList` with an empty list shows the targets view with the reload notice; a list emptied by the user shows its empty state.
- UI: a header link with the recipe count while the list is not empty; a "Shopping list" section after the adjustment section of the detail view (add; in the list with its servings and a link; update and remove when the current state differs; disabled with the reason at 10 recipes); `src/views/ShoppingListView.tsx` with the recipes (links that open the detail in the entry's state, summaries, remove), copy as text (Clipboard API, a selected read-only text area as the fallback, disabled when every item is checked), print (`window.print()`; header, buttons, checkboxes, and disclosures hidden and an empty box printed), clear all with an inline confirmation, the basis note, the two sections with checkboxes, per-recipe disclosures, and the frying oil note. Focus moves after removal and clearing; the live regions announce adding, updating, removing, clearing, and copying. Texts in `src/views/shopping/text.ts`; the copied text follows [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §3.7. The about page's privacy section and the reload notice now mention the shopping list.
- Catalogs: namespace `shopping`, `nav.views.shoppingList`, `about.privacy.shoppingList`, and a revised `nav.reloadNotice` in three locales; `en` and `es` drafted by Claude and accepted as drafted in the owner's review (2026-10-25, review sheet with Spanish back-translations), including food names kept capitalized after "Frying oil:" and the existing "enter them again" ending of the reload notice.
- Tests: `src/views/shopping/text.test.ts` (copied text in three locales, summaries), `test/ui/shopping.test.tsx` (add, update, list items equal to the module's, checks cleared on change, removal and focus, copy and its fallback, print, clearing with confirmation, language change, the limit, the reload notice, axe-core in three locales), reducer and URL tests, `e2e/shopping.spec.ts` (two recipes from the candidates, list, check, copy, print media, reload; three projects × three locales), and the shopping list in the axe E2E (light and dark).
- Browser check (built preview, 2026-10-25): the list with karaage and salmon tempura at desktop width and 375 px in `es`, `ja`, and `en`, dark and light; no page overflow (scroll width equals the viewport); the detail block in its "differs" state at 375 px in `en`; amounts unchanged across language switches; no console messages. Not checked by hand: real devices, screen readers, a real print dialog and paper output (print styles checked with print media emulation in E2E), the Clipboard API on a real phone.

### Stage 9.2 recommendation speed: plan and baseline (2026-10-25)
- The owner approved the plan with Q1 B (a synthetic library spread evenly over the repository recipes) and Q2 A (a committed, on-demand browser variant).
- Synthetic library (`tools/synthetic-library.ts`, measurement only, never published): the 86 recipes plus 44 recipes at even intervals (index ⌊i × 86 / 44⌋) again under `<id>_copy`, 130 recipes in total. `npm run bench:recommendation` uses it instead of the first 40 recipes again (Stage 6.2), and `TIMING_LIBRARY=synthetic npm run test:e2e:timing` serves it through a Playwright network stub (`page.route` on the content bundles; the copies reuse their originals' texts) and checks that 130 recipes were served. Grid states: 23,041 for the 86 recipes (median 126 per recipe) and 38,046 for the 130: the 44 copies add 15,005 (341 on average), because the even spacing includes `beef_steak_potatoes` (5,376 states, the largest) and `chicken_mapo_tofu` (1,368). It therefore stands for about 44 new recipes near the ADR-023 limit of about 400 states each, not for average ones.
- Profile, Node (Node 24.19.0, Apple M1, `--cpu-prof` over 300 cut and bulk searches after warm-up; scratch scripts outside the repository; share of non-idle time on 86 / 130 recipes): solver grid loop (`visit`, the linear prediction closure, `fromVector`, fit, cost) 45.9 / 48.5 %; nutrition engine 34.2 / 33.1 % (linear model probes about 22.6 / 22.5 %, the default state of each solve 6.8 / 5.8 %, the recalculation of the chosen state 4.8 / 4.8 %); solver setup (adjustables, side masks, slopes) 8.8 / 8.5 %; garbage collection 4.7 / 4.8 %; hard filters and validation 1.4 / 1.3 %; selection (`selectCandidates`) 0.7 / 0.6 %. Largest self times: the linear prediction closure 12.0 / 22.7 %, `visit` 17.3 / 12.5 %, `calculateIngredient` 7.0 / 6.6 %, `record` 6.5 / 6.3 %. Every pool entry still uses the linear method. The probes and the default state depend on the recipe, the forced state, the servings, and the side mask, not on the targets.
- Browser split at 4× (Chromium Headless Shell 156, Pixel 7 profile at 375 × 812, `ja`): the engine alone (`evaluatePool` and `selectCandidates` imported in the page from the Vite dev server, cut / bulk median) takes 51.9 / 51.7 ms on 86 recipes and 78.0 / 78.9 ms on 130 (1×: 13.8 / 13.0 and 18.6 / 18.4 ms; 6×: 79.6 / 79.1 and 121.7 / 121.4 ms), against about 80 ms for the whole UI search, so about 28 ms at 4× is UI. A CPU profile of an unminified production build (scratch only) puts about 26 ms of that in React: rendering about 15 ms (the five `RecipeCard`s about 9 ms, i18next `t` about 4.5 ms, number formatting with a new `Intl.NumberFormat` per call about 2.3 ms) and the commit about 11 ms, of which the heading's `focus()` in a layout effect about 9 ms (it forces the style and layout that the measurement waits for anyway). A timeline trace gives about 6.5 ms of layout and 1.8 ms of style per search at 4×.

Two runs give identical files (digest `13b71dd3…`). Its detection of an injected change is checked in Stage 9.3 before it is used.
- Options considered (4× gains estimated from the profiles): P+O3, the linear model probes and the default state of each recipe precomputed once per bundle in `recommendationDataFromBundle` for servings 1 and the unforced state, as immutable data (other states computed as now; no mutable cache, no module state), about −15 ms per search on 86 recipes and −23 ms on 130, moved to content loading; O5, the prediction of the grid loop updated incrementally with prefix sums per depth, in the same order of the same operations, about −5 to −10 ms (86) and −10 to −15 ms (130); O4, pruning states and side masks whose cost lower bound exceeds the current best's cost plus 1e-9 once that best meets every target (such states would be rejected in the original order too, so the sequence of best states is unchanged), gain unknown; U1, reusing `Intl.NumberFormat` instances in `src/format/`, about −2 ms; a Web Worker (does not shorten the wait; not pursued) and coarser grids for the heaviest recipes (changes results; not pursued).
- Owner decision (2026-10-25): option A: P+O3, O5, and U1; O4 as a prototype, kept only if it saves at least about 5 %. Target at 4× against a same-session baseline, for cut and bulk: median ≤ 65 ms and p90 ≤ 75 ms on the 86 recipes; median ≤ 90 ms and p90 ≤ 100 ms on the synthetic 130 recipes; the added content loading time is measured and reported.

### Stage 9.3 recommendation speed: equality tests (2026-10-25)
- The owner approved starting Stage 9.3 as planned, with the ADR-016 re-check (Stage 9.1) as its own commit after 2026-10-26 12:40 UTC, between the optimization commits if needed.
- Before optimizing: `test/node/recommendation-digests.test.ts` keeps a 16-hex-digit SHA-256 digest of the complete pool, the first selection (seed 0), and the next set (seed 1 with the first set shown) for 240 requests on the repository recipes (six condition sets, 20 target combinations, servings 1 and 2); all 240 digests differ, and two runs give the same snapshot (about 4 s). It complements `test/node/solver-results.test.ts` (eight requests), the engine snapshot, and the scenario snapshot.
- Detection checked by adding 1e-7 to the cost of a switched-off side in `domain/recommendation/solver.ts` (as in Stage 6.2): the digest test and `test/node/solver-results.test.ts` failed (the scenario snapshot did not), and the scratch comparison of 5,760 requests changed 836 of them (418 on each library). The change was reverted.

### Stage 9.3 P+O3: precomputed solver results (2026-10-25)
- `domain/recommendation/solver.ts`: `precompute(recipe, engine)` returns the engine result of the default state and the linear model (values at the default amounts and one slope per active ingredient) of every combination of solver-removable sides, for one serving without forced sides or substitutions. `recommendationDataFromBundle` builds them for every recipe as part of `RecommendationData` (`precomputed`, by recipe ID), which the UI already builds once per calculation bundle (`src/state/providers.tsx`). `SolveInput` takes them as an optional field, so `solve` keeps its two parameters (the type-level locale invariance test is unchanged); `solve` uses them only for one serving without forced sides or substitutions, and otherwise computes as before. The search setup moved into `prepare`, shared by `search` and `precompute`, so both derive the adjustables, the states, and the active ingredients the same way. Within a search, the combination without sides off now takes its values at the default amounts from the default state already computed (the same engine call on the same state). No mutable cache and no module state.
- Equality: the same functions on the same inputs give the same numbers. Tests: `domain/recommendation/solver.test.ts` (solutions with and without precomputed results on the hand-made recipes; one model per side combination; results of another recipe are ignored for two servings, forced sides, and forced substitutions, and change the result where they apply) and `test/node/solver-precomputed.test.ts` (every repository recipe × four target sets × one and two servings, each removable side locked, each other side forced off, and each substitution forced: identical solutions; the data's results equal `precompute`). The engine snapshot, `test/node/solver-results.test.ts`, the scenario snapshot, the digest test, and the determinism and locale invariance tests pass without updates; the scratch comparison of 5,760 requests is identical to the baseline (digest `13b71dd3…`).
- Node (`npm run bench:recommendation`, base `6889a7e` extracted with `git archive` and run with the same `node_modules`, alternating twice; median / p90, ms): 86 recipes cut 15.2–15.4 → 10.1–10.2 (−33 %), bulk 14.8–15.1 → 9.7–9.8 (−35 %), maintain with tofu required 2.9 → 2.0–2.3, bulk at ≤ 15 min 5.7–5.8 → 3.4–3.6; 130 recipes cut 22.2–22.7 → 14.6–15.3 (−34 %), bulk 21.9 → 14.5 (−34 %), 4.5–4.7 → 3.0, 8.8 → 5.3–5.4. Building `RecommendationData` takes 5.8 ms (86) and 7.7–8.3 ms (130) instead of 0.06 ms, once per calculation bundle. Browser timings follow after the remaining options, against the same-session baseline.

### Stage 9.3 O5: incremental prediction in the grid loop (2026-10-25)
- `domain/recommendation/solver.ts`: an evaluator now returns a scorer with `set(depth, value)` and `evaluate(values)`. The linear scorer keeps one partial sum per depth: `set` computes the next level from the previous one as `sum + rate × (value − default)`, so a complete state's prediction is the sum of the same terms in the same order as the single sum per state before (slope order equals the order of the active ingredients, which the scorer checks), and an unknown value or rate makes the prediction unknown as before. The leaf only reads the last level. The exhaustive scorer ignores `set`.
- Equality: every test passes without updates (engine, solver results, scenarios, digests, determinism, locale invariance, precomputed results); the scratch comparison of 5,760 requests is identical to the baseline. `domain/recommendation/solver.test.ts` adds a recipe with three adjustable ingredients, whole units from zero, an unmeasured carbohydrate, and a removable side to the linear-equals-exhaustive cases.
- Node: `npm run bench:recommendation` was too noisy on this machine at the time (background load; `1038a73` read 11.3–13.2 ms where it had read 10.1 ms), so a scratch script timed `1038a73` and this change in one process, alternating runs (41 runs after 5 warm-up runs, two rounds; median / p90, ms): 86 recipes cut 10.2–10.3 / 11.3–11.7 → 9.4–9.6 / 10.6–11.0 (−7 to −9 %), bulk 10.0–10.1 → 8.9–9.1 (−10 %); 130 recipes cut 15.5–16.0 → 13.9–14.5 (−9 to −10 %), bulk 15.6–15.9 → 14.0–14.1 (−10 to −12 %). Less than the profile suggested; the leaf still converts and evaluates the fit for every state.

### Stage 9.3 O4: pruning by the relative change (2026-10-25)
- Prototype in `domain/recommendation/solver.ts`, kept because it saves more than the agreed 5 %. Once the best state so far meets every target, a state whose relative change exceeds the best one's by more than the tie margin (1e-9) loses to it (`compareCandidates`: met fits compare equal, so the cost decides), whatever its fit. The search skips a side combination when its switched-off terms alone (1 each) exceed that, and a value at a depth when the switched-off terms plus the terms of the values set so far exceed it; every term is at least 0, so these are lower bounds. A skipped state would not have replaced the best state at the point where the search reaches it, so the sequence of best states, and the result, stay the same. The bound is summed in another order than the cost, so the comparison adds a margin of 1e-12 (rounding of these sums of at most about 20 terms below 10 is about 1e-14). The exhaustive search (reference and fallback) uses the same search and prunes the same way.
- Equality: every test passes without updates; the scratch comparison of 5,760 requests is identical to the baseline.
- Node (scratch script, `41697e8` against this change in one process, alternating, 41 runs after 5 warm-up runs, two rounds; median, ms): 86 recipes cut 9.3–9.5 → 8.1–8.5 (−11 to −14 %), bulk 9.0–9.2 → 7.9 (−13 to −14 %); 130 recipes cut 13.9–16.3 → 11.9–13.4 (−15 to −18 %), bulk 14.0–16.0 → 12.0–14.1 (−12 to −15 %).

### Stage 9.3 U1: reused number formatters (2026-10-25)
- `src/format/format.ts`: every `Intl.NumberFormat` comes from a small map keyed by the locale and the options, instead of a new formatter per call (Stage 9.2 measured about 2 ms per search at 4× for creating them). A formatter's output depends only on its locale and options; the keys are three locales times a handful of option sets. UI only; no engine or value changes. `src/format/format.test.ts` adds a check that alternating locales and option sets keep each one's output.
- Checks: `npm run check` (687 tests) and `npm run test:e2e` (107 passed, 1 skipped) pass. Built preview (`npm run preview`, 2026-10-25): a default search in `en`, then `es` and `ja`, at desktop width and at 375 px; the detail view of `beef_potato_tomato_stew` in `es` at 375 px shows salt as "2,4 g"; no page overflow; no console messages. Its timing is part of the browser comparison that follows.

### Stage 9.3 precomputation after the first view (2026-10-25)
- `domain/recommendation/data.ts`: `recommendationDataFromBundle(calc, { precompute: false })` returns the data without precomputed solver results (an empty map); `withPrecomputed(data)` returns the same data with them, sharing the engine data, foods, and recipes. The default (`precompute: true`) is unchanged for tools and tests.
- `src/state/providers.tsx`: `ContentProvider` renders with the data without precomputed results, then a `setTimeout(0)` started in an effect after the first commit builds them and replaces the data in the context (the views read the data only in renders and event handlers, so nothing resets). A search before that gives the same result without the speed-up (`solve` computes what is missing).
- Tests: `test/ui/providers.test.tsx` (the first render has no precomputed results; then every recipe has them, with the same engine data and recipes objects); `test/node/solver-precomputed.test.ts` (without precomputed results: an empty map; `withPrecomputed` equals the default data's results; the pools of the three presets are identical with and without). `npm run check` (689 tests) and `npm run test:e2e` (107 passed, 1 skipped) pass. Built preview at 375 px in `es`: a default search shows the same five candidates; no page overflow; no console messages.
- Content loading at 4× (same scratch script, three builds served side by side, two rounds in alternating order; time from the last content bundle response to the targets form, median / p90, ms): 86 recipes baseline 91.1 / 95.2 and 73.3 / 75.0, option A (`67132f0`) 114.0 / 114.5 and 113.3 / 113.6, option B 72.5 / 81.6 and 82.3 / 91.5; 130 recipes baseline 76.9 / 77.6 and 71.3 / 71.9, A 144.9 / 147.3 and 129.2 / 129.8, B 85.5 / 92.3 and 74.4 / 75.7. Option B shows the form as fast as the baseline. The precomputation then occupies the main thread once (about 40–70 ms at 4× on 86 recipes by the difference of A); an input in that short time waits for it.
- Search timing with option B against the baseline (same session, alternating, load average 3.0–3.7; median / p90 at 1×, 4×, 6×, ms): 86 recipes cut 12.5 / 14.7, **50.6 / 58.5**, 76.2 / 81.5 (baseline 20.5 / 23.4, 83.2 / 92.6, 120.4 / 137.0), bulk 11.8 / 13.7, **50.1 / 54.4**, 72.9 / 80.5 (baseline 19.2 / 22.6, 76.7 / 88.6, 115.4 / 129.6); 130 recipes cut 16.0 / 18.2, **63.4 / 70.9**, 95.7 / 107.3 (baseline 26.7 / 30.9, 110.8 / 123.1, 162.1 / 188.7), bulk 15.3 / 18.3, **62.3 / 72.0**, 93.5 / 109.7 (baseline 25.2 / 30.5, 107.9 / 128.3, 153.9 / 167.1). ### Stage 9.1 ADR-016 re-check and dependency update: i18next 26.5.12 and react-i18next 17.1.9 (2026-10-31)
- Re-check (npm registry, 2026-10-31 00:55 UTC): after the burst that ended with `react-i18next` 17.1.8 (2026-10-24 12:39 UTC), `react-i18next` 17.1.9 (2026-10-25 17:19 UTC) and `i18next` 26.5.11 (2026-10-25 18:34 UTC) and 26.5.12 (2026-10-30 09:19 UTC) followed. Within the 48 hours before the installation only `i18next` 26.5.12 was published, which is not a burst under ADR-016 (several releases within about 48 hours). The owner approved moving both to the latest versions now (option A; option B was to wait until 26.5.12 was 48 hours old).
- Changes read in both changelogs: bug fixes; the ones near this app's use are `{{…}}` inside interpolated values staying literal (`i18next` 26.5.4–26.5.5; the catalogs and content have no such values) and the fuzzy matching of `supportedLngs` (26.5.10; the app supports `ja`, `en`, `es` only and detects the locale itself). Peer ranges: `react-i18next` 17.1.9 requires `i18next >= 26.2.0` and TypeScript `^5 || ^6 || ^7`; `i18next` 26.5.12 TypeScript `^5 || ^6 || ^7`.
- `package.json` now uses caret ranges for both (`^26.5.12`, `^17.1.9`), as for every other dependency, because the ADR-016 exception ends. Only these two packages changed in the lockfile; `npm install` under `strict-allow-scripts` reported no install script; `npm audit --omit=dev`: 0 vulnerabilities (a development-only finding in `minimatch` is handled with the linting tools, next commit). `npm run check` (689 tests) and `npm run test:e2e` (107 passed, 1 skipped) pass; the JavaScript bundle grows from 496,396 to 496,756 bytes.

Re-check when `minimatch` publishes a fixed 10.x or the tools move to 11.x (open question 22).

### Stage 10.1 expansion plan (2026-10-31)
- Owner decisions (2026-10-31, the recommended options): D1 44 new recipes in three batches (15, 15, 14) for about 130 in total, with ADR-023 revised; D2 no new cuisine value (South Asian dishes would rely on curry powder alone, because the tables have no cumin, turmeric, or garam masala); D3 no new fried recipes (a breaded fish fry would need a new cooking method or another label for `tonkatsu`, whose Japanese label is とんかつ); D4 textured soy protein (粒状大豆たんぱく) at its dry raw value with the raw-value band, like rice noodles; D5 two more dishes in the `no_cook` family only (the proteins eaten without cooking are canned fish, boiled octopus, natto, and tofu, so more would skew the library to fish and cans); D6 the limitation of [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §3 corrected to the 16 recipes of "Phase 10 start"; D7 the synthetic library grows to 180 recipes in batch 1 (from 86 recipes, 94 copies are not possible: `copiedRecipeIds` allows at most one copy per recipe); D8 kimchi (shrimp) and oyster sauce (wheat, soybean) linked as may contain and listed under open question 12.
- Batch 1 (15): `soy_meat_tofu_lettuce_wraps` (cut, soy, Chinese, stir-fry), `soy_meat_bolognese_pasta` (bulk, soy, Western, batch), `gyeran_jjim_egg_white_shrimp` (cut, egg, Korean, ≤ 10 minutes, microwave only, steam, breakfast), `dakgalbi_chicken_sweet_potato` (maintain, chicken, Korean), `pork_kimchi_tofu` (cut, pork, Korean), `chicken_souvlaki_yogurt` (cut, chicken, Mediterranean, oven toaster, grill), `lentil_sasami_soup` (maintain, chicken, Mediterranean, batch), `octopus_chickpea_salad` (cut, fish, Mediterranean, ≤ 10 minutes, no cooking), `gapao_chicken_rice` (bulk, chicken, Southeast Asian), `cod_ginger_fish_sauce_steam` (cut, fish, Southeast Asian, microwave only, steam), `huevos_pericos_egg_whites` (maintain, egg, Latin American, ≤ 10 minutes, breakfast), `goya_champuru_pork` (maintain, pork, Okinawan), `chum_salmon_foil_mushroom` (cut, fish, Japanese, oven toaster, steam), `soy_meat_soboro_bowl` (bulk, soy, Japanese, batch), `cottage_egg_white_scramble_toast` (cut, egg, Western, ≤ 10 minutes, breakfast).

No policy change.

### Stage 10.3 batch 2: recipes (2026-10-31)
- Owner decision (2026-10-31): the ingredient amounts and adjustment bounds of the 15 recipes as evaluated with the engine.
- Recipes (content version `2026-10-31.4`): `beef_napa_spicy_soup`, `tofu_soy_meat_jorim`, `beef_eggplant_moussaka_lite`, `greek_egg_white_spinach_omelette`, `pork_noodle_salad_bun`, `khao_pad_egg_shrimp`, `chicken_tinga_rice`, `carne_asada_corn_salsa`, `soy_meat_chili_sin_carne`, `okinawa_soba_lean_pork`, `beef_asparagus_rolls`, `sardine_kabayaki_rice`, `steamed_chicken_cabbage_gyoza`, `tofu_soy_meat_napa_steam`, `pollo_al_ajillo_breast`. Every recipe meets the preset of its goal label after adjustment; met counts per preset: cut 45 → 55, maintain 80 → 93, bulk 45 → 52. Beef recipes meeting cut gain 4 (soup, carne asada, asparagus rolls, moussaka), soy recipes meeting cut 2 → 3, fish recipes meeting bulk gain 1 (sardine kabayaki).
- Changes against the Stage 10.1 plan, from the evaluation: the moussaka simmers its chopped beef before baking and the carne asada is seared in a frying pan (the tabulated grilled beef round has too much fat for cut), so both count as `simmer` and `pan` instead of `grill` (ADR-029 D5), and grilling is expected to reach 11 instead of 13 after batch 3; the khao pad counts as `pan` (scrambled egg). The bún uses boiled pork (a tabulated value) instead of grilled pork; the gyoza and the moussaka use chopped lean meat instead of mince (fat); the Greek omelette uses egg whites only and cottage cheese instead of feta (no table value); the pollo al ajillo uses 6 g of olive oil. The moussaka got a bread side and less oil so that its energy range stays above the maintain minimum with fat at most 20 g; the carne asada salsa got red kidney beans so that its protein range stays above 35 g.
- Adjustment grid: 487 states for the 15 recipes (at most 120, `beef_eggplant_moussaka_lite`).
- Owner review of the English and Spanish texts (2026-10-31, review sheet in two parts listing only texts not reviewed before, new safety notes back-translated): no findings. Fixed by Claude before the review: L1 the English pollo al ajillo step uses "medium heat" (glossary rule); L2 "la duodécima parte" instead of "una doceava parte" in the gyoza (standard form of the fraction).
- Safety notes: the existing doneness, `mince_doneness`, `egg_set`, `shrimp_doneness`, `hot_water`, `hot_oil` (tofu), `hot_dish`, `steam`, `fish_doneness`, `cooling`, and `wash_raw` texts; new texts for cooking beef round through (carne asada; [CONTENT_POLICY.md](../en/CONTENT_POLICY.md) §3), pan-fried thin beef slices, small bones in butterflied sardines, gyoza filling doneness, freezing uncooked gyoza (no storage duration), and hot thickened sauce.

### Phase 10 speed decision before batch 3 (2026-10-31)
- On 116 recipes a full recommendation takes 66–82 ms median and 72–93 ms p90 at 4× ("Stage 10.3 batch 2: recipes"); batch 3 is expected to bring the p90 to about 80–90 ms, above the ADR-023 note's limit of about 80 ms and below the ADR-030 D10 threshold of about 100 ms.
- Options: A raise the library limit to the ADR-030 D10 threshold (about 100 ms p90 at 4×) and continue with batch 3, measuring again after it; B a speed stage before batch 3 with every engine output unchanged, as in Phase 9 (gain unknown in advance); C a smaller batch 3 (for example 8 recipes, about 124 in total).
- Owner decision (2026-10-31): A. ADR-023 is revised accordingly; if the measurement after batch 3 exceeds about 100 ms p90 at 4×, the phase stops and options are proposed before release 7.

### Seventh production deployment (2026-10-31)
- The owner pushed `dev` (`cb086c1`) and reported CI passing, approved the Wrangler download, approved the deployment proposal of [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §5 up to the dry run, confirmed the account, and approved the production deployment.
- Cloudflare documentation re-checked on 2026-10-31: static asset requests are free and unlimited and storing assets has no additional cost (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/billing-and-limitations/, updated 2026-04-23).
- Wrangler 4.156.0 (still the latest) installed in a new folder of the session scratch directory with `npm install --ignore-scripts --save-exact` (integrity `sha512-kVLAShuL…fRuQ==`, the same as for release 6; 202 MB in `node_modules`; the install scripts of `esbuild` 0.28.2 and `workerd` 1.20261023.1 not run; `miniflare` 5.20261023.3-beta with `undici` 7.30.1); `npm audit` there: 0 vulnerabilities. The Node and npm versions, the global npm prefix and packages, the absence of a global `wrangler` command, and the absence of `~/.npmrc` were identical before and after. Telemetry disabled by `WRANGLER_SEND_METRICS=false`; the dry run still printed Wrangler's general telemetry notice.
- `compatibility_date` in `platform/cloudflare/wrangler.jsonc` was already 2026-10-31 (set for release 6), so `cb086c1` was deployed without a further commit. `wrangler whoami`: account [account name] (`[account ID]`), OAuth login of the earlier deployments, confirmed by the owner; `wrangler deployments list` showed release 6 (`4607ad0c…`) at 100 %.
- Build: `npm ci && npm run build` on `cb086c1` (content version `2026-10-31.7`, 130 recipes, 117 foods; 9 files, about 1.3 MB). Dry run: Wrangler read `dist/` (10 entries), 0.45 KiB of Worker upload, no bindings, nothing uploaded.
