### Stage 12.1 provider and platform check (2026-10-31)
- *Verified* (Anthropic pricing, https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing): per million tokens, Claude Haiku 5.5 $0.10 input and $0.50 output for prompts up to 100,000 tokens (cache hits $0.01), Claude Sonnet 5.5 $2 and $10 (cache hits $0.10), Claude Opus 5.5 $4 and $20; the Batch API halves token prices; US-only inference (`inference_geo: "us"`) costs 1.1×, global routing is the default. No free allowance is listed.
- *Verified* (Anthropic rate limits, https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/rate-limits): monthly spend caps per tier (Start $500, Build $1,000, Scale $200,000); an organization can set its own lower spend limit in the Console billing page, and requests over it return HTTP 400; new organizations may start in an Evaluation tier with lower limits.
- *Verified* (Anthropic structured outputs, https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs): `output_config.format` with a JSON schema is supported by `claude-haiku-5-5` and `claude-sonnet-5-5`; constrained decoding, except on refusals and `max_tokens` stops; enum casing may differ; `minLength`, `maxLength`, `minimum`, and `maximum` are not supported in the schema, so the endpoint validates them itself.
- *Verified* (Anthropic privacy center, "How long do you store my organization's data?", updated 2026-07-01, and "Is my data used for model training?", updated 2026-08-18): API inputs and outputs are deleted within 30 days by default, with exceptions for usage-policy enforcement and legal requirements; zero data retention needs an agreement; inputs and outputs of commercial products are not used for training by default.
- *Verified* (Workers AI pricing, updated 2026-10-20, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/platform/pricing/): 10,000 neurons per day free, then $0.011 per 1,000 neurons on Workers Paid; for example `@cf/meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast` $0.293 input and $2.253 output per million tokens.
- *Verified* (Workers AI model catalog, updated 2026-08-12, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/models/): no text-generation model names Japanese, Spanish, or French; some say "multilingual". JSON mode (updated 2026-09-14, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/features/json-mode/) is listed for six models and does not guarantee schema conformance. Limits (updated 2026-09-17): 300 requests per minute for models that do not need Workers Paid. Privacy (updated 2026-10-16, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/platform/privacy/): no training on customer content; inputs and outputs are not retained after the request unless a stateful feature such as AI Gateway logging is used.
- *Verified* (Workers pricing, updated 2026-10-02, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/pricing/): Free plan 100,000 requests per day and 10 ms CPU per invocation; Workers Paid from $5 per month with 10 million requests included.
- *Verified* (Workers Rate Limiting binding, updated 2026-10-21, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/rate-limit/): limits are approximate and counted per Cloudflare location. Whether the binding is available on the Free plan is not stated (要確認).

### Stage 12.2 contract, validation, and fallback (2026-10-31)
- `shared/suggestion.ts`: the provider-neutral HTTP contract (`POST /api/suggest-conditions`), its `zod/mini` schemas, the output validation, and the browser-side interpretation with the deterministic fallback ([ARCHITECTURE.md](../en/ARCHITECTURE.md) §7 "AI free-text conditions contract"). The request holds only the text (1–200 code points after trimming) and the locale. A suggestion has the shape of the engine's `Conditions` without allergens: required any-of food sets (5 × 5), excluded foods (10), `maxMinutes` (5–240, equal to `INPUT_LIMITS`, tested), equipment, and cuisines; there is no free-text field. Out-of-scope topics: `allergens`, `targets`, `other`.
- Validation order: output length (4,000 characters), the forbidden expressions of `content/glossary.ts` in all four locales on the raw text, JSON, forbidden key stems (targets, nutrients, allergens, health, heat, cooking time, free text), the strict schema, dictionary membership, required-and-excluded contradiction; duplicates are removed. The browser validates the response again; 404 counts as `disabled`, an empty suggestion as `empty`, and every failure gives the message key `ai.error.<code>` or `ai.outOfScope.<topic>`. The catalog strings come with the UI in Stage 12.4.
- `server/ai/`: a new layer for the platform-neutral endpoint core (`handleSuggest`: environment switch, request check, provider call raced against a 7 s timeout, output validation, error status), the `SuggestionAdapter` interface with `AdapterError`, and a fake adapter. `eslint.config.js` lets `server/` import only `shared/` and forbids the other layers to import `server/`; `tsconfig.app.json` type-checks it. Alternative: everything in `shared/` (rejected: `shared/` holds schemas, types, and constants, not request handling).
- Not built: the Anthropic adapter, any SDK or dependency, the Worker, `platform/cloudflare/` changes, the rate limiter, and the UI. Tests use the fake adapter and no network (`test/node/suggestion.test.ts`, `test/node/suggest-endpoint.test.ts`).
- 要確認: Anthropic structured outputs may return `null` for an absent optional field; the contract rejects `null`, so the Stage 12.3 adapter must send a schema without nullable fields or map them. The status codes for `disabled` and `budget_spent` (503) and for provider failures (502) are this stage's choice.

### Provider change: Workers AI free allocation only (2026-10-31)
- The owner decided (2026-10-31) that the AI feature must run within the Workers AI free allocation and that no billing is acceptable in any form. This replaces the Claude API of ADR-034 D2 and the spend limit of D5. No Anthropic account, key, or credit is needed. Recorded as revisions of ADR-034 and ADR-015.
- Also checked on the same day: Max, Ultra, and Team plans include monthly Claude API credits ($100 for Max) without a payment method, and API requests stop when they run out (https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/api-credits-for-subscribers). Not chosen: the owner excluded the Claude API.
- *Verified* (Workers AI pricing, updated 2026-10-20, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/platform/pricing/): 10,000 neurons per day at no charge on Workers Free and Workers Paid; past any limit, further operations fail with an error; Workers Free has no overage price ("Upgrade to Workers Paid"). Some models need a paid billing method (the kimi, glm, and deepseek-v4 models). Neurons per million tokens, input and output: `@cf/qwen/qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` 4,625 and 30,475; `@cf/google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` 9,091 and 27,273; `@cf/openai/gpt-oss-20b` 18,182 and 27,273; `@cf/meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast` 26,668 and 204,805.
- *Verified* (JSON mode, updated 2026-09-14, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/features/json-mode/): six models, including `llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast`; Workers AI cannot guarantee a response that follows the JSON Schema and returns the error "JSON Mode couldn't be met" instead. The model pages of `qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` ("multilingual support", 32,768-token context) and `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` (no language list, 256,000-token context) list a `response_format` parameter without details and name no plan requirement.
- *Estimated*: with about 3,000 input tokens (the dictionary in the request locale only) and 150 output tokens, a request uses about 19 neurons with `qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` (about 500 requests per day), 31 with `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` (about 300), 59 with `gpt-oss-20b` (about 170, more with reasoning tokens), and 111 with `llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast` (about 90). One evaluation run of 240 cases fits in one day's allocation for the first two models.
- 要確認: the owner's Cloudflare account is on Workers Free; when the daily allocation resets; the Rate Limiting binding on Workers Free; the 10 ms CPU limit per invocation of Workers Free for the endpoint (to be measured); how the evaluation script reaches Workers AI (an API token limited to Workers AI or Wrangler), which needs the owner's approval in Stage 12.3.

### Stage 12.3 evaluation set and script (2026-10-31)
- `data/ai-evaluation/conditions.json` (version 1): 60 intents, each with one locale-neutral expected output and one original request text in `ja`, `en`, `es`, and `fr` (240 cases). Categories: one required food (10), several required items or any-of sets (5), time (7), exclusions (7), equipment (5), cuisines (6), several fields (8), no condition (2), out of scope (10: allergens 4, targets 3, other 3, including a prompt injection). Regional vocabulary: `es` zapallo, camote, jitomate, palta, papas, camarones, hongos, setas, estufa, ternera, gambas; `en` aubergine, prawns, courgette, coriander and cilantro, hob. The file records its source (drafted by Claude) and a review status per locale, all `pending`, and the mapping rules the expected outputs follow (group words, "or" and "and", minutes, equipment implied by a pan or pot, ignored preferences, out-of-scope topics); the provider prompt must state the same rules.
- `tools/ai-evaluation/` and `npm run eval:ai` (on demand, never in CI): every case goes through `handleSuggest`, so a rejected output counts as no output. Metrics: food-ID precision and recall pooled over required and excluded IDs, accuracy per field (sets compared without order), the forbidden rate, out-of-scope declines and topics, errors and rejection reasons, and cross-locale consistency (all four locales return the same valid output). The ADR-034 D4 thresholds are checked, and the script exits with 1 when one fails. Only the fake adapter `expected` exists: it returns each case's expected output and scores 100 % on every metric, which checks the pipeline and that every expected output passes the runtime validation.
- Not done: the owner's review of the 240 texts (`es` and `fr` with back-translations, open questions 9 and 23), the Workers AI adapter and prompt, and any run against a model. No account, token, or Cloudflare change.
- 要確認: "not spicy" has no field and is ignored in `flagship_chicken_20` (an alternative is excluding the chili ingredients); "no eggs" excludes `egg` and `egg_white`; gluten-free counts as an allergen request.
- Owner review (2026-10-31): the owner accepted all 240 texts and expected outputs as drafted, from a review sheet with Japanese back-translations of the `es` and `fr` texts and Claude's findings on regional vocabulary (kept outside the repository). The three open points were decided as recommended: "not spicy" stays ignored, "no eggs" excludes `egg` and `egg_white`, and gluten-free stays an out-of-scope allergen request. The review status of every locale is `reviewed`; `es` and `fr` were reviewed without a native reviewer (open questions 9 and 23).

### Stage 12.3 Workers AI adapter and prompt (2026-10-31)
- *Verified* (2026-10-31): the model pages of `@cf/qwen/qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` and `@cf/google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` take `messages` and `response_format`, return an OpenAI-style `chat.completion` with `choices` and `usage`, and show `env.AI.run(model, { messages })` and the REST endpoint `POST https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/{account_id}/ai/run/{model}` with a bearer token; gemma lists `max_completion_tokens` and marks `max_tokens` deprecated. JSON mode (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/features/json-mode/, updated 2026-09-14): `response_format: { type: "json_object" | "json_schema", json_schema }`, and the result arrives as an object in `response`. Errors (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/platform/errors/, updated 2026-09-17): 3036 (429) daily free allocation used up, 3040 (429) out of capacity, 3007 and 3008 (408) timeout and aborted, 5035 (403) model needs Workers Paid.
- `server/ai/prompt.ts`: an English system prompt with the contract, the enums, the rules of the evaluation set, and the dictionary of the request locale (ID, name, and aliases); the user message passes the text between markers as data. `outputJsonSchema` mirrors `providerOutputSchema`, with the food IDs as an enum.
- `server/ai/workers-ai.ts`: `workersAiAdapter` with an injected transport, the candidate models of ADR-034 (revised), at most 400 output tokens, and temperature 0. It reads `response` (a string or an object) or the first chat completion, drops a leading `<think>` block and a Markdown code fence, maps the error codes above (anything else to `unavailable`), and turns a JSON mode failure into an empty output, which the validation rejects as `not_json`. Tests use a fake transport (`test/node/workers-ai.test.ts`); no transport, token, or call exists yet.
- *Measured*: the system prompt has 6,167 characters in `ja`, 7,377 in `en`, 8,036 in `es`, and 8,296 in `fr`, and the JSON Schema 3,915 characters. Input is therefore larger than the 3,000 tokens assumed in "Provider change: Workers AI free allocation only": roughly 2,500–5,000 tokens (要確認 until `usage` is read in a real run). With 5,000 input tokens a request uses about 28 neurons with `qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` (about 350 per day) and about 50 with `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` (about 200 per day); one run of 240 cases fits in a day for qwen but needs two days for gemma (`--locale` splits the run).
- 要確認: whether the two candidate models honour `response_format` (their pages do not list its variants); whether Qwen3 spends output tokens on reasoning within the 400-token limit; the error format of the Worker binding (the adapter reads a numeric `code` or a code in the message); whether the JSON Schema counts as input tokens.

### Stage 12.3 Workers AI REST transport (2026-10-31)
- The owner chose the Workers AI REST API with an API token limited to Workers AI for the evaluation (2026-10-31), over Wrangler. The token, the account, and every run stay within the free allocation; the account must stay on Workers Free.
- *Verified* (REST API get-started, updated 2026-09-15, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/get-started/rest-api/): the dashboard's Workers AI page → "Use REST API" → "Create a Workers AI API Token" (a custom token needs "Workers AI - Read" and "Workers AI - Edit"); the Account ID is on the same page; the response envelope has `result`, `success`, `errors`, and `messages`.
- `tools/ai-evaluation/workers-ai-rest.ts`: `restRunner` posts the adapter's inputs with the bearer token, turns `success: false`, a non-OK status, or a non-JSON body into an error with the first Workers AI code (so 3036 maps to `budget_spent`), and adds the reported `usage` tokens; `neurons` converts them with the verified rates. `credentialsFromEnv` reads `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` and `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`; `.env.example` has placeholders only, and `.env` is git-ignored. The token is never printed.
- `npm run eval:ai`: `--adapter workers-ai --model <id>`, `--intent <id>` to try a few cases, `--cases` for one line per case, and the usage with neurons in the output. A run stops at the first `budget_spent` and exits with 1 when incomplete. Tests use a fake `fetch` (`test/node/workers-ai-rest.test.ts`).
- Not done: no token exists and no request was sent. Next: the owner creates the token and `.env`, then a run of a few intents to measure tokens and neurons, then the full runs.

### Stage 12.3 Workers AI runs and the switch to a deterministic parser (2026-10-31)
- The owner created a Workers AI token limited to Workers AI - Read and Edit, with the account ID in a local `.env` (git-ignored, never shown). All runs used the REST API within the free allocation; about 2,000 of the day's 10,000 neurons, no billing.
- Run 1 (3 intents × 4 locales, prompt of "Stage 12.3 Workers AI adapter and prompt"): `qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` returned 7 of 8 in-scope outputs that failed the schema (`equipment: []`, invented `maxMinutes: 240`), and "no eggs" became an allergen request once; 270 neurons for 12 requests. `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`: 5 of 12 over the 7 s timeout, "no eggs" declined as allergens in 3 locales; 200 neurons for 7 answered requests. Both declined the egg allergy in every locale.
- Run 2 (same cases, Qwen): rules to omit unstated fields and to keep "no X" as an exclusion, and JSON Schema limits (`minItems`, minimum and maximum) gave 10 of 12 usable answers. The two Japanese failures were Qwen3 reasoning that used up the 400 output tokens (a truncated JSON object) and the 7 s timeout.
- Run 3: Qwen3's `/no_think` soft switch (Qwen3 model card, https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-30B-A3B) removed the reasoning (output tokens 2,690 → 380 for 12 cases) and the timeouts, but invented times and equipment returned. Explicit group lists and two few-shot examples (not evaluation intents) gave no errors on the 12 cases.
- Run 4 (12 intents × 4 locales = 48 cases, 762 neurons): food precision 77.9 %, recall 74.3 %, cross-locale consistency 25.0 %, forbidden 0 %, out-of-scope declined 100 %, topic 50 % (thresholds 95 %, 90 %, 90 %, 0 %, 95 %). Systematic errors: "fish" excluded shellfish, squid, octopus, and fish sauce while missing salmon or cod; a group word became separate required items instead of one any-of set; unstated `maxMinutes` and `equipment` in about 40 % of conditions; "no eggs" excluded `egg` without `egg_white`.
- Conclusion: the gap to the D4 thresholds is too large for prompt changes; reasoning mode would exceed the 7 s timeout and the 8 s browser limit. The owner chose the deterministic parser without AI, the fallback named in ADR-034 (revised) (2026-10-31). Recorded as the second revision of ADR-034; the Workers AI adapter, prompt, REST transport, their tests, and `.env.example` are removed. Owner steps: delete the Workers AI token in the dashboard and the local `.env`.
- Evaluation set version 2: `egg_tomato` requires the any-of set `egg`, `egg_white`, like the exclusion of `no_egg` (owner, 2026-10-31), so the group word "eggs" means the same in both directions.

### Stage 12.4 deterministic parser (2026-10-31)
- `shared/freeText/parse.ts` (`parseConditions`, `parserFoods`) and `shared/freeText/lexicon.ts` (word lists per locale): pure, no network, no AI; the browser and the evaluation build the same dictionary (names, aliases, and names without their qualifier in parentheses, from the bundles or `content/foods.ts`; a test checks they are equal). `normalizeForSearch` moved to `shared/search.ts` so Node tools can load it; `src/views/targets/foodSearch.ts` re-exports it. Alternative: the parser in `src/` (rejected: `node tools/evaluate-ai.ts` cannot load the UI's extensionless imports).
- Rules: out-of-scope words first (allergens, then health, recipe generation, and instructions, then nutrition words); food names and group words by longest match, a group word winning at equal length; other lexicon words (cuisines, equipment, meal words such as 晩ごはん) are read whole first, so a food inside them is not read; word boundaries, plurals, and singulars outside `ja`; in `ja`, a kana word of two characters is not read inside a longer kana run; a negation applies within its clause to the foods after it (`en`, `es`, `fr`) or before it (`ja`); "or" between two foods of a clause joins them into one any-of item; a time from a number and a unit or a duration phrase, ignored outside 5–240 minutes; a pot or pan adds the stove; `fr` keeps maïs and où apart from mais and ou. Group words: chicken, pork, beef, and eggs are their protein groups; fish is the fish protein group without shrimp, squid, and octopus; mushrooms are shimeji, shiitake, and eringi.
- `SUGGEST_LIMITS`: `foodsPerItem` 5 → 12 and `excludedFoods` 10 → 20, so "fish" (9 foods) fits as one item; the old limits guarded against provider output.
- Evaluation (`npm run eval:ai`, `--adapter parser` is now the default; `--split` alternates the intents by position, 30 each). Tuning used only `dev`. First `holdout` run, before any change after seeing it: food precision 96.4 %, recall 94.6 %, forbidden 0 %, declined 100 %, topic 80 %, cross-locale consistency 80.0 %: the consistency threshold (90 %) failed. General gaps found there were then fixed (singular forms, "odio", "déteste", lexicon words read whole, the order of the out-of-scope topics), so `holdout` is no longer unseen. After the fixes: `dev` 100 % on every metric; `holdout` precision 100 %, recall 98.2 %, consistency 96.7 %; all 240 cases precision 100 %, recall 99.4 %, consistency 98.3 %, all thresholds met. Left as a limitation: elliptical alternatives ("Chicken thigh or breast", "Muslo o pechuga de pollo") read only the full name.
- 要確認: the owner's review of the word lists in four locales (ADR-007, ADR-033; `es` and `fr` without a native reviewer, open questions 9 and 23); whether a fresh set of request texts written by the owner should confirm the result, since `holdout` was seen; the shape of the endpoint core (`server/ai/`) and the HTTP parts of `shared/suggestion.ts`, which the UI no longer needs (Stage 12.5).

### Stage 12.5 free-text conditions UI (2026-10-31)
- The owner reviewed the word lists of `shared/freeText/lexicon.ts` in four locales and accepted them without changes (2026-10-31); `es` and `fr` stay without a native reviewer (open questions 9 and 23). No owner-written set of fresh request texts: the evaluation stays as recorded in "Stage 12.4 deterministic parser".
- UI (`src/views/targets/FreeTextConditions.tsx`, `src/views/targets/freeText.ts`): a collapsible field below the time limit; "read" parses in the browser and validates with `validateValue`; the result is a labelled list with "apply" and "discard" in a polite live region; applying adds required items (a whole protein group keeps its group chip, other sets get the key `any:<ids>`), excluded foods, and cuisines, replaces the time limit and the equipment (mode "choose"), opens "More conditions" when something there changed, and never changes targets or allergens. Out-of-scope messages per topic; the allergen message has a button that moves focus to the first allergen checkbox. Catalog keys `freeText.*` in `ja`, `en`, `es`, and `fr`; the UI never says "AI".
- Contract reshaped: `server/ai/` (endpoint core, adapter interface, fake adapter) and its test are removed; `shared/suggestion.ts` keeps the request schema, the output schema, `validateValue`, `validateProviderOutput`, and `isEmptySuggestion`, without the endpoint path, timeouts, error codes, response schema, or `interpretResponse`. The evaluation calls the parser and `validateProviderOutput` directly. The `server/**` lint rules and the `server` entry of `tsconfig.app.json` are removed.
- Tests: `src/views/targets/freeText.test.ts` (apply), `test/ui/freeText.test.tsx` (every locale: read, apply, discard, allergen pointer with focus, nothing found, axe-core), and an E2E case in `e2e/flow.spec.ts` per locale and project (no network request while reading, then a search). Texts in these tests are written for them, not taken from the evaluation set.

### Workers Builds migration check (2026-10-31)
- Goal (owner, 2026-10-31): production deploys only through the owner's push, no local Wrangler login, Workers Free only, and no billing in any form (shared `deployment.md` and `paid-services.md`). Alignment with my-music-site, where every push to `main` is a production deploy.
- *Verified* (Builds overview, updated 2026-10-28, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/): an existing Worker is connected under Settings → Builds → Connect (GitHub or GitLab); a build of the production branch creates a version and, with a deploy command, promotes it to the active deployment; a build of another branch creates or updates a Preview. The Worker name in the dashboard must match `name` in the Wrangler config of the root directory, or the build fails.
- *Verified* (Builds configuration, updated 2026-09-22, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/configuration/): settings are Git branch (default `main`), build command (optional), deploy command (default `npx wrangler deploy`, customizable), preview command (default `npx wrangler preview`), root directory ("where the build command will be run"), API token (created automatically: account settings read, Workers Scripts, KV, and R2 edit, Workers Routes edit, user details and memberships read), and build variables; preview builds run for other branches only when enabled; Workers Builds uses the Wrangler version of `package.json`; `CI=true`, `WORKERS_CI=1`, and `WORKERS_CI_BRANCH` are set; build variables are not available at runtime.
- *Verified* (build image, updated 2026-10-29, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/build-image/): Ubuntu 24.04 x86_64; Node.js 24.22.1 by default (22.23.4 also installed), selected by `NODE_VERSION`, `.nvmrc`, or `.node-version`; npm 10.9.2 by default, set exactly by `packageManager` in `package.json`; the lockfile selects the package manager; `SKIP_DEPENDENCY_INSTALL` disables the automatic install.
- *Verified* (troubleshooting, updated 2026-10-30, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/troubleshoot/): the name check reads the config from the root directory; with Wrangler 3 or later Workers Builds overrides the name through `WRANGLER_CI_OVERRIDE_NAME`; a missing config gives "Missing entry-point"; `npm ci` fails when the lockfile was written by a newer npm than the image's ("set `packageManager`"); builds over 20 minutes are stopped; logs are kept 90 days.
- *Verified* (limits and pricing, updated 2026-10-28, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/limits-and-pricing/): Workers Free 3,000 build minutes per month, 1 concurrent build, 20-minute timeout; paid plans 6,000 minutes, 6 concurrent, overage $0.005 per minute. Not stated: what happens on Workers Free when the minutes run out, and whether a payment method is involved (要確認). A community reply says the site stays up (https://community.cloudflare.com/t/workers-free-plan-does-site-stay-up-if-build-limit-hit/966726); not an official source.
- *Verified* (Workers pricing, updated 2026-10-02, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/pricing/): requests to static assets are free and unlimited; Workers Free is the default plan. *Verified* (compatibility dates, updated 2026-04-23, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/compatibility-dates/): old dates are supported forever; update occasionally after reading the compatibility flags and testing. *Verified* (rollbacks, updated 2026-07-15, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/versions-and-deployments/rollbacks/): dashboard rollback to one of the 100 most recent versions; static assets and Workers Builds are not mentioned.
- Not found in the documentation: a Workers Builds example of `wrangler deploy --config <path>` with the config outside the root directory; whether the automatic install is `npm ci`; what Wrangler version is used when `package.json` has none; how preview builds are turned off once on (the page says they run only when enabled).
- Measured locally (2026-10-31, npm 11.17.0): `npm config get strict-allow-scripts` is `true` in the repository and `false` with `--prefix` set to a directory outside it, so an install into a temporary prefix is not subject to the repository's `.npmrc`. `git diff 2aa0c7b..dev` (release 8 to `dev`) touches no build input except one `package.json` script (`eval:ai`). Wrangler `latest` is 4.156.0 (2026-10-29, registry metadata), the version of releases 7 and 8.
- Found: `main` (`773ea57`, 2026-10-25) is 53 commits behind `dev`, and releases 2–8 were deployed from `dev` commits that are not on `main`. A build of the current `main` would deploy an older version, so `main` must reach the release commit before the repository is connected (ADR-035).

The owner accepted the word lists without a fresh set of texts ("Stage 12.5 free-text conditions UI").

- Open question 22 re-checked: unchanged. `eslint` 10.14.0 (latest) requires `minimatch` ^10.2.6 and `@typescript-eslint/typescript-estree` 8.72.1 requires ^10.2.2; the advisory covers `<11.0.1`, and `minimatch` 11.0.3 is the latest. `npm audit`: the same 12 moderate findings; `npm audit --omit=dev`: 0.
- ADR-017 re-checked: unchanged. `typescript-eslint` 8.72.1 (latest) declares peer `typescript >=4.8.4 <6.1.0`; `typescript` latest is 7.0.2.

### Stage 13.1 decisions (2026-11-01)
- The owner accepted every recommendation of Stage 13.1 (2026-11-01): ADR-036, with revisions of ADR-015 and ADR-030 D9 and SPEC §3 and §13.
- *Verified* (MDN, read 2026-11-01): "Storage quotas and eviction criteria" (last modified 2026-01-05): Web Storage allows up to 5 MiB of local storage per origin, and a write beyond the quota throws `QuotaExceededError`; data is best effort and is evicted with all of the origin's data under storage pressure; Safari with cross-site tracking prevention deletes script-written data of an origin without user interaction in the last seven days of browser use; private browsing usually deletes data when the mode ends. "Window: localStorage" (last modified 2026-10-12): data has no expiration time, is scoped to the origin and protocol, and stores strings only; accessing `localStorage` throws `SecurityError` when the user prevents the page from persisting data.
- Consent (要確認): the EU ePrivacy Directive Article 5(3) exempts storage that is strictly necessary for a service the user explicitly requested, and summaries of the Article 29 Working Party Opinion 04/2012 (WP194) say user-interface customization storage is likely to be exempt (Future of Privacy Forum and Hunton summaries, read 2026-11-01; the full opinion was not read). The switch is the user's explicit request, the record has one purpose, and nothing is sent, so no consent banner is planned. Japan's external transmission rules of the Telecommunications Business Act concern sending user information to other parties; nothing is sent, so they are taken not to apply (not checked against the primary source). Neither point has had a legal review. The owner accepted saving without a further consent step (2026-11-01, after release 10; "Phase 13 acceptance").

### Stage 13.2 storage module (2026-11-01)
- `src/state/savedConditions.ts` reads, writes, and deletes the ADR-036 record under `recipe-discovery.conditions`, with a `zod/mini` schema and every storage access wrapped; no new dependency. It is not used by the UI yet, so the app still writes nothing new.
- Cuisines, equipment, and allergens are stored as strings and checked against the current lists on restore, so that a value removed by a later release is dropped with a notice instead of deleting the record. Strings are limited to 64 characters and lists to 100 items.
- With version 1 as the first version, a record with a lower version fails the schema and is deleted; a higher version is reported as unsupported and kept (ADR-036 D5).
- A group key is restored only while the ingredient search offers that group (more than one food), so restored choices match what the user can pick.
- `npm run check`: 60 test files, 856 tests (18 new), content validation, and the production build pass.

### Stage 13.3 saved-conditions UI (2026-11-01)
- The targets view has a "Save on this device" fieldset above the search button: the save checkbox, the allergen checkbox while saving is on, the shared-browser note, the delete button, and a status for "deleted" and "could not be saved". A restored record shows a status at the top with the number of dropped items and its own delete button; the reload notice then says only that search results and the shopping list are not kept (`nav.reloadNoticeSaved`). The about page's privacy text is updated in four locales.
- The record is read once in a layout effect of the current view after the content loads (`src/state/useRestoreSavedConditions.ts`), so the restored form appears before the first paint; `cleared` keeps the saved-conditions status. "Find recipes" writes the form as typed before validation (ADR-036 D4, D5). Applying a relaxation on the candidates view does not write; the next "Find recipes" does.
- Observed limitation: restored values inside "More conditions" stay in the collapsed section; the restored notice says that the form was restored. Phase 14 may revisit this.
- Tests: `test/ui/savedConditions.test.tsx` (7 cases in each of the four locales, axe-core on the restored, dropped, and write-failed states) and `e2e/savedConditions.spec.ts` (per locale and project: no new key with saving off; save, reload, restore, the same cards after a search, turn off, reload, defaults). One existing test queried the only `role="status"` element and now queries its text.
- Checks: `npm run check` (61 test files, 884 tests; content validation; production build) and `npm run test:e2e` (164 passed, 1 skipped by design).
- Browser checks (dev server, built-in browser): at 375 px in Japanese, turning saving on wrote one record without allergens, and a reload restored it with the notice; at desktop width in French, the fieldset and its texts; no page overflow and no console errors in either.

### Phase 13 acceptance (2026-11-01)

### Dependency update: i18next 26.6.0 (2026-11-01)
- `i18next` 26.5.12 → 26.6.0 (published 2026-10-31 23:19 UTC, a single release, so ADR-016 exception 2 does not apply); `react-i18next` 17.1.9 accepts `>= 26.2.0`. `package.json` now requires `^26.6.0`.
- Impact checked before the update (`npm diff` of the two published packages, 2026-11-01; changelog in the `i18next` repository): only `package.json`, `dist/esm/package.json` (version), and the type definitions `typescript/options.d.ts` and `typescript/t.d.ts` change. The new `strictInterpolationChecks` type option defaults to `false`, and the selector-array overloads now require `count` for plural members. The app uses the string-key API with `CustomTypeOptions` limited to `defaultNS` and `resources` (`src/i18n/i18next.d.ts`), no selectors, no `strictKeyChecks`, and no `defaultValue`, so neither change applies. No install script (ADR-018).
- Checks: `npm ci`, `npm run check` (884 tests; the built `index-CgzjGBWD.js` keeps the same content hash as release 10, so the runtime bundle is unchanged), `npm run test:e2e` (164 passed, 1 skipped; a first run had one WebKit accessibility case fail and pass on rerun, with an identical bundle), `npm audit --omit=dev` (0); `npm audit` reports the accepted `minimatch` findings only. `npm outdated` lists only the recorded exceptions (`typescript`, `@types/node`).

### Stage 14.1 decisions (2026-11-01)
- Mockups: three directions ("fresh market", "kitchen lab", "bento pop") as static HTML pages of the targets view and a candidate card, with original SVG icons and illustrations, captured with Playwright at 375 px and 1280 px in light and dark mode. Files stayed in the session scratch directory and the git-ignored `generated/`; nothing was published.
- The owner preferred "fresh market" for readability, its calm palette, and how natural it looks in dark mode, and asked for more play. Two variants followed: "soft pop" (pastel fills, two-tone icons, colored nutrient tiles, soft shadows) and "bold pop" (the same with ink outlines and offset shadows). The owner chose "soft pop" and accepted the recommendations for the other decisions (ADR-037).
- Contrast was computed with the WCAG 2.2 relative-luminance formula for every text, marker, border, and focus pair in both modes. The light `met` green `#2E7D3A` reached only 4.2–4.4:1 on the pastels and became `#23692E`.

### Stage 14.2 tokens and base components (2026-11-01)
- `src/styles.css` holds the ADR-037 tokens for light and dark mode, a page background `bg` separate from `surface`, a default radius of 14 px (`--radius`), a soft shadow in light mode only, and the utilities `btn-primary` (with a 4 px press shadow in the text color, pressed by 3 px), `btn-lg`, `btn-secondary`, `btn-outline`, `panel`, `notice-warning`, and `chip`. Buttons and panels keep a transparent border, so forced-colors mode draws their edges.
- The blue `accent` became `primary` (green) in every class (`bg-`, `text-`, `border-`, and `accent-` for checkboxes and radios); `accent` is now the tomato coral of the illustrations. Repeated button and warning-notice class lists were replaced by the utilities; no markup, text, or behavior changed.
- `test/node/styles.test.ts` checks 52 pairs per mode, including `bg`, `primary`, `on-sun`, and every text and status color on each pastel; all pass.
- Checks: `npm run check` (61 test files, 952 tests), `npm run test:e2e` (164 passed, 1 skipped; axe-core with color contrast in real browsers in four locales and both modes). CSS 19.3 kB (gzip 5.0 kB) against 17.2 kB; JavaScript 556,440 bytes (smaller by about 1 kB because the class lists are shorter). Screenshots of the targets and candidates views at 375 px and 1280 px in light and dark mode showed no overflow.

### Stage 14.3 icons and illustrations (2026-11-01)
- 31 original icons and 6 illustrations, drawn by Claude for this app, in `src/components/icons/`. Shapes are path data with a fill token each (`iconData.ts`, `illustrationData.ts`), so tests and a showcase read them without rendering; `Icon` and `Illustration` (`Icon.tsx`) draw them with strokes in `currentColor` and fills from the tokens (Tailwind `fill-*` classes; dashes as an SVG attribute, so the CSP needs no change).
- Icons: protein groups (chicken, pork, beef, fish, egg, soy), the six `EQUIPMENT` items, time, effort, servings, actions (search, save, delete, compare, shopping, copy, print, back, language, close), notices (warning, information), and the status markers `status_met` (filled circle with a check), `status_near` (half-filled circle), `status_not_met` (circle with a cross), and `status_unknown` (dashed circle with a question mark drawn as a path). Illustrations: `hero`, `no_results`, `empty_list`, `not_found`, `content_error`, `about`. No icons for cuisines, nutrients, or allergens (ADR-037 D3).
- An icon without a label is `aria-hidden`; with a label it has `role="img"` and that name. Illustrations are always `aria-hidden`.
- `test/ui/icons.test.tsx` (40 tests): every shape is plain path data, every icon renders on the 24 px grid with no `text`, `foreignObject`, or `image`, the label rule, axe-core on a labelled icon, distinct status marker shapes, and decorative illustrations.
- A showcase page in the git-ignored `generated/showcase/`, served by the dev server and captured with Playwright in light and dark mode, showed every icon and illustration with no console error. The views do not use them yet (Stage 14.4).

### Stage 14.4 views (2026-11-01)
- Every view uses the ADR-037 identity: view headings at 30 px weight 900; panels for the preset choices, "More conditions", the about sections, the detail sections, the shopping list sections, the relaxation options, and the comparison table; pastel fills for the free-text field (`sky`) and the saved-conditions fieldset (`butter`); preset choices as tinted cards; the hero illustration next to the targets heading (hidden below 640 px) and the about illustration; `no_results`, `empty_list`, `not_found`, and `content_error` in those states; the warning, approximation, and error glyphs replaced by icons; `StatusMarker` draws the SVG markers.
- Candidate cards: the main protein group's icon, the time icon, and the four nutrients as tiles in a 2 × 2 grid (`butter`, `mint`, `peach`, `sky` by nutrient, labelled), with the value at 20 px weight 800 and the range, status, and bound below it; the reason line in a tinted block. Markup and texts are unchanged apart from layout wrappers.
- Layout changes (ADR-037 D6): "More conditions" holds four groups with `h2` headings and icons: targets per meal, cooking (effort, cuisines, equipment), foods, and allergens. ADR-037 named the second group "time and effort"; it became "cooking" because the time limit stays outside as a quick input and the group also holds cuisines and equipment. The section opens when a restored record differs from the preset defaults inside it (set during render, not in an effect). On phones the search button is full width in a bar at the bottom of the screen; from 768 px it is a normal button.
- New strings: `targets.groups.*` in four locales. New test: the section stays closed for a restored time limit only and opens for a restored excluded food, with the four group headings, in four locales.
- Checks: `npm run check` (62 test files, 996 tests), `npm run test:e2e` (164 passed, 1 skipped; axe-core with color contrast in real browsers). CSS 22.0 kB (gzip 5.5 kB); JavaScript 566,967 bytes (gzip 170 kB), about 10 kB more than release 10 for the icons and illustrations now in use. Screenshots of the targets view (closed and open), candidates, detail, not found, and about in `ja` and `fr` at 375 px and 1280 px in light and dark mode: no horizontal overflow.

### Stage 15.1 decisions (2026-11-01)
- Owner decisions (2026-11-01, the recommended options): D1 the cuisine values `french`, `north_african`, and `west_african` (ADR-038, ADR-026 revised); D2 20 new recipes in two batches of 10 for about 150 in total (ADR-023 revised); D3–D4 the recipe list below; D5 the foods below; D6 the food safety and policy points below.
- Coverage check (`node tools/extract-composition.ts --search` against the workbook in `data/raw/mext/`, and `data/composition/`):
  - The engine has no reason for "a similar food used" (`Reason` in `domain/nutrition/engine.ts` covers cooking only). A food mapped to another table food would be shown as exact, so a missing ingredient is either left out, stated in the recipe, or replaced by another ingredient named for what the table contains (as `chili_pepper_powder` for gochugaru, "Stage 10.1 expansion plan"). Dishes that cannot work that way are dropped.
  - Not in the tables: couscous, cumin, turmeric, coriander seed, ras el hanout, harissa, saffron, preserved lemon, plantain, cassava (attiéké), fonio, bay leaf, shallot (エシャレット 06307 is young rakkyo), Espelette pepper, crème fraîche, gruyère, dried fish.
  - In the tables but not used: lamb, duck, rabbit, and veal (raw values only, and no protein group in `PROTEIN_GROUPS`), mussels (10289, raw only; see D6), bouillon cubes (17027; broad allergen links).

  - `chakchouka` (Tunisia, north_african, maintain, egg, 20 min, effort 1, breakfast; 0; harissa and cumin left out).
  - `poulet_yassa` (Senegal, west_african, maintain, chicken thigh, 40 min, effort 2, grill; 0).

Goal labels and families may move once each recipe is evaluated with the engine in its batch.

  - P1 wine and beer (`carbonnade_flamande`, `emince_zurichois_porc`) are allowed. Each such recipe gets a neutral note that some alcohol may remain after simmering, and a substitution with water. No health statement. Recipes with cooking sake are unchanged.

### Stage 15.2 batch 1: data, foods, and cuisine values (2026-11-01)
- Composition subset: 15 codes added (01031, 01122, 01168, 05037, 06010, 06011, 07037, 07038, 11176, 13032, 16006, 17017, 17035, 17059, 17071); 207 foods extracted, the 192 existing entries unchanged.
- Cooking tables: weight change rate 06011 (green beans boiled from 06010, 94 %, PDF p. 43). `npm run data:verify-cooking`: 100/100 entries verified. Grilled ham (11304, 79 %, p. 58) was transcribed and then dropped: a food used as sold cannot also have a cooked state (`test/node/shopping.test.ts`, one amount basis per food), so `ham` has its as-sold value only and `croque_monsieur` (batch 2) uses it like the melted cheese.
- Foods (131 in total): `ham` (pork contains; milk, soybean, and egg may contain; note on Japanese-style loin ham), `emmental` (milk), `buckwheat_flour` (buckwheat contains, wheat may contain, note on blends), `baguette` (wheat), `indica_rice_cooked`, `green_beans`, `black_olives` and `green_olives` (pitted weight, salt note), `thyme`, `dijon_mustard` (named "prepared mustard"; note that the table value is Japanese-style smooth mustard), `wine_vinegar`, `tomato_paste`, `peanut_butter` (peanut; sugar and salt note), `beer` (wheat may contain).
- Cuisine values `french`, `north_african`, and `west_african` added to `CUISINES` with UI labels in four locales and free-text keywords (ADR-038). The free-text parser now ignores a cuisine word inside a longer word of another cuisine, because "África occidental" and "Afrique occidentale" contain the `western` words "occidental(e)" (`shared/freeText/parse.ts`; test in `test/node/free-text-parser.test.ts`). The bundle stays compatible (enum values added only).

### Stage 15.2 batch 1: recipes (2026-11-01)
- Recipes (content version `2026-11-01.1`): `poulet_basquaise`, `cabillaud_ratatouille`, `salade_nicoise`, `galette_complete`, `hachis_parmentier`, `carbonnade_flamande`, `tajine_poulet_citron`, `chakchouka`, `poulet_yassa`, `mafe_boeuf`.
- Changes from the Stage 15.1 list made while fitting the goals with the engine:
  - `hachis_parmentier` uses boiled lean beef chopped by knife (the dish's leftover pot-au-feu origin) instead of ground beef, whose fat kept it out of every preset; 0 approximations instead of 1; goal maintain instead of bulk.
  - `carbonnade_flamande`: goal maintain instead of bulk (oil 8 → 6 g, potatoes 200 g).
  - `chakchouka` adds 100 g of egg white (raw value, 1 approximation) for protein, as `pisto_soft_egg` does.
  - `salade_nicoise`: two cans of tuna, a baguette side the solver may switch off, less oil and olives.
  - `galette_complete` reaches only near for maintain after adjustment (protein 30.1 g, fat 20.6 g); its egg, ham, and cheese define it. Its egg uses the fried-egg value, which includes oil uptake from Table 14, and the pan oil is counted through it.
  - P1: a substitution of the beer by water cannot be authored (a substitution needs a food), so `carbonnade_flamande` has the `alcohol` note only.

### Stage 16.1 decisions (2026-11-01)
- Documentation checked on 2026-11-01:
  - Vite "Dynamic Import" (https://vite.dev/guide/features#dynamic-import): variable paths match one directory level; code-split dynamic imports get a preload step for their shared chunks; `import.meta.glob` is lazy by default.
  - Vite `build.chunkSizeWarningLimit` (https://vite.dev/config/build-options#build-chunksizewarninglimit): 500 kB by default, compared against the uncompressed chunk size.
  - i18next "Add or Load Translations" (https://www.i18next.com/how-to/add-or-load-translations) and API (https://www.i18next.com/overview/api): inline resources, `addResourceBundle`, backends such as `i18next-resources-to-backend`; `changeLanguage` returns a promise that settles after loading.
  - react-i18next `useTranslation` (https://react.i18next.com/latest/usetranslation-hook): Suspense by default; with `useSuspense: false` the hook returns a `ready` flag.
- Prototype (a copy outside the repository, Vite 8.3.5, UI catalogs only behind `import()`): the main bundle drops from 568,294 bytes (gzip 168.8 kB at level 9) to 461,868 bytes (gzip 139.8 kB); the catalog chunks are 24,662 (`en`), 27,078 (`es`), 28,126 (`ja`), and 28,354 (`fr`) bytes, gzip 8.6–9.8 kB. The free-text word lists add about 2.1–2.7 kB of source per locale, so the main bundle is expected at about 453–455 kB. The icon and illustration data (about 9.5 kB of source) is used by the first view and stays in the main bundle.
- The owner approved the recommendations:
  1. Per-locale loading with a dynamic `import()` per locale before the first render (ADR-039).
  2. Logo direction A "discovery bowl": a bowl with a sprout and a rising sun, matching the targets illustration; 32 px grid, strokes in `currentColor`, token fills, no text inside, drawn with the existing `Shapes` data format. Directions B (lens and leaf; too close to the search icon) and C (a plate of four tiles; crowded at 28 px, and the colors could read as nutrients) were not chosen.
  3. Detail nutrition tiles: energy spans the full width; protein, fat, carbohydrate, and salt equivalent form a 2 × 2 grid, with salt on `surface-muted` and no target; the same layout at 375 px and in the desktop right column. Each tile holds the value, the range, the status marker and text (one decimal with "just below" or "just above"), and the target or "no target"; an unknown value shows "unknown" with the foods that lack data. The change summary, the range explanation, and the estimates note stay below the tiles with today's texts; approximation reasons stay in "How this is calculated". Tiles and the change summary keep a visible edge in forced colors.
- Mockups: local HTML rendered with Playwright in light, dark, and forced colors (`forcedColors: "active"`), at 375 px and desktop width, in `ja` and `fr`; not stored in the repository. The app itself is checked in Stage 16.3.
- 要確認: whether browsers retry a dynamic `import()` that failed once; Stage 16.2 tests it in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit and falls back to a page reload.

- Impact checked before the update (`npm diff` of the two published packages and their changelog, 2026-11-01): only `Trans`, `IcuTrans`, and their helpers change (values are escaped before the markup is parsed). The app does not use `Trans` or `IcuTrans`. No install script (ADR-018).

### Stage 16.2 per-locale loading (2026-11-01)
- ADR-039 applied:
  - Word lists: `shared/freeText/lexicon/<locale>.ts`, each carrying its `locale`; `shared/freeText/lexicon.ts` keeps the types and group words; `shared/freeText/lexicon/index.ts` is the index for Node tools and tests. `parseConditions(text, lexicon, foods)` takes the word list instead of the locale, so the parser never reaches another locale's list.
  - Chunks: `src/i18n/locales/<locale>.ts` re-exports the catalog and the word list; `src/i18n/localeData.ts` imports them with `import()` and adds them to the i18next instance. The word lists are kept per instance in a `WeakMap` keyed by `i18n.store`, because `useTranslation` returns a copy of the instance after each language change and every copy shares the store.
  - Startup: `src/main.tsx` renders after the chunk has loaded; `src/BootError.tsx` with `src/i18n/bootMessages.ts` (equal to `content.error` and `content.retry`, tested) shows the first-load failure, and its retry reloads the page.
  - Switching: `src/i18n/useLocaleSwitch.ts` loads the chunk and the text bundle in parallel, applies only the latest choice, and keeps the current language on failure. `LanguageSwitcher` shows `language.loading` (new key in four locales) with `aria-busy`; the retry state uses the new `LoadError` component shared with the content error.
  - `src/i18n/resources.ts` holds the four catalogs for tests only; tests create instances with `createTestI18n` (`src/testing/i18n.ts`). An ESLint rule forbids app code to import `src/i18n/resources` and `shared/freeText/lexicon/index`.
- The import retry (要確認 of "Stage 16.1 decisions") works in Chromium and WebKit: after an aborted chunk request, the retry loads it (`e2e/localeLoading.spec.ts`). Firefox is not in the E2E projects and stays 要確認.
- Tests: unit tests for the chunks against the indexes, the word list through a copy of the instance, and the boot messages (`src/i18n/localeData.test.ts`); the boot error in four locales with axe-core (`src/BootError.test.tsx`); loading status, failure and retry, and the latest choice on a switch (`src/App.test.tsx`). E2E: first render per locale loads only its chunk and shows no other language or key; a failed chunk and its retry at the first render and on a switch (`e2e/localeLoading.spec.ts`). Startup and switch timing with the HTTP cache disabled (`e2e/startupTiming.spec.ts`, project `timing`).
- Checks: `npm run check` (64 test files with 1,019 tests, production build without the chunk-size warning), `npm run test:e2e` (194 passed, 1 skipped by design; 30 new).
- JavaScript chunks (raw / gzip at level 9, bytes): `index` 456,235 / 137,411 (release 12: 568,294 / 168,809); `en` 26,529 / 9,444; `es` 28,920 / 10,314; `fr` 30,198 / 10,566; `ja` 30,511 / 10,640. Startup loads `index` and one locale chunk: at most 486,746 bytes, every chunk below Vite's 500 kB warning.
- Timing (`npx playwright test --project=timing`, alternating with release 12's code `cc1bf80` extracted with `git archive` and built with the same `node_modules`, each served by `vite preview` on the same port, three rounds, 2026-11-01 09:07–09:13 UTC, Chromium 156, 1-minute load average 6.7–13.8 at each start, so the numbers are noisy; median / p90, ms; release 12 in parentheses):
  - Search cut at 4×: 76.7 / 81.9, 81.1 / 107.1, 78.4 / 86.1 (74.2 / 83.3, 70.5 / 79.7, 117.1 / 141.9). Bulk at 4×: 71.3–77.0 (65.5–142.0). Re-suggestion at 4× 25.2–30.6 (25.4–30.9); recalculation at 4× 9.7–11.2 (9.7–12.5). The search path is unchanged; the p90 of 107.1 ms came in the round with the highest load.

### Stage 18.1 decisions (2026-11-08)
- Before captures: the local production build (`vite preview`, same tree as release 14 apart from documentation), with Playwright: the targets view, the candidates after a default search, the first card, and its detail view, in `ja` at 375 px and 1280 px and in `fr` at 375 px, in light and dark mode. They confirm the findings of the prompt: dark panels blend into the page, the dark pastels look brown, the fat tile and the allergen notice share one color, and small `muted` text on the tiles is hard to read. Files stayed in the session scratch directory; nothing was published.
- Dark palette proposal: a cool ink base with three surface steps instead of the warm brown base (`surface` against `bg` 1.10:1 → 1.23:1), a hairline edge and a dark shadow on panels, tile fills that keep their hue, the fat tile on a new `lilac` pastel in both schemes, a warning surface and edge of their own, and the small texts on tiles in `text` at 13 px instead of `muted` at 12 px. Contrast was computed with the WCAG 2.2 relative-luminance formula for the 52 pairs of `test/node/styles.test.ts` plus the new pairs (`muted` on `warning-surface`, `warning-edge` on `warning-surface` and `surface`, every foreground on `lilac`), 55 pairs per scheme: none fails. Lowest text pair 4.78:1 in light (`near` on `lilac`) and 6.22:1 in dark (`not-met` on `mint`). The tokens are set in Stage 18.2 and may move slightly when checked in the app; the test stays the gate.
- Mockups: three directions as static HTML with the app's own icon and illustration data, captured at 375 px in light and dark mode: A "soft pop refined" (the palette, the stepper fix, an illustrated loading state with a skeleton), B "discovery" (A plus nutrition bars against the target range, a "new this session" mark, a confirmation when a target becomes met, a translucent header and bottom bar, and motion on "show a different type"), and C "sticker display" (B plus a Latin display typeface, sticker-style badges, and a tinted heading panel). C used Avenir Next as a stand-in for an OFL typeface. Not stored in the repository.
- Documentation checked on 2026-11-08:
  - MDN `color-mix()` (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color_value/color-mix): Baseline widely available, across browsers since May 2023.
  - MDN `backdrop-filter` (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/backdrop-filter): Baseline 2024, newly available since September 2024, so not yet widely available. The translucent bars need an opaque fallback under `@supports not (backdrop-filter: blur(1px))`. Whether the build adds `-webkit-backdrop-filter` for older Safari is not verified (要確認, checked in the built CSS in Stage 18.3).
  - Tailwind CSS v4 dark mode (https://tailwindcss.com/docs/dark-mode): the `dark` variant follows `prefers-color-scheme` by default; `@custom-variant dark (...)` switches it to a class or data attribute. Not needed: the theme keeps following the OS setting.
  - `light-dark()`, View Transitions, and font subsetting tools are not used by the chosen direction and were not checked.
- Owner decision (2026-11-08): direction B with the palette proposal, as recommended (ADR-041). Left out: the display typeface, the in-app theme switch (no new storage key), cuisine badges (ADR-037 D3), and the sticker style of C.
- Stage order: 18.2 palette, warning color, small text on tiles, and the stepper fix; 18.3 nutrition bars, the "new this session" mark, the loading state, the translucent bars, and the two-column desktop detail view; 18.4 motion, forced colors, print, and the E2E suite; 18.5 release 15.
- 要確認: the count-up of changed values (listed for 18.4 in the proposal) shows numbers between two calculated values for a moment, which conflicts with showing only calculated values (principles 3 and 4); ADR-041 D5 uses a short highlight of changed values instead unless the owner decides otherwise before Stage 18.4.
- Owner decision (2026-11-08, after Stage 18.3): a short highlight of changed values, no count-up (ADR-041 D5 unchanged).

### Stage 18.2 dark palette, warning colors, and the stepper fix (2026-11-08)
- Tokens (`src/styles.css`, [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §5): the dark palette of the proposal; a new `lilac` pastel for the fat tile in both schemes (`src/views/candidates/RecipeCard.tsx`, `src/views/DetailView.tsx`); a hairline `edge` on panels in both schemes and a dark shadow in dark mode; `notice-warning` takes its left edge from the new `warning-edge`. `peach` stays as a decoration fill of icons and illustrations.
- Warning colors changed from the proposal after checking the app: the proposed dark `warning-surface` `#3A2A10` was 0.026 apart from `butter` in OKLab, and the allergen notice looked like the energy tile; it became `#4B1D1D` (at least 0.078 from every tile). The proposed light `#FFF4E0` was 0.033 from `surface-muted` (the salt equivalent tile); it became `#FFDCC6`, and the light `warning` became `#8A4700` (5.5:1 on it) with `warning-edge` `#B35F00`. `warning` no longer equals `near`.
- Small texts on tiles (range, target, "no target", missing foods, the tile label) use `text` at 13 px instead of `muted` at 12 px.
- Stepper row (`Stepper` in `src/views/DetailView.tsx`): a two-column grid, the label in a column that wraps, and the decrease button, the value, and the increase button in one group that does not wrap.
- Tests: `test/node/styles.test.ts` adds `lilac` to the pastels, `muted` and `warning-edge` on `warning-surface`, `warning-edge` on `surface`, and a check that `warning-surface` is at least 0.04 apart in OKLab from every nutrient fill (`mint`, `butter`, `sky`, `lilac`, `surface-muted`); the check fails on the release 14 tokens and on the proposed ones. `e2e/flow.spec.ts` adds a test per locale that loads the detail view at 320 and 375 px and compares the vertical position of each decrease and increase button; it fails on the release 14 stepper in four locales and passes after the fix in the three projects.
- Checks in the browser (local production build, Playwright, 2026-11-08): the targets view, the candidates, a card, and the detail view in `ja` at 375 px and 1280 px and in `fr` at 375 px and 320 px, in light and dark mode, before and after; screenshots in the session scratch directory. Not checked by hand in this stage: forced colors and print (covered by the E2E suite as before), and real devices.
- Commands (2026-11-08): `npm run check` passes (64 test files with 1,043 tests, 20 more from the contrast test; content validation; production build); `npm run test:e2e` 206 passed and 1 skipped by design (194 before, plus the 12 stepper tests), including axe-core with color contrast in four locales and both color schemes.
- Sizes: CSS 23.43 → 23.64 kB (gzip 5.69 → 5.81 kB); startup chunk `index-*.js` 457.36 → 457.42 kB (gzip 139.52 → 139.54 kB). No calculation, ranking, content, snapshot, or text change.

### Stage 18.3 nutrition bars, "new to you", loading, translucent bars, and the two-column detail (2026-11-08)
- Nutrition bars (ADR-041 D4): `src/views/nutritionBar.ts` computes the geometry from the displayed numbers only (`displayedValue` in `src/format/format.ts`, the shown range rounded for display, and the user's targets through `targetBounds` in `src/views/candidates/display.ts`); the scale runs from zero to 1.25 times the largest of them. `NutrientBar` draws it, and `NutrientLine` puts the label and the value on one line with the bar and the notes below, using `subgrid` (Baseline widely available) so that the value stays inside the `dd`. No bar without a target or for an unknown value. The bar is `aria-hidden` and hidden in forced colors and print. Candidate cards and the detail view use it instead of the tiles; every text of the tiles stays (value, status and "just below/above", target or "no target", range, unknown with the foods that lack data).
- "New to you" (ADR-041 D4): `seen` in `src/state/reducer.ts` collects the recipes of every set that leaves the screen (a new search, "show a different type", "exclude this one"); a candidate not in it gets the mark once `seen` is not empty, so the first set of a page has none. Memory only; a new search keeps it, a reload clears it. New key `card.newToYou` in four catalogs: 「はじめて表示」, "New to you", "Nueva para ti", "Nouvelle pour vous" (es and fr follow the catalogs' `tú` and `vous`; no native review, open questions 9 and 23).
- Loading: the targets illustration and the loading text in a panel, then a card skeleton hidden from assistive technology (`Loading` in `src/App.tsx`). No motion yet (Stage 18.4).
- Translucent bars: the `glass` utility (85 % of the page color, `blur(14px) saturate(1.4)`, opaque without `backdrop-filter` support and `Canvas` in forced colors) on the header and on the bottom bars of the candidates and targets views. The built CSS keeps both `-webkit-backdrop-filter` and `backdrop-filter`, which closes the 要確認 of "Stage 18.1 decisions". Tailwind adds an opaque fallback `#FBF6EED9` for browsers without `color-mix()`, which would also show in dark mode; `color-mix()` is Baseline widely available, so this is accepted. The header sticks from `md` only, because it wraps to two rows on phones in `fr`; `scroll-padding-top: 5rem` from `md` keeps focused elements below it.
- Two-column detail: from `lg`, the nutrition section sits in a right column of up to 22 rem and sticks below the header when the window is at least 48 rem high; everything else stays in the left column in the DOM order of the mobile layout (the nutrition spans 5–7 rows, one per left item).
- Changed from ADR-041 D4 (accepted by the owner on 2026-11-08 after this stage; ADR-041 D4 revised): only the nutrition sticks, not the adjustment. A sticky column with both would either scroll inside itself, moving the values out of view while adjusting, or hide a focused stepper under the sticky nutrition when moving backwards (WCAG 2.4.11). With the nutrition alone on the right, the steppers on the left and the values stay side by side. The ingredient table in the left column is narrower and scrolls inside its region at 1024–1280 px.
- Tests: `src/views/nutritionBar.test.ts` (geometry: range, minimum-only, maximum-only, a value above the target, "just below" left of the band, zero); `test/node/ui-state.test.ts` ("new to you": none in the first set, only the replacement after an exclusion, kept across a new search, unchanged when a re-suggestion keeps the set); `test/ui/candidates.test.tsx` (no mark in the first set; only the replacement card after an exclusion); `test/ui/detail.test.tsx` (five nutrient lines and no bar without a search; three bars after a search with the cut preset).
- Checks in the browser (local production build, Playwright, 2026-11-08): cards (including the mark after an exclusion) and the detail after a search in `ja` at 375 px and in `fr` at 320 px, light and dark; the desktop detail at 1280 px, light and dark, at the top and scrolled to the ingredients; the loading state at 375 px, light and dark; forced colors (card and detail nutrition: bars hidden, every text kept) and print to PDF (one column, bars hidden). Screenshots in the session scratch directory. Not checked: real devices, Safari's rendering of `backdrop-filter` by eye (WebKit E2E runs it), and reduced motion (no new motion yet).
- Commands (2026-11-08): `npm run check` passes (65 test files with 1,050 tests); `npm run test:e2e` 206 passed and 1 skipped by design, including axe-core with color contrast in four locales and both color schemes.
- Phone-class timing (`npx playwright test --project=timing`, 2026-11-08): release 14 (`cf4ef7e`, extracted with `git archive` into the scratch directory and run with the same `node_modules`) and this stage, alternating in two rounds (release 14 first). Median / p90 at 4×, ms, rounds 1 and 2: search "cut" 69.5 / 75.9 and 70.4 / 72.4 → 73.5 / 77.2 and 71.1 / 76.7; "bulk" 65.3 / 69.8 and 64.9 / 71.7 → 71.5 / 95.4 and 69.5 / 76.1; re-suggestion 24.0 / 26.4 and 25.6 / 29.1 → 27.5 / 32.6 and 27.4 / 30.8; recalculation 9.1 / 10.0 and 9.1 / 10.0 → 9.3 / 11.2 and 9.7 / 11.4; startup to the view heading in `ja` 228.4 and 230.2 → 242.6 and 231.6. The bars and lines add about 2–6 ms to a search at 4×; the ADR-030 D10 threshold (about 100 ms at 4×) holds. The one p90 of 95.4 ms ("bulk", round 1) is a single slow sample (max 102.6); round 2 gives 76.1.
- Sizes: CSS 23.64 → 26.89 kB (gzip 5.81 → 6.39 kB); startup chunk `index-*.js` 457.42 → 460.54 kB (gzip 139.54 → 140.61 kB), 39.5 kB below Vite's 500 kB warning; catalog chunks grow by one string each. No calculation, ranking, content, snapshot, or existing text change.

### Stage 18.4 motion and finish (2026-11-08)
- Motion (ADR-041 D5), CSS only (`@keyframes` and `--animate-*` in the `@theme` of `src/styles.css`), applied through Tailwind's `motion-safe:` variant, so `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` never starts them and every element is in its final state at once (an animation delay under the global 0.01 ms rule would still have hidden cards for a moment):
  - Cards deal in when they enter the candidates (`deal-in`, 280 ms, 60 ms apart by position). Kept cards do not move after "exclude this one", because their `key` stays. The card fade of ADR-037 D5 had never been built; this replaces it.
  - A value whose displayed number changed with the last adjustment gets a fading ring in `sun` (`flash`, 1.2 s; `NutrientLine` restarts it with a new `key`). It is a `box-shadow`, so the text keeps its contrast, and forced colors drop it.
  - A status that the last adjustment turned into "met" pops once (`pop`, 420 ms) in the detail view; the change summary already names the status change (ADR-030 D8).
  - No count-up (owner, 2026-11-08). No new text, no new dependency.
- E2E: `e2e/motion.spec.ts` checks the deal-in and the highlight, that nothing animates and every card is opaque with reduced motion, and in forced colors that the bars are hidden while the nutrient texts stay and the header has no `backdrop-filter`. `expectNoAxeViolations` in `e2e/support.ts` now waits for running animations to finish: axe measured the contrast of cards that were still fading in and reported it in 16 runs. `test/ui/detail.test.tsx` checks that exactly the nutrients whose displayed value changed get the highlight, and none after a change of servings.
- Checks in the browser (local production build, Playwright, 2026-11-08): the deal-in at 140 ms and the highlight 120 ms after an adjustment, at 375 px in `ja`, light and dark. Print and forced colors were checked in Stage 18.3 and the layout has not changed since; the new motion does not apply to either. Not checked: the pop by eye (it needs a status change by one step), real devices.
- Commands (2026-11-08): `npm run check` passes (65 test files with 1,050 tests); `npm run test:e2e` 215 passed and 1 skipped by design.
- Phone-class timing (`npx playwright test --project=timing`, two rounds, compared with the release 14 runs of "Stage 18.3" in the same session): median / p90 at 4×, ms: search "cut" 69.6 / 77.9 and 70.3 / 75.4 (release 14: 69.5 / 75.9 and 70.4 / 72.4); "bulk" 68.5 / 75.8 and 67.4 / 75.5; recalculation 9.0 / 10.3 and 8.8 / 9.6; startup to the view heading in `ja` 225.9 and 227.2. The ADR-030 D10 threshold holds.
- Sizes: CSS 26.89 → 27.62 kB (gzip 6.39 → 6.63 kB); startup chunk `index-*.js` 460.54 → 461.00 kB (gzip 140.61 → 140.80 kB).

### Releases by fast-forward (2026-11-08)
- Owner decision (2026-11-08): `main` and `dev` point to the same commit after each release. The owner fast-forwards `main` to `dev` (`git merge --ff-only dev`) and pushes; no merge commit and no GitHub pull request merge, because every GitHub merge method creates new commits (ADR-035, revised). Alternative not chosen: keep the merge commits and fast-forward `dev` to `main` after each release (release markers stay, but every release needs one more push of `dev` and one more CI run).
- One-time alignment: `main` had ten merge commits that `dev` lacked (`42f7d77`, the merge of pull request #1, to `6e2ce04`), each with the same files as its `dev` parent (checked with `git diff` per commit). One merge of `main` into `dev` brings them in; `git merge-tree` gives the same tree as `dev` (`b93e4e8`), so no file changes. After that, `main` can fast-forward to `dev`.
- Updated: [DEPLOYMENT.md](../en/DEPLOYMENT.md) §3 "Release to production" with its Japanese twin, and `CLAUDE.md` "Deployment". Rollback stays a revert on `dev` followed by a fast-forward of `main`, or the dashboard rollback.
- Effect on the `main` ruleset: `main` now receives the commit that CI checked on `dev`, so **Require status checks to pass** could be added (owner step, optional; the earlier 要確認 in [PUBLIC_RELEASE.md](PUBLIC_RELEASE.md) applied to local merge commits). Fast-forward pushes are allowed by **Block force pushes**.
- Done (2026-11-08): the docs commit `6bdb73d` and the alignment merge `ef03259` (same tree as `6bdb73d`). Before that, a local merge commit `a383208` made on `main` with the old procedure was dropped by the owner (`git branch -f main origin/main`) without being pushed. The owner pushed `dev`, fast-forwarded `main` to `ef03259`, and pushed; `main`, `dev`, `origin/main`, and `origin/dev` all point to `ef03259`.

### Cleanup after release 16 (2026-11-08)
- The E2E guard in `e2e/support.ts` no longer tolerates WebKit's `style-src-attr` report for `<select>` ("Stage 5.4"): WebKit 27.2, which Playwright 1.65 brings since "ADR-016 dependency update after release 15", does not send it. `npm run test:e2e` passes with every CSP report rejected (227 passed, 1 skipped by design; all WebKit tests included). If an older WebKit is used again, the report returns and the guard fails, which is the intended signal.
- [RELEASE_READINESS.md](RELEASE_READINESS.md) §3 marks the limitation resolved; [PUBLIC_RELEASE.md](PUBLIC_RELEASE.md) notes that the reason for leaving out **Require status checks to pass** on `main` no longer applies since "Releases by fast-forward" (an optional owner step).

### Phase 18 legend fix (2026-11-08)
- Cause of the finding in "Release 15 and fifteenth production deployment": the targets view's panel fieldsets were flex containers with a `float-left` legend. A float has no effect on a flex item, and WebKit then draws the legend in the fieldset's border, so it crossed the panel's top edge; Chromium keeps it inside. Release 14 had the same layout, hidden by its transparent border. The saved-conditions fieldset had the same pattern on its `butter` fill.
- Fix: the panel variant of `Fieldset` in `src/views/TargetsView.tsx` and the fieldset of `src/views/targets/SaveConditions.tsx` are plain blocks; the legend floats inside them, and the hint and controls move into an inner `clear-left flex flex-col gap-2` element. The non-panel fieldsets keep their bordered legend. No text, order, or control changes.
- Test: `e2e/flow.spec.ts` checks per locale that no legend of a targets panel starts above its fieldset; it fails in WebKit in four locales on the release 15 code and passes in the three projects after the fix.
- Checks (2026-11-08): WebKit at 375 px (`ja`) and 320 px (`fr`), light and dark, and Chromium in forced colors at 375 px: both legends inside their panels. `npm run check` passes (65 test files with 1,050 tests); `npm run test:e2e` 227 passed and 1 skipped by design. Startup chunk 461.24 kB (gzip 140.84 kB); CSS 27.65 kB (gzip 6.63 kB). Reaches production with the next merge of `dev` into `main`.

### ADR-016 dependency update after release 15 (2026-11-08)
- Re-check (npm registry, 2026-11-08 03:43 UTC; the ADR-016 burst window starts 2026-11-06 03:43 UTC). `npm outdated` listed, besides the recorded exceptions: `@playwright/test` 1.65.0 (2026-11-05), `@tailwindcss/vite` and `tailwindcss` 4.3.5 (2026-11-06 15:16; 4.3.4 on 2026-11-02, so one release in the window), `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.2.1 (6.2.0 and 6.2.1 on 2026-11-02, a burst that ended six days earlier), `globals` 17.14.0 (2026-11-01), `i18next` 26.7.2 (2026-11-05 14:23) with 27.0.1 latest, `jsdom` 31.0.0 (2026-11-05), `react-i18next` 17.1.13 (2026-11-05 14:21) with 18.0.0 latest, `vite` 8.3.6 (2026-11-05), `vitest` 5.1.0 (2026-11-02).
- Owner decision (2026-11-08, as recommended): the minor and patch updates as one commit, `jsdom` 31 as its own commit, and `i18next` 27 with `react-i18next` 18 deferred under ADR-016 exception 2: `i18next` 27.0.0 (2026-11-07 09:06 UTC) and 27.0.1 (14:25 UTC) form a burst, and `react-i18next` 18.0.0 (09:11 UTC) requires `i18next >= 27.0.0`, so the two move together. Re-check after 2026-11-09 14:25 UTC and plan the migration as its own stage. Points the migration must check (changelogs of both packages): the `strictInterpolationChecks` type default becomes `true`, `returnEmptyString` defaults to `false`, the logger and post-processors belong to each instance, and `engines.node >=22`. The app already sets `fallbackLng: false`, uses `createInstance()`, and does not use `Trans`.
- Changes read: `i18next` 26.7 adds the APIs v27 builds on (`hydrate()`, `isSafeLanguageCode()`, `callbackApi`) and debug-mode deprecation warnings, and fixes `reloadResources()`; `react-i18next` 17.1.11–17.1.13 fix `Trans` and types (17.1.13 declares the `react` init options for i18next 27). `vite` 8.3.6 fixes the module runner, the optimizer, and SSR; `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.2 adds `refreshInclude` and `refreshExclude`; Tailwind 4.3.4–4.3.5 are fixes (parsing, canonicalization, `@scope`); `vitest` 5.1 adds features such as `--last-failed`. `@playwright/test` 1.65 lists breaking changes (class fields follow `useDefineForClassFields`, actions wait for elements to be not inert, RFC 6265 cookie matching, unrendered text left out of accessible names); the suite passes unchanged. Peer ranges are met (`@tailwindcss/vite` and `@vitejs/plugin-react` accept `vite` 8, `vitest` accepts `vite` 8 and Node 24, `react-i18next` 17.1.13 accepts `i18next >= 26.2.0`).
- Lockfile: the listed packages plus `@oxc-project/types` 0.156.0, `@vitest/mocker` and `@vitest/spy` 5.1.0, `enhanced-resolve` 5.26.1, `magic-string` 1.4.5, `playwright` and `playwright-core` 1.65.0, `postcss` 8.5.32, `rolldown` 1.2.16 with its optional bindings, and the optional Tailwind platform packages; `@tailwindcss/node` no longer nests its own `lightningcss` and `magic-string`. `npm install` under `strict-allow-scripts` reported no install script (ADR-018).
- Playwright browsers (downloaded with the owner's approval to the user cache outside the repository): Chromium headless shell 158.0.8127.18 (219 MB unpacked) and WebKit 27.2 revision 2388 (319 MB unpacked); `playwright install` removed the unused 1.64 browsers.
- Checks (2026-11-08, on `dev` with the uncommitted legend fix of "Phase 18 legend fix" in the same tree): `npm run check` passes (65 test files with 1,050 tests, licenses, content validation, production build); `npm run test:e2e` 227 passed and 1 skipped by design; `npm audit --omit=dev`: 0; `npm audit`: the accepted `minimatch` findings only (10 moderate, open question 22; unchanged: `eslint` 10.14.0 and `typescript-eslint` 8.72.1 still use `minimatch` 10.2.6, latest 11.0.5). ADR-017 re-checked: unchanged (`typescript-eslint` 8.72.1 declares peer `typescript >=4.8.4 <6.1.0`).
- Bundle: the startup chunk grows from 461,003 to 467,091 bytes on release 15's code (`2bff7f7` extracted with `git archive` and built with each lockfile), 467,330 bytes with the legend fix (gzip 142.72 kB), 32.7 kB below the 500 kB limit (ADR-039). The growth is `i18next` 26.7 (its ESM file grows from 84,647 to 92,886 bytes before minification); locale chunks, the calculation bundle, and the text bundles are unchanged.
- Phone-class timing (`npm run test:e2e:timing`, release 15's code with the old and the new lockfile, both with Playwright 1.65, alternating in three rounds; 1-minute load average 4.5–6.3; median / p90 at 4×, ms, old → new): search "cut" 71.2 / 77.6, 70.6 / 78.2, 77.5 / 81.4 → 70.5 / 81.2, 77.7 / 90.5, 72.7 / 77.0; "bulk" 68.1 / 75.9, 68.7 / 76.7, 71.3 / 79.0 → 67.8 / 76.2, 70.3 / 84.4, 67.5 / 78.9; recalculation 9.2–9.4 → 9.0–9.7; startup to the view heading in `ja` 230.2, 228.5, 246.7 → 229.9, 237.3, 229.9. Within the variation of the machine (the new lockfile's second round was slower in every scenario, also at 1×); the ADR-030 D10 threshold holds.
- `jsdom` 30.1.3 → 31.0.0 (own commit; development only, used by the UI tests through Vitest). Breaking changes read (release notes): a new CSS parsing, selector, and computed-style implementation, parsing that pauses at blocking scripts, asynchronous focus loss for elements that become non-focusable, page load events on the window only, and `@napi-rs/canvas` (an optional peer, not installed) instead of `canvas`. Node `^24.15.0` is met (24.19.0). Lockfile: `entities` 9.0.0, `parse5` 8.1.1, `undici` 8.12.4, and `bcp-47`, `bcp-47-match`, `parse5-parser-stream`, `is-alphabetical`, `is-alphanumerical`, `is-decimal` added (MIT); the `@asamuzakjp`, `@csstools`, `css-tree`, and related CSS packages removed. No install script. `npm run check` passes with the same 1,050 tests unchanged; the production build is byte-identical (`index-DTcRdxI5.js`); `npm audit` unchanged.
- `npm outdated` now lists only `typescript` (ADR-017), `@types/node` (ADR-018), and `i18next` 27 with `react-i18next` 18 (ADR-016 exception 2).

### Stage 19.1 decisions (2026-11-08)
The owner approved every recommendation of the proposal on 2026-11-08. Prototypes and screenshots stayed in the session scratch directory. Measurements used a local static server with the build's own CSP, Playwright 1.65, Chromium, and WebKit.
- **Theme switch**: ADR-042.
- **Comparison view (Stage 19.3)**: fixed column widths (table layout `fixed`; row headers 116 px on phones and 176 px from `lg`; recipes 152 px or more). Each nutrient cell shows the value, a bar, the status with the "just below" or "just above" note of the cards, and the range. The target or "no target" moves to the row header, once per row, because every recipe has the same target. Bars in a row share one scale: the largest number of the row. Measured on the prototype: page overflow 0 at 320, 375, and 1280 px; with two recipes 79 px scrolls inside the region at 375 px, with three 231 px; none at 1280 px; axe 0 violations in 32 runs (four locales, 375 and 1280 px, light and dark, two engines).
- **Finding in production (release 16)**: on WebKit in `ja`, the row headers of the comparison table shrink to 43 px and break after each character, so two recipes make the table 1,457 px high at 375 px. Fixed widths give 940 px. Stage 19.3 fixes it.
- **Desktop ingredient table (Stage 19.3)**: the table moves below the two columns at full width, and the steps, the safety notes, and the basis follow at full width. The table is 956 px wide and hides nothing from 1024 px, against 572 px with 68 px hidden today at every width from 1024 to 1440 px.
