Only the nutrition next to the adjustment and the shopping list stays sticky, the pairing that ADR-041 D4 wanted. Value cells do not wrap. Rejected: a right column of 17 rem (fits at 652 px, but cramped), a wider page (no help below 1280 px), and the source column inside the name cell (fits, but taller rows and misaligned totals).
- **Card height**: measured against release 14 (`cf4ef7e`) and the current build with three presets, four locales, device scale 1, light scheme, and reduced motion, as the mean natural height of the cards; the same recipes appear in every condition. ADR-041's aim did not hold: at 375 and 1280 px, every one of the 48 conditions is taller, by 2.3–13.4 %.

  | Engine, width | Release 14 | Current | Bar on the label row | Two columns |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Chromium 320 | 826 | 865 | 783 | 811 |
  | Chromium 375 | 708 | 774 | 692 | 694 |
  | Chromium 1280 | 766 | 827 | 745 | 752 |
  | WebKit 320 | 815 | 860 | 782 | 808 |
  | WebKit 375 | 703 | 768 | 690 | 684 |
  | WebKit 1280 | 765 | 815 | 737 | 748 |

  Decision: Stage 19.3 moves the bar onto the row of the label and the value on cards, with no text removed. Limits: the bar is as short as 44 px at 320 px and 76 px at 1280 px, so a narrow card may keep the bar on its own row (not measured). In Chromium, `ja` at 1280 px stays about 1 % above release 14 (686 against 679). Two columns were rejected because the `ja` label breaks over three lines. Release 14 was built with the current `node_modules`, as in "Stage 18.3".
- **Startup chunk (Stage 19.4)**: the chunk holds `react-dom` 207.0 kB, the app 114.2 kB, `i18next` 49.7 kB, `domain` 31.3 kB, `zod` 30.3 kB, `shared` 13.5 kB, and `react` 8.2 kB (attributed through the source map). `zod` validates the content at startup and the engines precompute right after the first view, so neither can load later. Rolldown moves shared code into chunks that the entry imports statically, so the size of `index-*.js` alone misleads. JavaScript of a first view, without the locale chunk (raw kB; gzip -9 kB; largest chunk raw kB):

  | Loaded later | Raw | Gzip | Largest |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Nothing (today) | 467.3 | 141.0 | 467.3 |
  | Comparison, shopping list, about | 455.7 | 141.4 | 339.0 |
  | The above and detail | 432.5 | 137.0 | 302.5 |
  | The above and candidates | 411.5 | 132.0 | 283.6 |
  | The above and free text | 401.3 | 128.7 | 282.5 |

  Decision: load the comparison, shopping list, about, detail, and candidates views later. Target: at most 420 kB of first-view JavaScript without the locale chunk, and no chunk above 300 kB. The main gain is room below the 500 kB warning; the transfer drops only about 6 %. Stage 19.4 preloads the candidates chunk after the first view, keeps the search and the shopping list when a chunk fails (a page reload would lose them), and reduces the split if the ADR-030 D10 threshold no longer holds. Timing with split chunks is not measured yet.
- **Order**: 19.2 theme switch; 19.3 comparison view, ingredient table, and cards; the `i18next` 27 stage if its re-check after 2026-11-09 14:25 UTC passes (not a blocker); 19.4 startup chunk; 19.5 release 17. The unpacked packages are close (`i18next` 27.0.1 590,562 bytes, 26.7.2 599,345 bytes); the effect on the bundle is not measured.
- **Left out**: the display typeface, theme sync between tabs, loading the free text later, and a hashed early script.
- **Sources checked (2026-11-08)**:
  - [Tailwind CSS dark mode](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/dark-mode) and [functions and directives](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/functions-and-directives): `@custom-variant` with a data attribute, and `@variant` inside a rule. These pages do not show a variant with several rules and `@slot`; the built CSS of Tailwind 4.3.5 confirms that it works.
  - [MDN `color-scheme`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color-scheme): Baseline widely available; it sets the canvas, scrollbars, and form controls.
  - [React `lazy`](https://react.dev/reference/react/lazy): `load` runs once and its promise is cached; a rejection goes to the nearest error boundary. That a rejection stays cached is an inference (要確認 in Stage 19.4).
  - [Vite building for production](https://vite.dev/guide/build): `vite:preloadError`, and assets of an earlier deployment may be gone.
  - [Cloudflare Workers static assets headers](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/headers/): the default `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` with an ETag.

### Stage 19.2 theme switch (2026-11-08)
- Implemented as ADR-042: `public/theme-init.js`, `src/theme/themePreference.ts`, `src/components/ThemeSwitcher.tsx`, the dark tokens under `@variant dark`, four catalogs, and the privacy text of the about page in four locales. `src/main.tsx` applies the stored choice again, so the page and the select agree when the early script failed to load.
- Both selects in the header are now 32 px high (`h-8`). In WebKit they were 23 px, below the 24 px of WCAG 2.5.8; WebKit ignores `min-height`, padding, and `line-height` on a select and honors only `height` (measured on both engines).
- Print: the dark tokens now apply on screen only, and print uses the light tokens under every choice. In release 16, a dark OS already gave light text in print media (`rgb(241, 243, 245)`, read with print emulation); a stored dark choice would have spread this to more users. Decided in this stage because the prompt requires readable print; it goes beyond the approved text of ADR-042.
- ESLint: `public/**/*.js` is linted as a classic script with browser globals.
- Reduced motion: the rule in `src/styles.css` gives every property a 0.01 ms transition, so a computed color read right after a theme change is the old one. `e2e/theme.spec.ts` reads colors through retrying assertions.
- Sizes (raw kB, gzip kB, against release 16's code): startup chunk 467.33, 142.72 → 468.27, 142.98, 31.7 kB below the 500 kB warning; CSS 27.70, 6.64 → 28.80, 6.72; locale chunks `ja` 30.58 → 30.73, `en` 26.58 → 26.69, `es` 28.98 → 29.08, `fr` 30.27 → 30.39; new `theme-init.js` 496 bytes.
- Phone-class timing (`npm run test:e2e:timing`, release 16's code against this stage, alternating in two rounds, 1-minute load average 5.4–8.0; median / p90 at 4×, ms, release 16 → this stage): search "cut" 90.6 / 100.3 and 85.3 / 93.7 → 93.0 / 96.7 and 83.2 / 95.4; "bulk" 88.1 / 97.3 and 84.7 / 89.8 → 88.8 / 96.3 and 83.4 / 94.8; recalculation 11.2 / 13.5 and 11.9 / 13.3 → 11.9 / 12.9 and 12.1 / 13.4; startup to the view heading in `ja` 285.8 and 274.6 → 292.1 and 284.1, in `fr` 302.5 and 279.2 → 302.8 and 284.8. The search stays within the variation of the machine and the ADR-030 D10 threshold of about 100 ms holds, though the load was higher than in earlier stages. Startup grows by up to about 10 ms at 4× on a local server. Measured before the print change, which only wraps rules in media queries.
- Checks (2026-11-08): `npm run check` passes (67 test files, 1,074 tests, licenses, content validation, production build); `npm run test:e2e` 320 passed and 1 skipped by design (321 tests, in the three projects); `npm audit --omit=dev`: 0.
- Browser check on the production build: the targets view at 320, 375, and 1280 px, in `ja` and `fr`, with system, light, and dark, in Chromium and WebKit: selects 32 px high, no page overflow, no page error. Header height 141 px at 320 px, 97 px at 375 px, and 57 px at 1280 px in both engines. `e2e/theme.spec.ts` checks axe on the targets, candidates, and detail views in four locales with system on a dark OS, light, and dark; the header at 768 px stays within the 80 px of `scroll-padding-top`.
- Forced colors (Chromium emulation): every choice follows the forced palette, which comes from the OS setting. WebKit in Playwright does not apply forced colors, so it is not checked there.
- Not checked: real devices, the cost of the revalidation of `theme-init.js` on Cloudflare (Stage 19.5 checks the header), paper print, and es and fr wording by a native reviewer (open questions 9 and 23).

### Stage 19.3 comparison view, ingredient table, and cards (2026-11-08)
- Comparison view: as approved in "Stage 19.1 decisions". `nutrientBar` takes the largest shown number of the row (`shownLargest`), so the geometry still uses only displayed numbers. The "just below" and "just above" notes and their one-decimal values are new in this view and come from the same functions as on the cards. "No target" moves from each cell to the row header. Long words break inside their cells (`wrap-anywhere`), because a fixed table does not widen for them.
- Ingredient table: as approved. Value cells do not wrap (`whitespace-nowrap`). A phone still scrolls inside the region: 333 px at 375 px, as before, and 388 px at 320 px.
- Cards: the `dl` sets the columns and each line takes them through `subgrid`, so the bars of a card line up. A container query keeps the bar on its own row when the inside of the card is narrower than 17 rem (272 px).
- Pitfall: Tailwind's `row-span-*` sets the `grid-row` shorthand, so a variant `@min-[17rem]:row-span-2` also resets `row-start-1`, and the value and the bar fell to a second row. `e2e/layout.spec.ts` caught it; the variant now sets `row-start-1` too.
- Card height on the production build (mean natural height, px; three presets, four locales; the same recipes in all 36 conditions per engine):

  | Engine, width | Release 14 | Release 16 | This stage | Conditions taller than release 14 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Chromium 320 | 826 | 865 | 833 | 9 of 12 |
  | Chromium 375 | 708 | 774 | 692 | 1 of 12 |
  | Chromium 1280 | 766 | 827 | 745 | 3 of 12 |
  | WebKit 320 | 815 | 860 | 832 | 9 of 12 |
  | WebKit 375 | 703 | 768 | 690 | 1 of 12 |
  | WebKit 1280 | 765 | 815 | 737 | 0 of 12 |

  At 375 and 1280 px, the cards are shorter than in release 14. At 320 px, which keeps the bar on its own row (254 px wide instead of 44 px), they stay about 1–2 % taller than in release 14 and 3–4 % shorter than in release 16. The smallest bar is 99 px at 375 px and 76 px at 1280 px.
- Sizes (raw kB, gzip kB, against Stage 19.2): startup chunk 468.27, 142.98 → 470.34, 143.48, 29.7 kB below the 500 kB warning; CSS 28.80, 6.72 → 29.80, 6.92; locale chunks unchanged. Stage 19.4 wins this back.
- Phone-class timing (`npm run test:e2e:timing`, release 16's code against this stage, alternating in two rounds, 1-minute load average 4.9–7.8; median / p90 at 4×, ms, release 16 → this stage): search "cut" 94.6 / 104.6 and 77.5 / 87.9 → 81.6 / 86.9 and 75.6 / 87.8; "bulk" 92.7 / 99.4 and 73.5 / 83.6 → 76.4 / 85.3 and 77.2 / 86.5; recalculation 11.2 / 12.2 and 10.3 / 11.3 → 10.5 / 12.0 and 10.8 / 12.0; startup to the view heading in `ja` 324.7 and 256.2 → 259.7 and 257.2. Release 16's first round ran under the highest load, so the second round is the fairer comparison; the stage stays within the variation of the machine, and the ADR-030 D10 threshold holds.
- Checks (2026-11-08): `npm run check` passes (67 test files, 1,076 tests, licenses, content validation, production build); `npm run test:e2e` 419 passed and 1 skipped by design (420 tests, in the three projects). `test/ui/candidates.test.tsx` now expects the target in the row headers of the comparison.
- Browser check on the production build: cards, detail, and a comparison of three recipes at 320, 375, and 1280 px, in `ja` and `fr`, in light and dark, in Chromium and WebKit: no page overflow and no page error; the ingredient table hides nothing at 1280 px, and `e2e/layout.spec.ts` checks 1024, 1152, and 1280 px in four locales.
- Print and forced colors (Chromium, with the dark choice): the bars are hidden in both; the comparison prints with the light tokens and nothing hidden at 1280 px emulation width (PDF read).
- Not checked: real devices; a "just below" note and an unknown value in the comparison view on screen (they use the functions and markers of the cards, but no test reaches them in this view); paper print; and WebKit in forced colors.

---

## ADR-001: Static site on Cloudflare with low platform lock-in
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: the MVP is a static SPA served from Cloudflare static assets. Platform-specific configuration lives only in `platform/cloudflare/` and generated header files; domain, content, and UI code use no Cloudflare APIs. The build output is a plain static `dist/` that any static host can serve. A Vite plugin specific to Workers is not used while no Worker exists.
- Rationale: the owner intends to use Cloudflare but will switch providers if needed.
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, Phase 12 Stage 12.1, ADR-034): one thin Worker may serve the optional AI endpoint at a same-origin path under `/api/`, next to the static assets. Its Cloudflare-specific code stays in `platform/cloudflare/`, the HTTP contract is in `shared/`, and the provider code is a thin adapter. The static `dist/` still works on any static host; without the endpoint the AI feature is hidden. Not built yet.
- Versions and the exact assets configuration are verified in Phase 0.

## ADR-002: Repository-managed content, no database or admin UI in the MVP
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: recipes, foods, translations, and allergen links are files in the repository, validated at build time and shipped as static JSON. No D1, no admin UI, no authentication.
- Trade-off: content changes require a build and deployment; in exchange, diffs are reviewable and there is no admin attack surface.

## ADR-003: Curated recipes with deterministic adjustment and substitution
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: original structured recipes; bounded amount adjustment and predefined substitutions are deterministic. AI recipe generation is out of scope.
- Open: how much automatic "fit to targets" adjustment is in the MVP (Phase 0).

## ADR-004: No AI at runtime in the MVP
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: no AI calls. Reason texts come from deterministic data and message templates; ingredient input uses the dictionary with aliases.
- Later candidates: free-text input structuring and explanation text, each requiring evaluation in three locales (fixed evaluation sets, ID precision/recall, cross-locale consistency, human review), a server endpoint, and approval.
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, Phase 12 Stage 12.1): free-text input structuring is accepted as an optional feature (ADR-034); explanation text stays a later candidate. Evaluation covers every supported locale (four since ADR-033). Every core feature still works without AI.
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, Stage 12.3): the measured Workers AI models missed the ADR-034 D4 thresholds, so free-text input structuring is built as a deterministic parser without AI (ADR-034, revised again). The app again makes no AI calls.

## ADR-005: Japanese, English, and Spanish from the MVP
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: `ja`, `en`, `es` for UI and all content, including input, errors, and accessibility. Published content must be complete in all locales (build check). The owner's typed-catalog i18n approach (i18next) is the candidate library, confirmed in Phase 0. Default locale and detection: *Proposed* as in [ARCHITECTURE.md](../en/ARCHITECTURE.md) §4.
- Rationale: multilingual support is part of the owner's identity across web apps.

## ADR-006: Food identity separated from translation
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: ingredients reference locale-neutral food IDs mapped to food codes and data versions. Names and aliases are display and search data. Calculation functions take no locale; a locale invariance test is part of the acceptance criteria.

## ADR-007: Translations as canonical repository content
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: Claude drafts `en`/`es` at authoring time; the owner reviews diffs and reports issues; translations are committed as canonical content. A glossary keeps terms consistent; safety-relevant texts are back-translated during review.
- Risk: Spanish errors may go unnoticed if review capacity is limited.

## ADR-008: Best-effort allergen exclusion
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: allergen links with `contains` / `may_contain` / `unknown`; exclusion removes all three; no guarantee wording anywhere; disclaimer always visible. Details: [CONTENT_POLICY.md](../en/CONTENT_POLICY.md) §2.

## ADR-009: Discovery-first ranking; required ingredients as an optional hard filter
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: the main value is discovering dishes the user would not think of. Pantry input is not required. Users may optionally specify required ingredients, which act as a hard filter. Candidate sets are diversified deterministically (main protein, cuisine, cooking method); "show a different type" gives a reproducible next set.
- Viewed recipes are remembered only in memory for the session; nothing is stored in the browser (ADR-030 D9).

## ADR-010: Fried foods allowed as labelled estimates
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: fried recipes are included. Uncertainty is classified in advance from data availability (cooked fried form exists or not), shown with a note and, where needed, a range. Ranges that straddle a target give "near / uncertain". Details: [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §6.

## ADR-011: Displayed nutrient columns
- Status: **Accepted** (owner accepted the Stage 0.5 recommendation, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: energy `ENERC_KCAL`; protein `PROT-` (たんぱく質); fat `FAT-` (脂質); carbohydrate `CHOCDF-` (炭水化物, includes dietary fiber). Dietary fiber is not displayed in the MVP. The detail view explains that energy is the table value and differs from 4P + 9F + 4C (median +4 %).
- Evidence: verification log "Stage 0.5 calculation checks". The alternative columns are incomplete for most recipes and would show protein several grams lower than common labels; `CHOAVLDF-` adds residual "carbohydrate" to meat, fish, and eggs (Stage 0.2 log).

## ADR-012: Source data handling
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: the original workbook is never committed; the extracted subset and manifest (URL, update date, SHA-256, script version) are committed. Details: [DATA_SOURCES.md](../en/DATA_SOURCES.md).

## ADR-013: Attribution
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: cite the source with MEXT's requested wording in the detail view and about page, with the data version and a statement that the values are this app's own calculations. No separate inquiry to MEXT is planned.

## ADR-014: Technology stack candidate
- Status: **Proposed** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Direction: the owner's established stack — TypeScript (strict), React, Vite, i18next with typed catalogs, Tailwind CSS, Zod, Vitest, Playwright, axe-core, ESLint, npm with lockfile and install-script allowlist. Not needed in the MVP: D1, Drizzle, Hono, jose, Cloudflare Access, Workers runtime. Versions and fit are verified in Phase 0.

## ADR-015: No accounts and no server-side user data
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: no accounts; targets and conditions stay in the browser and are not put in URLs. Browser storage of targets or history: none in the MVP (ADR-030 D9).
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, Phase 12 Stage 12.1, ADR-034): when the user asks for an AI suggestion, the free text they typed and the locale are sent to the same-origin endpoint and from there to the AI provider. Targets, form conditions, allergens, and history are never sent. The endpoint stores and logs no request body; the provider deletes API inputs and outputs within 30 days by default and does not train on them by default ("Stage 12.1 provider and platform check"). There are still no accounts and no server-side user data.
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, before Stage 12.3, ADR-034 revised): the provider is Workers AI, which does not retain inputs and outputs after the request and does not train on customer content, as long as no AI Gateway logging is used ("Stage 12.1 provider and platform check").
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, Stage 12.3, ADR-034 revised again): the free text is parsed in the browser and sent nowhere. The two revisions above no longer apply; no user data leaves the browser.
- Revised (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 13 Stage 13.1, ADR-036): when the user turns saving on, targets and conditions are stored in the browser's `localStorage` on that device. They are still never sent, put in a URL, or stored in a cookie, and there are still no accounts and no server-side user data.

## ADR-016: Dependency version policy
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: use the latest stable release of every dependency. Only two exceptions are allowed, each recorded here with its reason and the condition for moving to the latest version:
  1. **Compatibility constraint**: the latest version cannot work with another required tool (for example ADR-017).
  2. **Release burst**: several releases of the same package were published within a short time (about 48 hours) right before installation. Use the last version before the burst and re-check after a few days.
- The lockfile pins exact versions; `package.json` uses caret ranges except where an exception requires a narrower range.
- Current exceptions:
  - TypeScript 6.0.x instead of 7.x (exception 1, ADR-017).
  - `i18next` 26.x and `react-i18next` 17.x instead of 27 and 18 (exception 2, since 2026-11-08): `i18next` 27.0.0 and 27.0.1 were published within six hours on 2026-11-07, and `react-i18next` 18 requires `i18next` 27. The caret ranges `^26.7.2` and `^17.1.13` keep them on the current majors. Re-check after 2026-11-09 14:25 UTC ("ADR-016 dependency update after release 15").
  - Earlier: `i18next` 26.4.7 and `react-i18next` 17.0.20 were pinned exactly from Stage 0.1 until "Stage 9.1 ADR-016 re-check and dependency update".

## ADR-017: TypeScript 6.0 until type-aware linting supports TypeScript 7
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: `typescript ~6.0.3` for type-checking and linting.
- Rationale: TypeScript 7 does not provide the compiler API that `typescript-eslint` needs, and no `typescript-eslint` release supports it (verification log "Stage 0.1 toolchain"). Type-aware lint rules (`strictTypeChecked`) are part of the quality baseline.
- Alternatives: TypeScript 6 for linting plus TypeScript 7 for type-checking (two type checkers that can disagree, aliased package installation); TypeScript 7 without type-aware linting (loses safety rules). Both rejected for now.
- Migration trigger: when a stable `typescript-eslint` release supports TypeScript 7, upgrade in a dedicated commit. Check at the start of every phase.

## ADR-018: Install-script policy and Stage 0.1 toolchain
- Status: **Accepted** (owner approved the Stage 0.1 proposal, 2026-10-18)
- `.npmrc`: `engine-strict=true` and `strict-allow-scripts=true`. Any dependency with an install script fails `npm install`/`npm ci` until it is reviewed and listed in `package.json` `allowScripts`. Never use `--dangerously-allow-all-scripts` or approve all scripts at once.
- Measured on 2026-10-18 (npm 11.17.0, macOS): the only package with an install script is `fsevents@2.3.3` (optional dependency of `vite`, macOS only; implicit `node-gyp rebuild`). It ships a prebuilt `fsevents.node`, so the script is not needed; it is denied with `"allowScripts": { "fsevents": false }`. `npm audit` reported 0 vulnerabilities.
- Node: `engines` `>=24.15.0` (required by `jsdom` 30.1.3); `.nvmrc` pins 24. `@types/node` follows the runtime major version (24.x), not the latest types major.
- Dependencies are deliberately minimal for Phase 0: React, i18next/react-i18next, Zod; dev tooling TypeScript, ESLint (`strictTypeChecked`, React Hooks, React Refresh, import boundaries), Vite with `@vitejs/plugin-react`, Vitest with jsdom and Testing Library. Tailwind CSS, Playwright, axe-core, Wrangler, and a router are added in the phase that needs them, each re-checked against ADR-016.
- TypeScript: `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, `noImplicitOverride`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`, `noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`; split into `tsconfig.app.json` (browser: `src`, `shared`, `domain`) and `tsconfig.node.json` (configs, `platform`, `test/node`).
- Security headers are defined once in `platform/headers.ts`; the build writes `dist/_headers` (Cloudflare format) and `vite preview` serves the same headers. The dev server has no CSP because React Fast Refresh injects an inline script.
- Re-check at the start of Stage 0.2 (2026-10-18): no further `i18next`/`react-i18next` releases since the burst; less than a day has passed, so the pins stay (ADR-016).
- Verified locally (2026-10-18): lint, type-check, 17 tests, and build pass; the production build served by `vite preview` sends the CSP, logs no console errors, detects the browser language (`es-419` → `es`), switches locale and persists only the locale preference, and has no horizontal overflow at 375 px.

## ADR-019: Dependency-free workbook reader
- Status: **Accepted** (Stage 0.2, 2026-10-18; within the approved Phase 0 scope)
- Decision: `tools/xlsx/` implements just enough ZIP and SpreadsheetML parsing for the MEXT workbook (shared strings without phonetic runs, inline strings, values, sheet lookup by name). Unexpected structures fail with an error rather than being guessed.
- Rationale: one trusted input file read at build time does not justify a spreadsheet library and its supply-chain surface; the code is small and fully tested with synthetic fixtures.
- Alternatives: a spreadsheet package from npm (more features, more dependencies to review); converting the workbook to CSV by hand (not reproducible).
- Revisit if the workbook format changes in ways the reader cannot handle.

## ADR-020: Hand transcription of cooking tables with automated checks
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: transcribe only the needed rows of Tables 12–14 from rendered PDF pages into `data/composition/cooking.json`, cite the page of every entry, and verify with (1) a macOS-only text-layer check and (2) composition-based plausibility tests that run everywhere. Details: [DATA_SOURCES.md](../en/DATA_SOURCES.md) "Cooking tables".
- Alternatives: automated PDF table parsing (new dependency, error-prone on this layout); asking MEXT for machine-readable data (possible in parallel, outcome uncertain); ignoring weight change rates (protein 6–13 % too high for cooked meat).
- Limits: the text-layer check cannot detect a wrong number that also appears elsewhere on the page; the plausibility tests cover that for protein and fat but not for every field (for example coating masses).

## ADR-021: Provisional content format
- Status: **Accepted for Phase 0** (owner, 2026-10-18); refined in Phase 1.
- Decision: content is TypeScript (`content/foods.ts`, one file per recipe in `content/recipes/`, `content/glossary.ts`) typed with and validated by the `zod/mini` schemas in `shared/content.ts`. Recipes state raw edible grams (or grams as sold for `as_is` foods) and the composition state used; `cooking` records the real method when the tables lack it; `includedIn` lists ingredients already contained in another ingredient's cooked value (frying oil, coatings, discarded cooking water additions).
- Rationale: compile-time types plus runtime validation without a parser dependency; diffs are reviewable.
- Imports between repository modules use the `.ts` extension everywhere (`allowImportingTsExtensions` in `tsconfig.base.json`), so the same files work in Vite, Vitest, and Node type stripping.
- Alternatives: JSON (no comments, verbose), YAML (parser dependency).

## ADR-022: Count cooking oil fully when no oil-cooked value is used
- Status: **Accepted** (owner accepted the Stage 0.5 recommendation, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: keep [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §5 rule 4: oil that is not already contained in an oil-cooked value is counted in full and shown as an assumption.
- Rationale: using the Table 14 median retention (88 %) would lower fat by only 0.3–0.9 g per serving in the validation recipes; Table 14 has no meat rows; dressings and finishing oil are eaten entirely.

## ADR-023: Initial recipe library size and composition
- Status: **Accepted** (owner accepted the Stage 0.6 recommendation, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: about 60 recipes for the MVP, with composition rules: each goal (cut, maintain, bulk) has at least 15 recipes; each main protein group (chicken, pork, beef, fish, eggs, soy foods) appears in at least 8 recipes; at least 30 % quick (≤ 20 min); fried dishes at most 15 %; every recipe has at least one adjustable main protein or staple.
- Evidence: Stage 0.6 log. About 60 recipes give ≥ 3 met candidates for the general scenarios with a wide margin (estimated need 8–20), and the per-ingredient rule targets the required-ingredient bottleneck.
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-24, Phase 7 Stage 7.1): about 86 recipes. The composition rules stay (each goal at least 15 recipes, each main protein group at least 8, at least 30 % quick, fried at most 15 %, every recipe adjustable); new recipes keep their adjustment grid within about 400 states so that a full recommendation stays near the ADR-030 D10 threshold; no single cuisine exceeds half of the library. Note (Phase 9 Stage 9.3, 2026-10-25): the limit of about 400 states per new recipe stays; with it, a library of about 130 recipes stays within about 80 ms p90 at 4× (measured on a synthetic library whose 44 added recipes average 341 states, "Stage 9.3 browser timing"). Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, Phase 10 Stage 10.1): about 130 recipes. The composition rules and the limit of about 400 grid states per new recipe stay; Japanese dishes stay below about 35 % of the library; a full recommendation stays within about 80 ms p90 at 4×, measured after every batch ("Stage 10.1 expansion plan"). Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, before batch 3): the limit for the library is the ADR-030 D10 threshold of about 100 ms p90 at 4× instead of about 80 ms, measured again after batch 3; the expansion stops if it is exceeded ("Phase 10 speed decision before batch 3"). Revised (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 15 Stage 15.1): about 150 recipes. The composition rules, the limit of about 400 grid states per new recipe, Japanese dishes below about 35 %, and the library limit of about 100 ms p90 at 4× stay, measured after every batch ("Stage 15.1 decisions").

## ADR-024: Bounded adjustment in the MVP
- Status: **Accepted** (owner accepted the Stage 0.6 recommendation, 2026-10-18)
- Decision: include deterministic "fit to my targets" in the MVP, limited to the authored adjustable ingredients (main protein, and carbohydrates that are part of the dish or a side). Linked ingredients scale with their leader. The selection keeps the best status and the smallest relative change, as in the evaluation tool.
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-18, Stage 1.1): the "staple is either 0 g or at least a minimum portion" rule is replaced by **sides** (ADR-026). Things served with the dish (rice, boiled potatoes, a vegetable side) can be switched on and off; "fit to my targets" may switch off only sides marked for it (carbohydrate sides) and otherwise keeps a side's amount within its authored range, whose minimum is the smallest natural portion (rice: 100 g per serving). Vegetable sides are switched off only by the user. Carbohydrates that belong to the dish (rice in a rice bowl, pasta, toast) are adjustable within bounds but never removed.
- Evidence: Stage 0.6 log (adjustment roughly doubles the met recipes; unnatural results without portion rules).

## ADR-025: Fit tolerance, display rounding, ranges, and presets
- Status: **Accepted** (owner accepted the Stage 0.7 recommendation, 2026-10-18)
- Tolerance: ±10 % of a bound; protein up to 3 g below its minimum.
- Rounding: kcal to the nearest 10; protein, fat, and carbohydrate to whole grams; salt to 0.1 g. Fit status is computed from full-precision values; contradictions caused by rounding are explained in the detail view.
- Ranges: per-ingredient error bands by approximation reason, summed per nutrient; a range is shown when wider than twice the display unit. Bands and their evidence: [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §7 (two bands are measured, three are assumptions).
- Presets (per meal, labelled as examples): cut 450–650 kcal, protein ≥ 35 g, fat ≤ 15 g; maintain 550–750 kcal, ≥ 30 g, ≤ 20 g; bulk 750–950 kcal, ≥ 45 g, ≤ 30 g. With bounded adjustment, 7–11 of the 20 validation recipes meet each preset (Stage 0.6).
- Evidence: Stage 0.3, 0.6, and 0.7 logs.

## ADR-026: MVP content schema
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18, Stage 1.1)
- Decision (`shared/content.ts`):
  - **Adjustment**: `adjust` is `{ kind: "grams", min, max, step }` or `{ kind: "count", unitGrams, min, max }` for whole units (eggs, cans, slices); only `main_protein` and `main_carb` ingredients have it, and the default amount lies on the grid. `linkedTo` makes an ingredient scale in proportion to an adjustable leader (one level).
  - **Sides**: `sides` lists things served with the dish, each with a localized label, its ingredients, and `solverMayRemove`. Switching a side off sets its ingredients and their linked ingredients to 0 g. Steps that only prepare a side carry `side` and are hidden when it is off. Cooking time and equipment stay those of the full recipe (a cautious limitation).
  - **Hard to estimate**: `estimate: "slow_frying"` marks cooking that no table value represents (slow frying in a lot of oil); the range rules of [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §7 apply. It does not change the point value.
  - **Household measures**: content stores the unit and quantity (a multiple of 1/12); labels come from the UI catalogs. Units: piece, tbsp, tsp, clove, slice, pinch, knob, block, can, stalk. For `piece`, English and Spanish show the number alone.
  - **Steps and safety notes** have stable keys. Steps may carry notes shown from an amount upward (`atLeastGrams`, whole recipe after scaling).
  - **Substitutions** have keys and may replace step texts and safety notes, add safety notes, change minutes, and give their own bounds.
  - **Metadata**: `mainProteinGroup` (chicken, pork, beef, fish, egg, soy) must match the `proteinGroup` of one main-protein food; authored tags are `batch`, `no_cook`, `breakfast`. "Quick" (≤ 20 minutes) and "fried" are derived (`shared/recipe.ts`).
  - **ADR-023 adjustability**: every recipe has an adjustable ingredient or a side the solver may switch off.
  - **Versioning**: `content/version.ts` holds `CONTENT_VERSION` as `YYYY-MM-DD.n`, updated with every content change; bundle compatibility is a separate schema version (Stage 1.4).
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-24, Phase 7 Stage 7.1): `CUISINES` adds `latin_american`, `mediterranean`, and `southeast_asian`. A cuisine value names the region a dish comes from (taco rice is Okinawan, piperrada Spanish). Diversification and the cuisine preference (ADR-029 D5) use the values unchanged; more values only give the cuisine attribute more distinct values.
- Revised (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 15 Stage 15.1): `CUISINES` adds `french`, `north_african`, and `west_african` (ADR-038).
- Rationale: portion rules without unnatural amounts (Stage 0.6), traceable estimates for unrepresented cooking, and derived flags that cannot contradict the minutes or states.
- Alternatives: a "staple" with a removable minimum portion (replaced by sides at the owner's request); semver for content (no meaningful major/minor distinction for content).

## ADR-027: Content bundle format
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18, Stage 1.4)
- Decision: `tools/build-content.ts` writes `generated/content/calc.json` (locale-neutral) and `text-ja.json`, `text-en.json`, `text-es.json`; the files are generated at build time and not committed. `src/main.tsx` imports them with `?url`, so Vite emits them with a content hash in the name; `assetsInlineLimit` keeps JSON out of `data:` URLs, which `connect-src 'self'` would block.
  - Every bundle carries `schemaVersion` (`BUNDLE_SCHEMA_VERSION`, bumped on incompatible changes), the content version, and the composition versions (workbook update date and SHA-256, explanation PDF SHA-256).
  - The calculation bundle has foods (ID, codes per state, allergens, protein group), the composition values of `ENERC_KCAL`, `PROT-`, `FAT-`, `CHOCDF-`, `NACL_EQ` with notations and the refuse rate, weight change rates (removal after cooking as a flag only), and recipes without texts. It contains no Japanese, English, or Spanish text.
  - Text bundles key every text by food ID, recipe ID, and step, safety note, side, ingredient, and substitution keys.
  - The browser validates each bundle with the `zod/mini` schemas in `shared/bundle.ts`, checks that the text bundle matches the locale, versions, and keys of the calculation bundle (`bundleMismatches`), and shows the error state with a retry action otherwise.
- Rationale: plain static JSON that any host can serve (ADR-001); content hashes allow long caching; translations cannot affect the data the calculation uses.
- Alternatives: a custom Vite plugin that emits hashed files and serves them in development (more code); content as dynamically imported JavaScript modules (simpler, but executable code instead of JSON).

## ADR-028: Nutrition engine design, approximation reasons, and error bands
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18, Stage 2.1)
- API (`domain/nutrition`, no locale parameter):
  - `engineDataFromBundle(calc)` turns the calculation bundle into lookup maps (foods, composition of the five displayed nutrients, weight change rates, versions).
  - `resolveAmounts(recipe, state, data)` and `calculateRecipe(recipe, state, data)` take a recipe state: `servings`, `sidesOff` (side keys), `amounts` (grams for `grams` adjustment, a count for `count` adjustment, keyed by ingredient), and `substitutions` (substitution keys).
  - Invalid user states (amount outside the bounds or off the grid, a non-integer count, an unknown key) return a typed error result. Data inconsistencies (missing food code or weight change rate) throw, because build-time validation rules them out.
  - Output per ingredient: resolved grams, effective grams, food code, state, cooking method, weight change rate, counted or the reason it is not (`included_in`, `side_off`), class, reasons, notations, amounts, error band, assumptions. Per recipe: totals, per-serving values, per-nutrient ranges with a "shown" flag, incompleteness (ingredient keys with unmeasured values per nutrient), and the basis (content version, workbook date and SHA-256, explanation PDF SHA-256).
- Reasons, classes, and bands (resolves open question 15; [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §7; bands per nutrient revised in Stage 6.5):

  | Reason | Trigger | Class | Band (energy / protein / fat / carbohydrate / salt) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `raw_value_for_cooking` | `cooking` set, state `raw` | approximation | −25 / −15 / −35 / −30 / −15 % to +5 / +5 / +5 / +10 / +5 % |
  | `related_cooked_value` | `cooking` set, state is a cooking method | approximation | ±15 / ±10 / ±35 / ±10 / ±5 % |
  | `post_cooking_refuse` | weight change rate removes parts after cooking | approximation | ±5 % |
  | `fried` | fried state or fried cooking method | approximation | ±30 % of fat and of the energy from that fat |
  | `slow_frying` | `estimate: "slow_frying"` | hard to estimate | 0 to +50 % of fat and of the energy from that fat |
  | `estimated_source_value` | `( )` value in a displayed nutrient | approximation | none |

- Decisions:
  - **A. Substitutions** are applied during amount resolution in Phase 2 (food, state, cooking, ratio, bounds; reasons are re-evaluated for the new food). Offering and choosing substitutions stays in Phase 3 (`domain/recommendation`). Links keep their proportion because the leader's default scales with the ratio.
  - **B. Energy from fat** in the fried and slow-frying bands uses 9 kcal/g. This is an *assumption*, stated in the basis.
  - **C. Notations**: `( )` estimated values give the approximation reason `estimated_source_value` with no band. `Tr`, `(0)`, and `(Tr)` count as 0 and are flags only (class unchanged).
  - **D. Household measures** such as "a pinch" do not make an ingredient hard to estimate: the recipe states grams, and the measure is a display aid. The basis notes this.
  - **E. Tools and tests** obtain engine input from the in-memory calculation bundle (`buildCalcBundle` then `engineDataFromBundle`), so they test exactly what the browser uses. Hand cross-checks keep computing independently from `data/composition/`.
  - **F. Servings**: `amounts` and adjustment bounds refer to the authored servings; a servings change multiplies all resolved grams afterwards. Per-serving values therefore do not depend on servings. A scaled count may be fractional; the display is decided in Phase 4.
  - **G. Combination**: bands of several reasons on one ingredient add up, except that `slow_frying` replaces the `fried` band. The class is the most severe of the reasons (exact < approximation < hard to estimate), so an ingredient with a tabulated fried form is an approximation. Bands are summed per nutrient; whether a range is shown is decided on the per-serving range, with threshold twice the display unit (20 kcal; 2 g for protein, fat, and carbohydrate; 0.2 g for salt, ADR-025).
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-24, Phase 6 Stage 6.5): the bands of `raw_value_for_cooking` and `related_cooked_value` are set per nutrient at the extremes found in the evidence ("Stage 6.5 evidence for the error bands"): fat and energy of fatty meat and fish fall to 0.66 and 0.73 of the raw value, vegetables lose up to 30 % of their carbohydrate in boiling, and non-oil methods of the same food differ by up to 8 % in protein, 34 % in fat, and 14 % in energy. Previously −15 % to +5 % and ±5 % for every nutrient. Protein and salt keep their bands. `post_cooking_refuse`, `fried`, and `slow_frying` are unchanged and remain assumptions. Effect on the 60 recipes: a fat range is shown for 35 instead of 17 recipes; after adjustment, met recipes per preset go from 26 / 44 / 32 to 23 / 41 / 30. Option not chosen: a milder M (fat −25 %, energy −20 %; related fat ±20 %, energy ±10 %), which leaves the observed fat losses of about 30 % outside the band.
- Rationale: one pure engine shared by the browser and the tools; the two §7 bands for untabulated cooking can now apply separately; no new numbers except the stated energy factor.
- Alternatives: substitutions only in Phase 3 (the detail view could not recalculate them in Phase 2); trace and estimated values as approximations with an invented band; "a pinch" as hard to estimate (contradicts the authored grams); computing tools from the full source subset (would not test the browser's data).
- Evidence: verification log "Stage 1.3" and "Stage 2.1 engine design".

## ADR-029: Recommendation engine design
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-18, Stage 3.1)
- API (`domain/recommendation`, pure, deterministic, no locale parameter; nutrition values only from `calculateNutrition`):
  - `recommendationDataFromBundle(calc)`: engine data plus food allergens and protein groups, and the bundle recipes. No change to `domain/nutrition` is needed.
  - `evaluatePool(request, data)`: input validation, contradiction checks, hard filters, and the solver for targets, servings, and conditions. `selectCandidates(pool, session, seed)`: tiers, diversification, seeded order, close alternatives, and relaxation options. The split lets re-suggestion reuse the solver results.
  - Targets: kcal range (required), optional protein minimum, fat maximum, and carbohydrate range; `presetTargets` returns the ADR-025 presets. Conditions: hard `maxMinutes`, available `equipment`, `excludedFoods`, `excludedAllergens`, `requiredFoods` (each item an any-of set of food IDs); soft `effort` (preferred maximum) and `cuisines`. Session: `shown`, `excluded`, `previous` (the set on screen), `keep` (cards kept when one is replaced). Recipe `goals` are not used for ranking.
  - Input errors are typed values (`invalid_number`, `out_of_range`, `inverted_bound`, `unknown_food`, `required_and_excluded`, `unknown_recipe`, `invalid_servings`, `invalid_seed`). Provisional input limits: kcal 100–2,000, protein 0–200 g, fat 0–150 g, carbohydrate 0–400 g, minutes 5–240, servings an integer 1–20.
  - Output: candidates (met tier, up to 5) and close alternatives, each with the chosen recipe state, the engine result, per-nutrient and overall fit, the fit at default amounts, amount changes, sides switched off, states forced by excluded foods, relative change, diversity attributes, and reason data (typed codes and numbers; texts in Phase 4); contradictions; per-constraint exclusion counts (`count` and `onlyThis`); relaxation options; tier counts; an `exhausted` flag.
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Ranges**: when a range is shown, the status is evaluated on the range: entirely inside → met; touching or straddling a bound, or partly inside the tolerance zone → near; entirely outside the tolerance zone → not met. A range that is not shown is evaluated as its point value. Bounds and tolerance limits are inclusive; comparisons use full precision without an epsilon. Gaps are reported from the point value.
  - **D2. Unknown nutrient** (unmeasured counted value): per-nutrient status `unknown`; the overall tier treats it as not met, so it is never met and never described as close.
  - **D3. Overall status and tiers**: the worst status among the set targets (kcal always; protein, fat, carbohydrate when set; salt is never a target). Within the near tier, fewer near nutrients first, then the smaller sum of gap ÷ tolerance width; within the not-met tier, fewer not-met nutrients first, then the same gap sum.
  - **D4. Solver**: the search space of the evaluation tool (every grid combination × every subset of solver-removable sides), except that sides forced off by excluded foods stay off and sides containing a required food stay on. A linear model per side combination (one engine call per adjustable ingredient) scores the grid; the chosen state is recalculated with the engine and its fit is taken from that result; if it differs from the prediction, that recipe falls back to the exhaustive search. A test requires identical choices to the exhaustive search. Objective: tier, then the D3 order, then the smallest relative change Σ|g − g₀| / g₀ over changeable ingredients (as in the evaluation tool), then fewer sides off, then the lexicographically smallest amounts in authored ingredient order. The solver never chooses substitutions.
  - **D5. Diversification**: attributes main protein group, cuisine, and a cooking-method family derived in `shared/recipe.ts` (no schema change): fried recipe → `fry`; `no_cook` tag → `no_cook`; otherwise the main protein ingredient of the recipe's group by `cooking ?? state`: `grill`; `simmer` (boil, simmer, poach); `steam` (steam, microwave); `stir_fry`; `pan` (saute, scramble, fry_egg); raw or as sold → `no_cook`. Greedy selection within the best tier with a lexicographic key, no weights: (1) the number of attributes whose value is not yet among the chosen and previous recipes, (2) preference score (cuisine match +1, effort at most the preferred level +1), (3) smaller relative change, (4) seeded order.
  - **D6. Re-suggestion**: the seeded order is `mix32(seed, fnv1a(recipeId))` with the recipe ID as the final tie-break, so adding or removing recipes does not reorder the others; the UI supplies the seed (0 first, +1 for "show a different type"); the domain never calls `Math.random`. "Show a different type" adds the current set to `shown` and `previous`; when fewer than 3 unshown recipes meet the targets, the result sets `exhausted` and selects again from all except the previous set. "Exclude this one" adds to `excluded`, keeps the other cards (`keep`), and fills only the free slot. State stays in memory (ADR-015).
  - **D7. Hard filters**: allergens exclude a recipe when any ingredient (including frying oil, coatings, and sides) has `contains`, `may_contain`, or unknown allergen information, with no rescue by sides or substitutions. An excluded food that appears only in user-switchable sides switches those sides off; one that a substitution replaces applies that substitution; the forced state is reported and time, equipment, and allergens are checked again on it; otherwise the recipe is excluded. Required foods must appear in the default ingredients (not through a substitution). Time and equipment use the full recipe (ADR-026); equipment must be a subset of the available equipment.
  - **D8. Relaxation**: offered when fewer than 3 recipes meet the targets; one constraint at a time: the smallest authored recipe time that adds a met recipe, one more piece of equipment, removing one required food, removing one excluded food. Allergens and recipes excluded by the user are never relaxed. Counts are recomputed exactly (filters again on the cached solver results). Order: more met recipes gained, then more near recipes gained, then the fixed constraint order; options with no gain are not returned.
  - **D9. Contradictions**, before searching, with the general factors 4 kcal/g for protein and carbohydrate (an *assumption*; table energy differs, median +4 %): blocking (no search) when 4 × (protein minimum − 3) + 4 × 0.9 × carbohydrate minimum > 1.1 × kcal maximum; warning (search runs) when 4 × protein minimum + 4 × carbohydrate minimum ≥ 0.8 × kcal maximum. An inverted bound is an input error, not a contradiction.
  - **D10. Fried dishes** (open question 18): wide fried ranges giving "near" instead of "met" is intended; the bands stay as in ADR-028.
- Rationale: one pure engine for the browser and the tools; statuses never claim more certainty than the shown range allows; the linear model keeps the exhaustive result while avoiding about 0.5 s per request at 60 recipes in Node (more on phones); a lexicographic order is explainable by the reason data.
- Alternatives: point-based "met" with a range guard (changes ADR-025 and [NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md](../en/NUTRITION_CALCULATION.md) §9); `unknown` as near or not met; exhaustive engine calls only; a weighted preference score; an authored `method` field; a PRNG stream; rebuilding the whole set on "exclude this one"; rescuing allergen recipes by switching sides off; excluding every recipe that contains an excluded food.
- Evidence: verification log "Stage 3.1 recommendation design".

## ADR-030: MVP user interface design
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-19, Stage 4.1)
- Screens, wireframes, navigation, state, styling, and catalog structure: [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md). The UI changes no engine behavior, formula, tolerance, limit, or threshold.
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Views**: targets and conditions (quick inputs and a collapsible advanced section), candidates (3–5 cards, up to 3 selected for comparison), comparison, and recipe detail with adjustment; every exceptional state of [SPEC.md](../en/SPEC.md) §12 is a state of these views.
  - **D2. Navigation**: no router library; a small History API wrapper in `src/navigation/`. Only the recipe detail has its own path (`/recipes/<id>`); the other views use `/` with the view name in `history.state`. Targets and conditions are never in the URL or in `history.state`. After a reload, candidates and comparison fall back to the targets view with a notice; the detail view shows the recipe at its default amounts without fit statuses. State lives in one `useReducer` behind a context; no store library. Revised (owner, 2026-10-23, Stage 5.1 P7): the about page also has its own path (`/about`).
  - **D3. Styling**: Tailwind CSS v4 (`tailwindcss`, `@tailwindcss/vite`) with tokens in CSS, system fonts only, WCAG 2.2 AA contrast checked by a test over the token pairs, and status markers with distinct shapes and texts. Revised (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 14 Stage 14.1): the visual identity of ADR-037 replaces the proof-of-concept tokens; Tailwind, system fonts, the contrast test, and the marker shapes stay.
  - **D4. Input limits and contradiction thresholds** (open question 19): unchanged (`INPUT_LIMITS`, ADR-029 D9). Input errors appear at their fields and in a summary on submit; a blocking contradiction stops the search with an explanation; a warning is shown and the search runs.
  - **D5. "Show a different type" when no new recipe meets the targets** (Stage 3.7 observation): the current set stays on screen with a notice that every recipe meeting the targets has been shown, plus "show close alternatives" and "change conditions". A result with only alternatives does not replace the set.
  - **D6. Ingredient input** (open question 14): an ARIA combobox over the active locale's names and aliases, normalized per locale ([ARCHITECTURE.md](../en/ARCHITECTURE.md) §4); no match shows "not supported". Aliases stay optional because names are always searchable. Protein groups are offered as "any cut" items: a required group is one any-of item; an excluded group adds every food of the group.
  - **D7. Relaxation and equipment** (open question 20): no engine change. Relaxation options are listed with their met and near gains and an "apply" action. Equipment is chosen as "no limit" (default), "microwave only", or individual items; when no single relaxation exists, the UI suggests reviewing the equipment and links to that field.
  - **D8. Adjustment**: servings, steppers on the authored grid and whole units, side switches, substitutions from the recipe's list, "fit to my targets" (the pool entry's solver state; only after a search), and reset. Differences from the previous state are the differences of the displayed (rounded) values, so that the shown numbers add up, and status changes are listed.
  - **D9. Browser storage** (open question 5): nothing except the locale preference is stored in the MVP. Opt-in storage of targets or viewed recipes may be proposed later with its own privacy review. Revised (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 13 Stage 13.1): targets and conditions may be saved on the device by the user's choice (ADR-036). Viewed recipes, search results, the comparison, the adjustments, and the shopping list stay in memory. Revised (owner, 2026-11-08, Phase 19 Stage 19.1): a theme choice of light or dark may be stored (ADR-042).
  - **D10. Where the recommendation runs** (open question 17): the main thread. Stage 4.7 measures a full recommendation, a re-suggestion, and a recalculation in the browser, including a phone-class CPU throttling profile if the browser offers one; a worker is proposed only if a full recommendation exceeds about 50 ms there. Revised (owner, 2026-10-24, Stage 5.4): the recommendation stays on the main thread; the threshold becomes about 100 ms for a full recommendation at a 4× CPU slowdown (a response within 100 ms is perceived as immediate). Measured: median 81 ms, p90 94 ms at 4×; about 120 ms at 6× is a known limitation, and the timing is measured again when the library grows or before real-device checks ("Stage 5.4 E2E suite and phone-class timing"). Update (Phase 6 Stage 6.2, 2026-10-24): after optimizations that change no result, median 66 ms at 4× and about 100 ms at 6× on the 60 recipes; about 92–110 ms is estimated for 100 recipes at 4× ("Stage 6.2 recommendation speed: optimization"). Update (Phase 9 Stage 9.3, 2026-10-25): after optimizations that change no result (precomputed solver results built after the first view, incremental prediction, pruning by the relative change, reused number formatters), median 50–55 ms and p90 54–64 ms at 4× on the 86 recipes (about 73–81 ms at 6×), and median 62–70 ms and p90 71–80 ms at 4× on a synthetic library of 130 recipes ("Stage 9.3 browser timing", "Stage 9.3 precomputation after the first view").
  - **D11. Accessibility tests**: `axe-core` in Vitest with jsdom for every view and state in `ja`, `en`, and `es`; color contrast is covered by the token test because jsdom cannot compute it. Playwright stays in Phase 5.
  - **D12. Card reason line**: at most two reasons besides the fit status, in this order: `highest_among_candidates` / `lowest_among_candidates`, `uses_required_foods`, `new_attribute`, `matches_cuisine`, `quick`, `within_effort`; when none applies, `meets_all_targets` or `meets_with_adjustment`. Close alternatives show all their gap reasons (`near_target`, `misses_target`, `unknown_value`, `range_straddles`).
  - **D13. Seed**: 0 for every new search, +1 for each "show a different type" (ADR-029 D6).
- Dependencies (verification log "Stage 4.1 UI design"): `tailwindcss` 4.3.3 and `@tailwindcss/vite` 4.3.3 (dev), `axe-core` 4.14.0 (dev). No new install script; no router.
- Revised (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 16 Stage 16.1): the UI catalogs and free-text word lists load per locale before the first render and before a language switch (ADR-039).
- Alternatives: React Router or wouter (more than two views with URLs are not needed, and targets must stay out of URLs); plain CSS (the owner's other apps use Tailwind); opt-in local storage (privacy review not needed for the MVP flow); a worker from the start (Node timings are far below a frame budget); combined equipment relaxation in the engine (needs an ADR-029 change for one rare case); differences of full-precision values (shown numbers would not add up).

## ADR-031: Repository license
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-25)
- Decision: the [MIT License](../../LICENSE) for the repository, with the copyright holder `musica-sanare` (the GitHub handle, not a personal name) and the year 2026. It covers the code, configuration, docs, and the original content in `content/` (recipes, food names, translations).
- Excluded: `data/composition/`, whose values come from the MEXT tables and are used under the MEXT terms with the requested source statement ([DATA_SOURCES.md](../en/DATA_SOURCES.md) "License of the extracted data").
- `package.json` and the lockfile root declare `"license": "MIT"`; `"private": true` stays so that the package is never published to npm. Production dependency licenses (MIT, ISC, Apache-2.0, BSD, 0BSD; `tools/licenses.ts`) are compatible, and `dist/third-party-licenses.txt` still lists them.
- Alternatives: CC BY 4.0 for recipe texts (a second license for one repository; rejected for simplicity); no license (the owner plans to make the repository public).
- Before the repository is made public: scan the full history for secrets and review the docs for data that should not be public (shared public-repository safety rule).

## ADR-032: Shopping list
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-25, Phase 8 Stage 8.1)
- Scope: shopping lists move from "out of scope for the MVP" to a feature added after it ([SPEC.md](../en/SPEC.md) §3 and §18). Prices, stores, package sizes, stock tracking, meal planning, accounts, server-side storage, sharing services, analytics, and AI stay out. Nutrition calculation, the recommendation engine, and the recipe content do not change.
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Module**: `domain/shopping` is pure, deterministic, and takes no locale. It gets amounts from `resolveAmounts` of `domain/nutrition`, returns food IDs, grams, household measures, roles, sections, and per-recipe contributions, and computes no nutrition value. It runs only when the list changes or is shown, never on the recommendation path (ADR-030 D10).
  - **D2. Entry point**: the recipe detail only. Adding copies the current recipe state (servings, amounts, sides, substitutions); one entry per recipe; at most 10 recipes. The entry is a snapshot: when the detail state differs, the detail view offers "update the list" and "remove from the list". The list shows each recipe's name, servings, and changes; items show their per-recipe amounts.
  - **D3. Amounts and combination**: items are the resolved grams, combined by food ID (the cooking state does not split them); sides switched off add nothing; substitutions list the substitute food; `includedIn` ingredients are listed. Grams are the raw edible portion or the weight as sold, said in a note; purchase amounts from refuse rates are not shown. Frying oil (an oil whose `includedIn` target is in a fried state or marked `slow_frying`) is a separate item with a note that the amount depends on the pan, scaled like every other amount. Totals are rounded only for display (`formatGrams`, up to one decimal); a total household measure is shown only when every contribution has the same unit and the sum is a multiple of 1/12, without unit conversion.
  - **D4. Pantry items**: two sections, "ingredients" and "check whether you have"; the second holds foods whose every role in the list is `seasoning` or `fat`. Both sections are shown and checkable. Order: a fixed role order (main protein, main carbohydrate, vegetable, other and coating; seasoning, fat, frying oil), then first appearance (recipes in the order added, ingredients in authored order); no alphabetical order.
  - **D5. Persistence**: in memory only; kept across view changes, new searches, and language changes; lost on reload (the reload notice says so). Checked items are kept in memory and cleared when an item's total changes. Nothing new is stored in the browser (ADR-015, ADR-030 D9).
  - **D6. Output**: an on-screen checklist; copy as plain text of the unchecked items through the Clipboard API, with a selectable text area as the fallback; print through `window.print()` with print styles, every item with an empty box. No Web Share or other sharing service. Text format: [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §3.7.
  - **D7. UI**: a view `shoppingList` at `/` with the view name in `history.state` (ADR-030 D2); a header link with the recipe count while the list is not empty; an add block in the detail view; native checkboxes, live region announcements, focus rules, an inline confirmation for clearing, and an empty state ([UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §3.4, §3.5, §3.7). The list shows no nutrition values and makes no allergen, food safety, or health statement of its own.
- Rationale: the detail view is where the state is visible and adjustable; a snapshot cannot change silently when a new search resets adjustments; combining by food ID is correct because the content keeps one amount basis per food; roles are authored and reviewed, so the pantry rule needs no schema change; memory-only keeps the privacy model unchanged.
- Alternatives: adding from candidate cards and the comparison (crowded cards, more test combinations); a list that follows the detail state live (changes on a new search); combining by food and state (splits one purchase into several lines); purchase amounts from refuse rates (food codes do not always match the purchased form, for example skinless thigh values for skin-on thighs; a new calculated display); frying oil as the largest unscaled amount (differs from the detail view); a single list without pantry distinction, or a per-food pantry flag (schema and content change); opt-in local storage (needs a privacy review and consent UI); the list in the URL (adjusted amounts derive from targets); a route of its own (the list is empty after a reload anyway).

## ADR-033: French as a fourth locale
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-31, Phase 11)
- Decision: `fr` joins `ja`, `en`, and `es` for the UI and all content (extends ADR-005). Published content must be complete in all four locales (build check); there is no runtime fallback. French follows ADR-007: Claude drafts, the owner reviews diffs with back-translated safety texts, and the commit approval is the review record. Vocabulary is metropolitan French; dish names follow the region the dish comes from.
- Formatting: `Intl` with `fr` (decimal comma, U+202F group separator, `25 %`); mixed fractions as "1 et 1/2"; household units as cuillère à soupe and cuillère à café. Search folds case, accents, and the ligatures œ and æ.
- Rationale: multilingual support is part of the owner's product identity; adding a locale does not change food identity or calculation (ADR-006), and the locale invariance tests iterate over `SUPPORTED_LOCALES`.
- Risk: French errors may go unnoticed without a native reviewer (open question 23).

## ADR-034: Optional AI free-text conditions
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-10-31, Phase 12 Stage 12.1); revised twice on 2026-10-31, the second time to a deterministic parser without AI (see the end of this ADR). The parser was built in Stage 12.4 and the UI in Stage 12.5; released in release 9 (2026-11-01, "Phase 12 acceptance").
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Feature**: the user types a request in their own words (for example 「鶏むね肉で20分以内、辛くないもの」 or "something with chickpeas, no fish"), and the AI suggests structured conditions: required and excluded foods as food IDs, maximum minutes, equipment, and cuisines. The suggestion never contains targets, allergens, nutrition values, health statements, or cooking temperatures and times. Food IDs are restricted to the dictionary by an enum in the output schema and checked again at runtime. A request about allergies gets a localized pointer to the allergen field and nothing else. Explanation text is not part of this phase (ADR-004).
  - **D2. Provider and model**: the Anthropic Claude API with structured outputs, called only from the endpoint. Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) is the candidate and Claude Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) the comparison; Stage 12.3 measures both, and the owner chooses the cheaper model that meets the D4 thresholds. The contract in `shared/` is provider-neutral, so another provider (for example Workers AI) can replace the adapter.
  - **D3. Endpoint and privacy**: a Worker at a same-origin path under `/api/` next to the static assets, so the CSP `connect-src 'self'` stays as it is (ADR-001 revised). The request holds only the free text (length-limited) and the locale (ADR-015 revised). The endpoint validates input and output at runtime, logs no request body, and uses no AI Gateway logging or caching. The input field asks users not to enter personal information; the about page states the provider's retention before release. Abuse handling: a rate limit, an input length limit, and an output token limit.
  - **D4. Evaluation**: 60 cases per locale (240 in total), the same intents in `ja`, `en`, `es`, and `fr`, covering regional vocabulary, aliases, and out-of-scope requests (allergens, targets, recipe generation). Claude drafts the cases and the owner reviews them, `es` and `fr` with back-translations (open questions 9 and 23). Release thresholds: food-ID precision ≥ 0.95 and recall ≥ 0.90; forbidden fields in 0 % of outputs; out-of-scope requests declined in ≥ 95 %; the same structured result across locales in ≥ 90 % of intents. The evaluation is an on-demand script, never a CI job.
  - **D5. Cost and limits**: the owner owns the Anthropic account, the key, and billing; the key is a Worker secret and never in the repository or the bundle. The owner sets a monthly spend limit in the Console (for example $5); when it is reached, the provider's error makes the app show the deterministic fallback. An environment switch disables the feature without a redeploy of the static assets.
  - **D6. UX and failure states**: a collapsible "describe in your own words (AI)" field in the conditions area. The suggestion is shown as condition chips labelled as an AI suggestion, with "apply" and "discard"; nothing is applied without the user. Timeout (about 8 s), rate limit, spent budget, refusal, invalid output, and offline each show a localized message and leave the form unchanged. With the endpoint disabled or absent, the field is hidden and the app behaves as release 8.
- Rationale: free-text conditions widen the entry to the deterministic search, which serves the product's discovery goal, and their output is structured, so every field can be validated. Explanation text adds little over the deterministic reason templates and risks numbers and health wording in every response. The Claude API was chosen for language quality in four locales and schema-constrained output; Workers AI names none of the four languages and does not guarantee JSON conformance ("Stage 12.1 provider and platform check").
- Alternatives: explanation text; Workers AI (lower cost, no external account, no retention); returning food words for the dictionary search instead of IDs (no schema enum, but aliases may miss); an endpoint on another host (CSP change, cross-origin requests).
- Risks: the provider retains inputs for up to 30 days; users may type personal information; `es` and `fr` cases have no native reviewer; the per-request cost is an estimate until Stage 12.3 (要確認).
- Revised (owner, 2026-10-31, before Stage 12.3, "Provider change: Workers AI free allocation only"):
  - D2: Cloudflare Workers AI through a binding of the endpoint Worker, replacing the Claude API. Candidates for Stage 12.3: `@cf/qwen/qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` and `@cf/google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`; models that need a paid billing method are excluded. The owner chooses the model that meets the D4 thresholds. Schema conformance is not guaranteed, so the runtime validation of `shared/suggestion.ts` is the only guard.
  - D3: inputs and outputs are not retained by Workers AI after the request, as long as no AI Gateway logging is used; the about page states this before release.
  - D5: no billing in any form. The Worker and Workers AI run on Workers Free only, within 10,000 neurons per day; no upgrade to Workers Paid, no paid model, and no AI Gateway credits. When the allocation is used up, Workers AI returns an error and the app shows the `budget_spent` fallback. The environment switch stays.
  - Risks, revised: none of the candidate models names Japanese, Spanish, or French, so the D4 thresholds may fail; one user can use up the day's allocation for everyone (no billing, but the feature stops until the reset). If a threshold fails, the options include a deterministic free-text parser without AI, a narrower scope, or stopping the feature.
- Revised again (owner, 2026-10-31, Stage 12.3, "Stage 12.3 Workers AI runs and the switch to a deterministic parser"): no AI. The candidate models stayed far below the D4 thresholds, so the free text is parsed by a deterministic parser in the browser.
  - D1: the feature and its output shape are unchanged: suggested conditions the user applies or discards, never targets, allergens, nutrition values, health statements, or cooking temperatures and times. Requests about allergies, targets, or health are still declined with a localized pointer. The UI labels the result as conditions read from the text, not as an AI suggestion.
  - D2: no provider and no model. The parser matches the request against the food dictionary (names, aliases, and the search normalization of ADR-030 D6) and against word lists per locale for negation, time, equipment, cuisines, group words, and out-of-scope topics. The word lists are canonical repository content in `ja`, `en`, `es`, and `fr`, drafted by Claude and reviewed by the owner (ADR-007, ADR-033). The same text and locale always give the same result; text it does not recognize is ignored, never guessed.
  - D3: no endpoint, no Worker, and no network request: the text never leaves the browser, ADR-001 and ADR-015 need no AI revision, and the CSP stays as it is.
  - D4: the thresholds and the evaluation set apply unchanged. To limit tuning to texts already seen, the 60 intents are split into 30 for development and 30 held out; the held-out half decides acceptance, and both halves are reported.
  - D5: no cost and no allocation; the environment switch and `budget_spent` are no longer needed.
  - D6: unchanged except the timeout and the provider errors, which no longer occur.
- Stages after this revision: 12.4 the parser, its word lists, tests, and evaluation adapter; 12.5 the UI in four locales with E2E tests; release 9 after the owner's approval. The endpoint core in `server/ai/` and the HTTP parts of `shared/suggestion.ts` are reshaped in Stage 12.4.

## ADR-035: Deploy through Workers Builds on push to `main`
- Status: **Accepted** (owner's approval of the commit `75f58c9`, 2026-10-31); confirmed by the first build ("First Workers Builds deployment") and by release 9
- Decision: Cloudflare Workers Builds (Git integration) deploys the existing Worker `recipe-discovery`. A push to `main`, made only by the owner, is a production deploy; `dev` is the integration branch checked by CI and does not deploy. `main` receives only `dev` commits that passed CI there: the owner fast-forwards `main` to `dev` by hand and pushes, so both point to the same commit (revised 2026-11-08, "Releases by fast-forward"; from 2026-11-01 to release 15 the owner merged with a merge commit, locally or through a pull request, and the decision first said "by fast-forward"). No local deploy and no Wrangler login on any machine; GitHub Actions holds no Cloudflare credentials. Details: [DEPLOYMENT.md](../en/DEPLOYMENT.md) §3.
- Settings (the owner sets them in the dashboard): repository `musica-sanare/recipe-discovery-app` with the GitHub app limited to it; production branch `main`; builds for non-production branches off; root directory `/`; build command `npm ci && npm run check`; deploy command `node platform/cloudflare/deploy.ts`; build variable `SKIP_DEPENDENCY_INSTALL=1`; the API token Workers Builds creates.
- Wrangler: not a repository dependency (unchanged). `platform/cloudflare/deploy.ts` installs an exact version (`WRANGLER_VERSION`, 4.156.0) into a temporary prefix with `--ignore-scripts` and runs `wrangler deploy --config platform/cloudflare/wrangler.jsonc` with metrics off; the config stays in `platform/cloudflare/` (ADR-001). The version changes only in a reviewed commit under ADR-016.
- npm: `packageManager` `npm@11.17.0` in `package.json`, so the build image uses the npm that wrote the lockfile and enforces `strict-allow-scripts` (ADR-018); the image's default npm 10.9.2 would not.
- `compatibility_date`: changes only in a reviewed commit, never automatically or to a future date, when a Worker script needs newer runtime behavior and after reading the compatibility flags in between. It no longer follows the deployment date. The assets-only Worker keeps 2026-10-31.
- Previews: off. CI covers `dev`; a preview would publish unreleased code on a public URL and use build minutes. Can be revisited for the AI endpoint (ADR-034) with the owner's approval.
- Billing: the account stays on Workers Free, with no payment method, no plan change, and no purchased build minutes. Expected use is one build of a few minutes per release, far below 3,000 minutes per month.
- Migration order: (1) the owner approves and commits this change on `dev`, and CI passes; (2) the owner fast-forwards `main` to that commit and pushes, before connecting, so nothing deploys yet; (3) the owner connects the repository with the settings above; (4) the first build of `main` deploys content equal to release 8 (only docs, tooling, and `server/` changed since); (5) Claude checks the site with `curl`; (6) the owner runs `wrangler logout` and removes the scratch Wrangler installation.
- Rationale: matches the shared deployment rule (deploys only through the host's Git integration, by the owner's push) and my-music-site; removes the local login and manual steps; keeps the static `dist/` portable.
- Alternatives: config at the repository root with the default deploy command (simplest, but moves platform configuration out of `platform/cloudflare/`, ADR-001; the fallback if `--config` fails the name check); root directory `platform/cloudflare/` (the build would run outside the repository root); Wrangler as an exact dev dependency (a locked tree and `npm audit`, but `workerd` and `esbuild` in every CI install and two more `allowScripts` entries); deploying from GitHub Actions with a token (rejected by the shared rule).
- Risks and 要確認: the name check accepts a config passed with `--config` while the root directory has none (confirmed by the first build). Still open: what happens on Workers Free when the build minutes run out; whether a dashboard rollback restores static assets; the Wrangler dependency tree is not locked (installed fresh per build without install scripts); whether the automatically created token can be narrowed (it has no billing permission in the documented list).

## ADR-036: Opt-in saved targets and conditions
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 13 Stage 13.1); built in Stages 13.2 and 13.3 and released in release 10 (2026-11-01, "Phase 13 acceptance").
- Scope: a user can keep the targets and conditions form on their own device and find it again on the next visit. No account, sync, server, cookie, URL, or network request is involved (principle 20, ADR-015 revised). With saving off, the app behaves as release 9 and writes nothing new to storage.
- Decisions:
  - **D1. What is saved**: the targets and conditions form (`TargetsDraft`): the preset, the four target fields, servings, the time limit, effort, cuisines, the equipment mode and items, and the required and excluded foods. Allergens are saved only when the user also ticks a separate checkbox, which is off by default and says that allergen choices will be saved on this device. Not saved: the free text of Phase 12, search results, the comparison, the adjustments, the shopping list, viewed recipes, and the locale (which keeps its own key). No timestamp is stored.
  - **D2. Mechanism**: one `localStorage` key, `recipe-discovery.conditions`, holding one JSON record with a schema version. The record is about 1–2 KB, far below the 5 MiB per origin ("Stage 13.1 decisions"). Every access is wrapped as in `src/i18n/localePreference.ts`: a `SecurityError`, a `QuotaExceededError`, or missing storage is caught, and the app keeps working with the form in memory.
  - **D3. Opt-in and control**: a checkbox "save these conditions on this device" in the targets view next to "Find recipes". Turning it on saves the current form at once; turning it off deletes the record. The checkbox state is derived from whether a record exists, so no further key is stored. A "delete saved conditions" action is shown while a record exists; it deletes the record and turns the checkbox off. A short note says that anyone using the same browser can see the saved conditions and that they are not sent anywhere. A failed write shows a notice that the conditions could not be saved on this device.
  - **D4. When to save and restore**: saved when saving is turned on and on each "Find recipes" while it is on; never on each keystroke, and not when free-text conditions are applied (they are saved with the next "Find recipes"). On load, a valid record is restored into the form without starting a search, and a notice says that saved conditions were restored, with the delete action next to it.
  - **D5. Validation and versioning**: a `zod/mini` schema checks the record at runtime (no new dependency). Numbers are stored as the text the user typed and restored unchanged; invalid or out-of-range numbers show the usual input errors on the next search. Food choices are stored by key: a food ID, or a group key such as `group:chicken`, which is expanded from the current content on restore. Food IDs, groups, cuisines, equipment, and allergens that no longer exist are dropped, with a notice that some saved items are no longer available. A record that fails the schema is deleted without a notice. The record carries `version: 1`; older versions are read through explicit conversions; a record with a newer, unknown version (after a rollback) is ignored and not deleted.
