  - **D6. Determinism and locale**: the record holds no locale-dependent text; restoring it gives the same search request, and so the same results, as typing the same values in any locale (principles 2 and 8). Restoring changes no engine input other than the form.
  - **D7. Documentation and privacy**: [SPEC.md](../en/SPEC.md) §3 and §13 describe the feature; the about page's privacy text is updated in four locales in Stage 13.3. No consent banner is added: the checkbox is the user's explicit request, the record has one purpose, and nothing is sent ("Stage 13.1 decisions"). The owner accepted this without a legal review (2026-11-01, "Phase 13 acceptance").
- Rationale: saving only by the user's choice keeps the privacy model of the MVP for everyone else; `localStorage` is small, synchronous, and already used for the locale; saving on "Find recipes" keeps half-typed values out of the record; a separate allergen checkbox lets a user with allergies avoid re-entering them, which reduces the risk of forgetting them, while keeping health-related choices off a shared device unless asked.
- Alternatives: IndexedDB (asynchronous, more code for one small record); `sessionStorage` (lost when the tab closes); saving on each change (writes half-typed values); not saving allergens (re-entry each visit, and forgetting them is the risky case); allergens under the same switch (health-related data saved without a choice of its own); several named profiles, history, or saved shopping lists (out of scope for Phase 13); a consent banner (adds friction to a feature the user turns on).
- Limits: storage is best effort. Private browsing, the user clearing site data, storage pressure, or Safari's seven-day deletion under tracking prevention can remove the record; the app then starts from the defaults.

## ADR-037: Visual identity "soft pop"
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 14 Stage 14.1); revises ADR-030 D3. Applied in Stages 14.2 to 14.4 and released in release 11 (2026-11-01, "Phase 14 acceptance"). Revised by ADR-041 (owner, 2026-11-08, Phase 18 Stage 18.1): the dark palette, the warning color, the nutrition bars, and the motion of D5.
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Direction**: "soft pop": the warm, calm base of the "fresh market" mockup with pastel accents (mint, butter, sky, peach), a sun yellow for decoration, two-tone icons, colored tiles for the nutrition numbers, and soft shadows. Tokens, contrast values, type, and shapes: [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §5 "Visual identity".
  - **D2. Fonts**: system fonts only, as before; a type scale with weight 900 for `h1` and `tabular-nums` for numbers. No web font.
  - **D3. Icons**: original two-tone SVG components (protein groups, equipment, time, effort, servings, actions, notices, and the four status markers with their current shapes). No icons for cuisines (to avoid regional stereotypes), nutrients (an energy "flame" suggests fat burning, principle 15), or allergens (a picture can suggest safety, principle 14).
  - **D4. Illustrations**: the targets heading, the empty and exceptional states, and the about page; decorative, without text.
  - **D5. Motion**: a small press on buttons and a short fade for cards; none with `prefers-reduced-motion`.
  - **D6. UX scope**: presentation plus three small changes: open "More conditions" when restored values are inside it, group the targets form under headings, and keep "Find recipes" at the bottom of the screen on phones. No change to the flow of views, calculations, ranking, content, or stored data.
- Rationale: the owner found "fresh market" the easiest to read and the most natural in dark mode; the pastel tiles and two-tone icons add the requested play without the noise of heavy outlines; system fonts avoid the weight of Japanese web fonts and a new dependency.
- Alternatives: "kitchen lab" (clear numbers, but less appetizing), "bento pop" (most playful, but loud on dense views such as the comparison), "bold pop" (outlines and offset shadows; louder in dark mode), a self-hosted Latin display font (mismatched with Japanese text, a license review and more bytes).
- Limits: pastel tiles must never be the only way to tell nutrients or statuses apart; every new color pair joins the contrast test in Stage 14.2.

## ADR-038: Cuisine values for French-speaking regions
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 15 Stage 15.1); revises ADR-026. Applied in Stages 15.2 and 15.3 and released in release 12 (2026-11-01, "Phase 15 acceptance").
- Decision: `CUISINES` adds `french`, `north_african`, and `west_african`.
  - `french` covers dishes from France. Dishes from Belgium, Switzerland, and Québec stay `western`, because a cuisine value names the region a dish comes from (ADR-026) and these are not French dishes.
  - `north_african` covers the Maghreb (Morocco, Algeria, Tunisia); `west_african` covers West Africa (for example Senegal, Mali, Côte d'Ivoire).
  - Existing recipes keep their values.
  - UI labels: `french` フランス料理 / French / Francesa / Française; `north_african` 北アフリカ料理 / North African / Norteafricana / Nord-africaine; `west_african` 西アフリカ料理 / West African / De África occidental / D'Afrique de l'Ouest. The free-text lexicon (`shared/freeText/lexicon.ts`) gets keywords for each value in four locales.
- Effects: diversification and the cuisine preference (ADR-029 D5) use the values unchanged; the cuisine attribute has 12 values instead of 9, so a recipe of a new value counts as a new attribute until one is shown. A user who prefers `western`, including saved conditions (ADR-036), does not get the new French dishes through that preference. The bundle stays compatible (enum values added only).
- Rationale: French-speaking users can find dishes of their own food cultures with the cuisine filter; West African dishes fit no current value.
- Alternatives: keep the current values (French dishes as `western`, Maghreb dishes as `mediterranean`; West African dishes would have no value and be dropped); one value per country or region (five or six values with two to four recipes each, 15 filter choices, a finer diversification attribute).

## ADR-039: Per-locale UI catalogs and free-text word lists
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-01, Phase 16 Stage 16.1); revises ADR-030 (startup path) and closes "Bundle-size warning accepted". Applied in Stage 16.2 and released in release 13 (2026-11-01, "Phase 16 acceptance").
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Split**: each locale's UI catalog (`src/i18n/messages/<locale>.ts`) and free-text word list (moved to `shared/freeText/lexicon/<locale>.ts`) form one dynamically imported chunk. Node tools and tests keep a static index of all four locales. Icon and illustration data stay in the main bundle.
  - **D2. First render**: `main.tsx` resolves the locale (stored, then browser, then `ja`), imports that locale's chunk, and renders only after it has loaded; no other language and no message key is shown.
  - **D3. Switching**: the switcher loads the chunk and the text bundle in parallel, adds the catalog with `addResourceBundle`, then calls `changeLanguage`; the view, its values, and `lang` behave as today. While loading, a status next to the switcher says so (one new catalog key in four locales) and the switcher has `aria-busy`.
  - **D4. Failures**: a failed first load shows a small built-in error message with a retry in the four locales (checked against the catalogs by a test); the retry reloads the page. A failed switch keeps the current language and shows the existing retry state.
  - **D5. Unchanged**: no runtime fallback to another locale (catalogs stay complete by type); the CSP and headers stay as they are; chunks are same-origin hashed assets under `/assets/` with the immutable cache rule. No new dependency.
- Expected sizes (prototype, "Stage 16.1 decisions"): main bundle about 455 kB instead of 568 kB; one locale chunk about 27–31 kB (gzip about 10 kB); startup JavaScript about 485 kB in total, every chunk below Vite's 500 kB warning.
- Rationale: only one locale is used at a time, the catalogs are the largest part of the bundle that grows with each locale, and loading before the first render keeps the synchronous i18next instance and today's tests.
- Alternatives: `i18next-resources-to-backend` with Suspense or the `ready` flag (a new dependency, an asynchronous init for a single namespace, and a risk of showing keys); vendor chunks or a higher `chunkSizeWarningLimit` (the warning goes away, but startup bytes do not drop).
- Limits: the first render waits for one more small request; Stage 16.2 measures startup and switching. Whether a failed `import()` is retried by browsers is 要確認 ("Stage 16.1 decisions").

## ADR-040: Public hostname on musica-sanare.com
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-03). Configured in `platform/cloudflare/wrangler.jsonc` and attached by the deployment of release 14 (2026-11-03, "Release 14 and fourteenth production deployment"). The registry of `musica-sanare-home` lists it as `live` since Phase B there (`5882b07`), after the owner's next-day Billing check showed no new charge.
- Context: the owner serves the hub page and every web app under `musica-sanare.com`; the `musica-sanare-home` repository owns the hostname rules and the registry (its ADR-0001 and `docs/en/DOMAINS.md`), and app repositories record only their own decision. The zone is on the Cloudflare Free plan. The site so far ran only on `workers.dev` (ADR-035).
- Decision:
  - The Worker `recipe-discovery` serves **`recipe-discovery.apps.musica-sanare.com`**, the name the hub's rule `<full-app-name>.apps.musica-sanare.com` gives the product name in full.
  - It is attached as a **Workers Custom Domain** through `routes` with `"custom_domain": true` in `platform/cloudflare/wrangler.jsonc`, so the config stays the source of truth and each deployment applies it. Nobody creates a DNS record for it by hand; Cloudflare creates the record and the certificate.
  - `"workers_dev": true` is set explicitly: a `routes` entry would otherwise turn `workers.dev` off, and earlier links and the data people saved there keep working. Both URLs serve the same assets.
  - `"preview_urls": false` is set explicitly: Workers Builds builds no other branch (ADR-035), and Version and Preview URLs would be further public copies of the site.
  - `localStorage` is per origin, so the locale preference and the opt-in saved conditions (ADR-036) stored on `workers.dev` are not available on the new hostname; people who saved conditions there start empty. No migration between origins.
- Verified facts (2026-11-01 to 2026-11-03): a Workers Custom Domain gets its own certificate, also for multi-level subdomains, without Advanced Certificate Manager, and cannot be created on a hostname with an existing CNAME record ([Custom Domains](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/routing/custom-domains/), updated 2026-09-29); Universal SSL covers only the apex and first-level subdomains ([Universal SSL limitations](https://developers.cloudflare.com/ssl/edge-certificates/universal-ssl/limitations/)); `workers_dev` defaults to `false` with `routes`, and `preview_urls` keeps an existing setting when omitted ([Wrangler configuration](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/), updated 2026-10-29). The app needs no code change ("Stage 17.1 decisions").
- Cost: Workers Free; the zone is on the Free plan. No Cloudflare page lists a fee for Workers Custom Domains, and none says they are free; the same kind of attachment for Travel Discovery showed no charge the next day. The owner checks Billing the day after this deployment as well.
- Alternatives: disabling `workers.dev` (one URL only; rejected for now, because earlier links and saved data would break); declaring the domain only in the dashboard (the config would no longer describe production); a first-level hostname such as `recipe-discovery.musica-sanare.com` (rejected by the hub's rules).
- Web Analytics (2026-11-03): after the attachment, Cloudflare Web Analytics, in its automatic setup, injected its beacon script into the HTML served to browsers on the custom domain (and on `travel-discovery.apps.musica-sanare.com`), not on `workers.dev`. The CSP (`script-src 'self'`) blocked it, so no beacon data was sent, but the console showed a CSP error. The owner disabled Web Analytics for the whole zone; the HTML had no beacon from 09:41 UTC. The app uses no analytics (CLAUDE.md), so it stays disabled; DEPLOYMENT "Verify after a deploy" checks for it. `Cache-Control: no-transform` on the HTML would also stop the injection ([Web Analytics: Get started](https://developers.cloudflare.com/web-analytics/get-started/), read 2026-11-03); not added, because the zone setting is enough and the header would need a reviewed change of `platform/headers.ts`.
- Risks and 要確認: whether a deployment without the `routes` entry detaches the domain (the rollback in DEPLOYMENT removes it in the dashboard as well if it stays); the exact build-log wording for the attached domain; Web Analytics turned on again for the zone, for example for another site (the check in DEPLOYMENT finds it).

## ADR-041: "Soft pop" refresh: readable dark mode and discovery touches
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-08, Phase 18 Stage 18.1); revises ADR-037. Applied in Stages 18.2 to 18.4.
- Context: the owner finds dark mode hard to read and the app short of character, playfulness, and a modern feel. In dark mode, panels barely separate from the page, the pastels look brown, the fat tile and the allergen notice share one color (in light mode too), and small `muted` text on tiles is hard to read ("Stage 18.1 decisions").
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Dark palette**: a cool ink base with three surface steps (`bg`, `surface`, `surface-muted`), a hairline edge token and a dark shadow on panels, and tile fills that keep their hue. Every pair passes `test/node/styles.test.ts`, which gains the new tokens. Tokens and contrast values: [UI_DESIGN.md](../en/UI_DESIGN.md) §5 (Stage 18.2).
  - **D2. Warning apart from nutrients**: the fat tile moves from `peach` to a new `lilac` pastel in both schemes, and the warning gets its own `warning-surface` and `warning-edge` in both schemes, so no nutrient tile or bar shares a color with an allergen or safety notice. Notices keep their wording, icon, and left edge.
  - **D3. Small text on tiles**: ranges, targets, and similar small texts on colored fills use `text` at 13 px instead of `muted` at 12 px.
  - **D4. Discovery touches** (Stage 18.3):
    - Nutrition as a bar per nutrient against the target range, on candidate cards and in the detail view, instead of the colored tiles. The bar is drawn from the displayed values and adds no number; every text shown today stays next to it: the value, the estimate range, the status marker and text with "just below" or "just above", the target or "no target", and unknown values with their foods.
    - A "new this session" mark on candidates not shown before in the session, held in memory only (ADR-030 D9); new text in four catalogs.
    - An illustrated loading state with a skeleton instead of the bare loading text.
    - A translucent, blurred header and bottom bar, with an opaque fallback where `backdrop-filter` is not supported and in forced colors.
    - The two-column desktop detail view with the nutrition in a sticky right column next to the adjustment (UI_DESIGN §3.4). Revised (owner, 2026-11-08, after Stage 18.3): only the nutrition sticks; a sticky adjustment would hide a focused stepper under the nutrition when moving backwards (WCAG 2.4.11). Revised (owner, 2026-11-08, Phase 19 Stage 19.1): the ingredients, the steps, the safety notes, and the basis follow below both columns at full width, and on cards the bar shares the row of the label and the value ("Stage 19.1 decisions").
  - **D5. Motion** (Stage 18.4): a short shuffle on "show a different type", a short confirmation when an adjustment makes a target met (reusing the status-change texts of ADR-030 D8), and a short highlight of changed values. CSS only, no animation library; none with `prefers-reduced-motion`.
  - **D6. Unchanged**: system fonts only (ADR-037 D2); icon rules (ADR-037 D3); the theme follows the OS setting with no in-app switch and no new storage key; CSP and headers; no calculation, ranking, content, or stored-data change. Revised (owner, 2026-11-08, Phase 19 Stage 19.1): the theme may be chosen in the app, with one new storage key (ADR-042).
- Rationale: the cool base and surface steps fix the readability problem without changing the identity; separating the warning color is a safety point (principle 14); bars shorten the cards and show where a value sits in the target range at a glance, while the texts keep the meaning; the discovery touches add play where the product's value lies, discovering new dishes, without loud styling on dense views.
- Alternatives: A "soft pop refined" (palette and loading only; safe, but adds little character); C "sticker display" (a Latin display typeface and sticker badges; strongest character, but a font review, more bytes, and mixed Japanese and Latin type, as in ADR-037); an in-app theme switch (needs a new `localStorage` key); a count-up of changed values (shows numbers between calculated values for a moment; rejected by the owner on 2026-11-08).
- Limits: colors still carry no meaning alone; the bar scale must not suggest more precision than the estimate range; contrast in the app and forced colors are checked in each stage.

## ADR-042: In-app theme choice stored on the device
- Status: **Accepted** (owner, 2026-11-08, Phase 19 Stage 19.1); revises ADR-041 D6 and ADR-030 D9. Built in Stage 19.2.
- Context: the owner asked for an in-app theme switch. ADR-041 D6 left it out because it needs a new stored key.
- Decisions:
  - **D1. Control**: a native select labelled "Theme" in the header, after the language switcher, with the options system, light, and dark in four catalogs. "System" is the default.
  - **D2. Storage**: one `localStorage` key, `recipe-discovery.theme`, holding `light` or `dark`. Choosing "system" removes the key, so nothing is stored. Any other value is ignored. Every access is wrapped as in `src/i18n/localePreference.ts`; when storage fails, the choice lasts for the page. Nothing leaves the browser.
  - **D3. Early application**: a classic same-origin script, `public/theme-init.js`, loaded in `<head>`, sets `data-theme` on the root element before the first paint. The CSP and headers do not change. The file is not hashed, so it has the host's default revalidation (Cloudflare: `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` with an ETag), which adds at most one round trip on a repeat visit. A test keeps its key and values equal to those of the app.
  - **D4. One copy of the dark tokens**: `@custom-variant dark` expands to `@media screen and (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` with `:root:not([data-theme="light"])`, and to `@media screen` with `:root[data-theme="dark"]`. The tokens sit once under `@variant dark`. `color-scheme` follows the choice. On "system", an OS change applies through CSS alone. Print always uses the light tokens (added in Stage 19.2, "Stage 19.2 theme switch").
  - **D5. Tests**: `test/node/styles.test.ts` reads the dark tokens from `@variant dark`. An E2E test checks that the OS dark scheme and the dark choice give the same value for every token.
  - **D6. Privacy text**: the about page and [SPEC.md](../en/SPEC.md) §3 and §13 name the stored theme choice in four locales. No consent banner: the user makes the choice, the record has one purpose, and nothing is sent.
- Measurements (2026-11-08, prototype, local server, 10 runs per case in Chromium and WebKit): with the head script, no frame showed the wrong theme, also with the startup chunk delayed by 600 ms. Applying the choice in `src/main.tsx` showed the wrong theme for at most 33 ms without the delay and about 600–630 ms with it.
- Alternatives: applying in `src/main.tsx` only (a visible flash on slow networks); an inline script (blocked by the CSP); a hashed file under `/assets/` (no revalidation, but needs a build plugin and a separate path in the dev server); `light-dark()` (changes every token and the contrast test); icon radios (a taller header from `md`, three new icons); a footer control (hard to find).
- Limits: storage is best effort, as in ADR-036. Forced colors keep overriding the palette. The cost of the revalidation on a real network is not measured.
- Correction to ADR-041: its rationale says that bars shorten the cards. Measured under equal conditions, the cards of release 15 are 2–13 % taller than those of release 14 ("Stage 19.1 decisions").

---

## Open questions (要確認)

1. ~~Carbohydrate column (ADR-011)~~: `CHOCDF-` accepted. Localized display names are set with the UI catalogs (Phase 4).
2. Extraction method for Tables 12–14 from the PDF; use of chapter 3 group-level rates for missing cooked forms.
3. ~~Tolerance, display rounding, and range threshold values~~: ADR-025. Evidence for the assumed error bands: researched in Stage 6.5; the bands of `raw_value_for_cooking` and `related_cooked_value` are set per nutrient (ADR-028, revised); `post_cooking_refuse`, `fried`, and `slow_frying` remain assumptions.
4. ~~Initial recipe count and the scope of automatic adjustment~~: accepted in ADR-023 and ADR-024 (Stage 0.6); portion rules and linked seasonings are designed in Phase 1/3.
5. ~~Browser storage of targets and viewed recipes~~: nothing except the locale preference in the MVP (ADR-030 D9). Since Phase 13, targets and conditions can be saved by the user's choice (ADR-036); since Phase 19, a theme choice of light or dark is stored (ADR-042); viewed recipes are still not stored.
6. Whether the 2015 English edition's names and food codes can serve as a reference for English names.
7. Applicability of the Health Promotion Act and the Pharmaceutical and Medical Device Act to this app; applicability of allergen labelling rules (policy already applies the stricter interpretation).
8. Copyright status of recipes under Japanese law (primary sources not checked).
9. Spanish review capacity (ADR-007). No native Spanish reviewer is available; the owner reviews with Claude's findings and back-translations, and the regional vocabulary rule of [CONTENT_POLICY.md](../en/CONTENT_POLICY.md) §6 applies ("Stage 6.3 summary"). Remains a known limitation.
10. Raw-egg dishes and other regionally sensitive food safety rules across the three locales. Eggs: decided (owner, 2026-10-23, Stage 5.1 P3) — soft or runny yolks are allowed with the `soft_yolk` note; raw-egg dishes stay excluded. Other regional rules are reviewed per batch in Stage 5.2.
11. ~~Allergen item lists in `shared/content.ts`~~: match the CAA lists of April 2026 ("Stage 5.7 release readiness").
12. Best-effort allergen lists for cooking sake, wholegrain mustard, Chinese-style stock granules, and karaage coating mix are based on typical ingredients, not product labels (Stage 0.6 log). Phase 7 adds chili powder (a spice blend; no allergen listed, like curry powder) and fish sauce (shrimp as may contain, because some products add it). Phase 10 adds kimchi (shrimp as may contain, for salted shrimp or fish sauce in some products, and apple, which many products add) and oyster sauce (wheat and soybean as may contain) ("Stage 10.1 expansion plan"). Phase 15 adds ham (milk, soybean, and egg as may contain, for binders some products add) and beer (wheat as may contain, because some beers are brewed with wheat) ("Stage 15.1 decisions").
13. ~~Owner review of the English and Spanish texts added or rewritten in Phase 1~~: the owner accepted the remaining texts (validation recipes, Phase 0 foods, UI catalogs outside `about.*`) as they are for this release (2026-10-24, "Release blocker decisions"); batches 1–3 and the about page were reviewed with their commits. Reviewed in Phase 6 Stage 6.3 (rounds R1–R6, 2026-10-24, "Stage 6.3 summary"): every text that had not been reviewed is now reviewed.
14. ~~Whether every food must have at least one search alias per locale~~: aliases stay optional (ADR-030 D6).
15. ~~Phase 2 must distinguish raw values from related cooked values for the error bands~~: separate reasons `raw_value_for_cooking` and `related_cooked_value` (ADR-028).
16. ~~Re-check the `i18next`/`react-i18next` pins~~: moved to `i18next` 26.5.12 and `react-i18next` 17.1.9 on 2026-10-31 ("Stage 9.1 ADR-016 re-check and dependency update"), after deferrals for releases 1 to 5 ("Release blocker decisions", "Stage 6.6", "Stage 7.4", "Stage 8.4", "Stage 9.4").
17. ~~Recommendation and recalculation time on phones~~: measured with CPU throttling in Stage 5.4 (about 80 ms at 4×); the recommendation stays on the main thread with a 100 ms threshold (ADR-030 D10, revised). A real-device check remains a manual item.
18. ~~Fried dishes with wide ranges become "near"~~: intended (ADR-029 D10). The fried and slow-frying bands remain assumptions (open question 3).
19. ~~Provisional input limits and contradiction thresholds~~: unchanged (ADR-030 D4).
20. ~~Combined equipment relaxation~~: no engine change; equipment modes and a link to the equipment field instead (ADR-030 D7).
21. ~~Japanese search by reading~~ (closed in Stage 5.3): the owner approved adding hiragana readings as Japanese aliases (2026-10-20); 26 foods gained readings in content version 2026-10-20.1, and `test/node/food-search.test.ts` checks reading search. A long-vowel mark typed in katakana ("トーフ") still does not match a reading written with う ("とうふ"); fixed in Stage 5.3 (query variants for the long-vowel mark).
22. `minimatch` 10.2.6 in the development dependencies has a moderate advisory (GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249) without a fix compatible with `eslint` and `typescript-eslint`; accepted by the owner as development-only with repository-controlled patterns ("Stage 9.1 other dependencies"). Re-check at the start of every phase and before each release. Re-checked at the start of Phase 10 (2026-10-31): unchanged ("Phase 10 start").
23. French review capacity (ADR-033). No native French reviewer is available; the owner reviews with Claude's findings and back-translations ("Phase 11 start: French locale"). Remains a known limitation.
